目标检测/分割多模态/VLM
2026-10-08

Kernelized Parameter Sensitivity Atlases for Amortized Conditional Interpretability

Kernelized Parameter Sensitivity Atlases for Amortized Conditional Interpretability

Davide Wiest、Ahanaf Hasan Ariq、Abdul Monaf Chowdhury

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Gradient-based attribution provides input-specific information about model parameters, but computing it via backpropagation for many samples is expensive, while static importance scores discard input dependence. We derive KPSA (Kernelized Parameter Sensitivity Atlas) from a block decomposition of the empirical neural tangent kernel. It measures normalized parameter-group sensitivity, embeds those sources in an explicit query space, and uses a lightweight kernel to estimate sensitivities for unseen inputs. We derive bounds that separate finite-source from irreducible representation error and give margin-based top-$k$ recovery. This paper studies two interpretability applications. Firstly, KPSA identifies parameter groups with high susceptibility to perturbations across vision, forecasting, and protein-modeling setups. Across a fivefold range of parameter budgets ($0.02$-$0.1\%$), its selected groups attain $39$-$85\%$ of the perturbation susceptibility of a first-order backpropagation oracle. In vision and forecasting, these parameter groups form sparse sensitivity circuits that extend across depth and vary systematically with the input. Secondly, we use KPSA on an activation-attribution problem for MLP features in transformers. It retains $81\%$ of the gap between a backpropagation oracle and a constant-sensitivity baseline on vision while increasing batched query-time throughput from 551 to 2,041 images/s and reducing peak GPU memory from 4.55 to 0.44GB. On the protein-modeling task, it recovers $68\%$ of this gap. An ablation of KPSA's kernel aggregation shows that nearest-neighbor retrieval in KPSA space can remain competitive. The empirics show that compact kernel atlases can store useful input-dependent parameter sensitivity while amortizing repeated gradient attribution, a step toward scalable conditional interpretability. Code can be found at https://github.com/Axym-Labs/kpsa .

目标检测/分割3D视觉视频理解
2026-10-08

Reference-Free Food Volume Estimation from Egocentric Stereo Video

Reference-Free Food Volume Estimation from Egocentric Stereo Video

Vivan Kumar、Gautham Vinod、Fengqing Zhu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Current stereo vision-based approaches for food portion estimation are hindered by the user burden of using a mobile phone to capture multiple images or placing a reference object in the scene for scale calibration. As a step towards passive dietary intake monitoring, we introduce a method that uses stereo video from a wearable egocentric device to reconstruct food items in true physical scale to accurately estimate volume. By utilizing FoundationStereo for zero-shot stereo matching, SAM 2 for food segmentation, and TSDF fusion over a set of keyframes, our method yields a visually and metrically accurate 3D mesh of any plated food item from a single stereo recording. We leverage the unique offerings of wearables, such as a factory-calibrated stereo baseline and camera poses estimated on-device via visual-inertial odometry (VIO), to perform fully automatic reconstruction without a scale reference object in the scene. We show strong initial volume estimation results across five recorded food items, achieving a mean absolute percentage error of 9.1%.

We show strong initial volume estimation results across five recorded food items, achieving a mean absolute percentage error of 9.1%.

2026-10-08

Unsupervised Multimodal Reward Modeling

Unsupervised Multimodal Reward Modeling

Yueying Li、Jingxuan Fan、Kianté Brantley、Sham M. Kakade、Hanlin Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-language reward models are trained on annotated preference pairs, which are expensive to collect, can disagree, and are ranked by benchmark accuracy. We build one with no human preference label: a linear head on an intermediate layer of a frozen Qwen3-VL backbone, trained to score a web caption above a perturbed version of the same caption; labeled data enters only to select the layer. The layer is the decisive choice, worth 5.8 points over the last layer, and one recipe transferred without re-tuning beats or at least matches GPT-4o on three benchmarks at 32B, 6.6 points ahead on VL-RewardBench, from a few thousand parameters at about a thousandth of fine-tuning compute. At an equal pair budget, perturbations teach a frozen head 3 to 5 points more than curated RLAIF-V pairs. Because the backbone is frozen, the objective is convex, and the perturbation sampler specifies the reward, which the benchmark does not identify: two heads trained on disjoint halves of the same perturbations tie on the benchmark and differ by 6.4 points in hallucination judging. Retraining the head on the policy's own verifier-labeled candidates cuts caption hallucination by 5.1 CHAIR points and lifts image-generation pass rate by 5.4, while scoring lower on every reward benchmark. Together, an intermediate-layer probe, free same-image perturbations, and a verifier-driven bootstrap turn give a recipe for multimodal reward modeling that needs no annotator, costs a fraction of fine-tuning, and is specified by the comparisons it is trained on rather than by a benchmark rank.

2026-10-08

Scaling Multi-Teacher Distillation for Digital Pathology

Scaling Multi-Teacher Distillation for Digital Pathology

Sofiène Boutaj、Pierre Marza、Varun Belagali、Dimitris Samaras、Maria Vakalopoulou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multi-teacher distillation allows transferring knowledge from multiple "teacher" networks to a single "student" network. This method is promising in fields such as digital pathology, where many powerful foundation models were proposed recently. However, as we show in this paper, the standard multi-teacher distillation approach reacts poorly to an increase in the number of teachers used to train a student encoder. We demonstrate that learning teacher-specific representations is a key to scaling in MTD. Importantly, different design choices, such as learnable teacher tokens, a tailored attention scheme, additional mixture-of-experts layers and a contrastive loss, are proposed to better learn such teacher-specific representations. We show that our method better scales with respect to the number of considered teachers, allowing us to train compact student encoders, up to 10x more efficient than larger teacher foundation models, while matching their performance and even outperforming them on a large set of tile-level and slide-level tasks. We conduct a thorough empirical validation, evaluating more than 10 foundation models on 39 tasks spanning tile and slide levels. As a result, we release a new collection of strong and efficient foundation models, named OMNI, trained from the knowledge of 10 state-of-the-art foundation models. We finally conduct an analysis of the learned teacher-specific representations, highlighting their complementarity and explaining why they can be easily aggregated at downstream time.

2026-10-08

CASTER: Formalizing Zero-Shot Manipulation from Synthetic Videos as Constrained Optimization

CASTER: Formalizing Zero-Shot Manipulation from Synthetic Videos as Constrained Optimization

Yinlong Dai、Homanga Bharadhwaj、Dylan P. Losey

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Prior research on learning from humans often requires real human demonstrations. But advances in video generation models offer a new paradigm: we can now prompt these generators to make synthetic videos of the task. On the plus side, these synthetic videos take place in the robot's current environment, and do not require actual human teaching. However, while the generated videos appear to show plausible motions, they lack both physical consistency and robot grounding, and thus the robot cannot directly roll out the trajectory shown by the video. In this paper we seek to formalize the process of going from synthetic video generation to real-world robot trajectories for zero-shot manipulation tasks. We recognize that this problem is an instance of constrained optimization. The object trajectories extracted from the synthetic video provide an initial guess at the correct motion, and the robot must refine these trajectories (for instance, within a digital twin) while enforcing physical feasibility and semantic consistency. Our optimization-based formalism provides insights into the video generation properties necessary for success. Specifically, we show that the video generation must probabilistically seed the search within a semantic basin of attraction where the trajectory can be locally modified without losing its task alignment. Our analysis further reveals that state-of-the-art approaches can be viewed as instances of our unified formalism with key simplifications. In our experiments we remove these simplifications to more closely match the underlying optimization, leading to more robust zero-shot trajectories from synthetic videos and improving average task success by 34.1% compared to the best-performing baseline. See videos at our project website: https://collab.me.vt.edu/CASTER

See videos at our project website: https://collab.me.vt.edu/CASTER

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM医学图像遥感
2026-10-09

Multi-Modal Graph Neural Network-Inspired Vessel Identification and Tracking for UAV-Based Maritime Surveillance

Multi-Modal Graph Neural Network-Inspired Vessel Identification and Tracking for UAV-Based Maritime Surveillance

Chenjie Zhao、Jiangtian Nie、Jiawen Kang、Yuxu Lu、Ryan Wen Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

The unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-based maritime surveillance is becoming an important part of maritime Internet of Things (IoT) and intelligent transportation systems. It provides a flexible and cost-effective way to large-scale maritime traffic monitoring. However, achieving robust situational awareness remains challenging in practical maritime environments. The main challenges arise from heterogeneous sensing modalities, adverse imaging conditions, and spatial misalignment caused by UAV motion. To address these problems, this paper proposes an airborne multi-modal graph learning-driven framework, termed AMGT, for vessel identification and tracking in UAV-assisted maritime IoT. Specifically, visible and infrared images are first aligned and fused through a multi-parameter perspective transformation model. This improves visual perception under low visibility and complex illumination conditions. Automatic identification system (AIS) messages are then temporally synchronized using interpolation and linear prediction. To detect small and oriented vessels, we develop a mixed-modality MAir-YOLOv8-OBB detector, followed by the MAir-SORT algorithm for stable vessel tracking. We further design a graph learning-driven association network to model temporal dynamics and spatial dependencies among vessels and between vessels and UAVs. A motion-aware coordinate unification (MCU) strategy is also introduced to reduce nonlinear misalignment caused by UAV motion. Experiments on the MAP-M2UAV50 dataset show that AMGT achieves high accuracy and efficiency. The results demonstrate its potential for UAV-assisted maritime IoT applications, including vessel monitoring, traffic situational awareness, and intelligent maritime transportation services. The dataset and code will be available at https://github.com/MAPGroup/AMGT.

The results demonstrate its potential for UAV-assisted maritime IoT applications, including vessel monitoring, traffic situational awareness, and intelligent maritime transportation services. The dataset and code will be available at https://github.com/MAPGroup/AMGT.

2026-10-09

Complexity-Calibrated Reconstruction Discrepancy for No-Reference Point Cloud Quality Assessment

Complexity-Calibrated Reconstruction Discrepancy for No-Reference Point Cloud Quality Assessment

Haocheng Wu、Xiangjie Sui、Xin Sun

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

The discrepancy induced by surface reconstruction in a distorted point cloud is jointly influenced by its intrinsic structural complexity and signal degradation. This observation motivates a referencefree point cloud quality assessment (PCQA) strategy that estimates and compensates for the complexity-induced component. We implement this strategy through successive reconstruction. Specifically, reconstructing the distorted point cloud produces an input-toreconstruction discrepancy that combines the effects of signal degradation and content complexity. Reapplying the reconstruction operator with stronger smoothing produces a discrepancy between successive reconstructions, which serves as a proxy for the complexityinduced component. This proxy is then used to calibrate the inputto-reconstruction discrepancy. We apply the same strategy to 3D geometry patches and their aligned 2D regions, enabling local comparison of the resulting discrepancies. The calibrated geometric and projected-view discrepancies are then fused into a final degradation score. For both branches, the discrepancies are measured using frozen pretrained perceptual representations. Our method does not require PCQA-specific training or fine-tuning. Experiments on three public databases show that the proposed method consistently outperforms most generic baselines and achieves performance comparable to several full-reference metrics.The code is available at https://github.com/HC-W-whc/CCRD-NR-PCQA.git.

2026-10-09

Golden Anchor for Video Generation

Golden Anchor for Video Generation

Jinyuan Xu、Shitong Shao、Lichen Bai、Zikai Zhou、Guoxi Huang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Video generation supports digital content creation, with applications in virtual avatars, AI-driven filmmaking, interactive game engines, and world models. State-of-the-art systems achieve controllability primarily through image-to-video (I2V) generation, in which an injected anchor image determines the identity, composition, and style of the resulting video. In modern pipelines, this anchor is synthesized by a text-to-image (T2I) generator. This raises a largely overlooked question: Are images obtained directly from the T2I model truly suitable for current video models? Our analysis shows that anchor aesthetic and quality scores are weakly correlated with downstream video quality. The structure-and-texture score is negatively correlated with I2V video quality in both the visual and motion dimensions. Fine-tuning the T2I model on an image reward accordingly yields anchors with visibly denser structural and character detail: they score higher on image metrics, yet lower the video reward on every I2V model we test and change downstream motion unpredictably. We therefore introduce Golden Anchor Learning (GAL), to our knowledge the first framework for learning golden anchors by optimizing a T2I generator with downstream video feedback. GAL evaluates candidate anchors through the videos they induce under a frozen I2V model and converts video rewards into image-level preferences for DPO fine-tuning of the T2I generator. On all four I2V models, GAL raises the VBench average by $2.97\%$--$8.46\%$ over the untuned generator. The rendered clips also score higher under VideoReward-Overall, and their extracted frames under HPSv3 and UniPercept. GAL yields consistent motion gains across all four I2V models, as measured by a $5.62\%$--$38.24\%$ increase in VBench dynamic degree. Although GAL is trained only on HunyuanVideo-1.5 renders, these gains extend to the other three I2V models.

Fix the Match, Not the Solver: STITCH -- a Self-Supervised Compatibility Measure for Eroded Visual Puzzles

Fix the Match, Not the Solver: STITCH -- a Self-Supervised Compatibility Measure for Eroded Visual Puzzles

Nadav Alali、Ohad Ben-Shahar

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Unlike the clean and pixel-aligned complete pieces of standard jigsaw benchmarks, real-world puzzle fragments lose their boundary regions to erosion, destroying the signal that classical compatibility measures (CMs) rely on. We first re-establish, via oracle experiments, that the CM, not the solver, is the performance bottleneck: with a perfect CM, even a simple greedy solver achieves perfect reconstruction. This motivates STITCH, a self-supervised CM that sidesteps missing boundaries by comparing frozen ViT patch tokens from piece interiors via a lightweight cross-attention head (~249K trainable parameters), trained without manual annotation. Plugged into unchanged compatibility-based solvers, STITCH reaches .992/.954 Abs/Perf on JPwLEG-5 (+.629 Perf over the best prior method), nearly saturating the benchmark. On a new ImageNet 7x7 benchmark, it outperforms all alternatives, including inpaint-then-match pipelines built on modern generative models. Importantly, performance remains robust across erosion levels and generalizes to puzzles with over 40% missing pieces without retraining. Trained on the recent GAP datasets of irregular, heavily eroded fragments, the same CM recipe enables compatibility-based solvers to surpass recent learning-based solvers on GAP-3 and GAP-5. Since this leap in performance is achieved by a pluggable CM module, the takeaway is clear: fix the match, not the solver.

多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散
2026-10-09

PreCoG: Preview-Edit for Efficient Video Correction in Generation-Time

PreCoG: Preview-Edit for Efficient Video Correction in Generation-Time

Yilai Liu、Wenqi Pei、Zhifeng Lu、Shiyuan Zhang、Zongxi Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Iterative text-to-video generation relies on feedback to correct compositional and temporal failures. However, existing methods typically require another full generation run for each correction, repeating computation and potentially disrupting already correct content. We introduce PreCoG, an iterative framework for correcting semantic failures during video generation. PreQA diagnoses failures from low-cost previews and constructs corrective conditions, while PreEdit edits cached generation trajectories under these conditions, attenuating orthogonal changes to limit disruption of already correct content. PreCoG completes generation only for the selected candidate, reusing its cached trajectory to reduce redundant computation and help preserve semantic consistency between the preview and the final video. Experiments show that PreCoG achieves the highest VBench and T2V-CompBench scores and approaches VSR's leading VS2 score at $0.56\times$ its inference time. It also outperforms GENMAC on all three quality and alignment metrics at $0.86\times$ its inference time.

目标检测/分割生成模型/扩散医学图像
2026-10-09

Stochastic Finite-Transition Learning and Exploration for Accelerated MRI Reconstruction

Stochastic Finite-Transition Learning and Exploration for Accelerated MRI Reconstruction

Muhammad Usama Mirza、Esin Öztürk Işık、Tolga Çukur

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generative MRI reconstruction maps an initial source distribution to the target image distribution along a learned recovery trajectory. Common generative methods, such as diffusion and flow, typically learn fine-grained local dynamics whose integration defines this global mapping, hence requiring many network evaluations at inference. Existing few-step sampling approaches can lower computational burden by approximating large transformations from local dynamics, albeit at the expense of reconstruction fidelity. FLEX-MRI instead partitions recovery into a small number of finite intervals between retained states defined by progressive changes in k-space support, and directly learns the reconstruction change across each interval. Because such finite transitions can admit multiple plausible outcomes, FLEX-MRI combines support-relaxed finite-transition learning with stochastic exploration. During training, randomized k-space sampling masks define finite-transition targets with varying support, while the prediction loss remains unrestricted by each individual mask. At inference, FLEX-MRI perturbs a random subset of currently estimated k-space coefficients, allowing the learned predictor to stochastically explore alternative transition paths. Each predicted state is then reconciled with the acquired measurements through data consistency. We theoretically show that this stochastic exploration induces nonzero transition-level variation through the learned recovery map and data consistency without requiring an auxiliary generative sampler. Experiments on multi-coil MRI datasets show that FLEX-MRI outperforms competing methods while maintaining computational efficiency comparable to few-step baselines.

Generative MRI reconstruction maps an initial source distribution to the target image distribution along a learned recovery trajectory. Common generative methods, such as diffusion and flow, typically learn fine-grained local dynamics whose integration defines this global mapping, hence requiring many network evaluations at inference. Existing few-step sampling approaches can lower computational burden by approximating large transformations from local dynamics, albeit at the expense of reconstruction fidelity. FLEX-MRI instead partitions recovery into a small number of finite intervals between retained states defined by progressive changes in k-space support, and directly learns the reconstruction change across each interval. Because such finite transitions can admit multiple plausible outcomes, FLEX-MRI combines support-relaxed finite-transition learning with stochastic exploration. Each predicted state is then reconciled with the acquired measurements through data consistency. Experiments on multi-coil MRI datasets show that FLEX-MRI outperforms competing methods while maintaining computational efficiency comparable to few-step baselines.

Space Over Statistic, Retraining Over Ranking: Auditing Training Labels Under Label-Only Backdoors

Space Over Statistic, Retraining Over Ranking: Auditing Training Labels Under Label-Only Backdoors

Arnab Dey、Mohammad Mahfuzul Islam

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Some backdoor attacks never touch an image. FLIP only changes labels: on CIFAR-10, flipping 3% of them makes a ResNet-32 send 99.7% of triggered source-class images to the attacker's class. The usual defenses look for the poison inside a model trained on those same labels. So when they struggle, is the problem the test they run, or the space they run it in? We answer with a controlled swap: we keep the test, the labels and the metric fixed, change only the feature space, and judge each defense by retraining the victim on what it keeps. The space decides. On dog$\to$cat the same nearest-neighbor check scores AUROC 0.546 on the poisoned model's features, 0.967 on frozen DINOv2 features that never saw a label, and 0.995 on the same network trained on clean labels, an oracle control rather than a defense. We reproduce SPECTRE, the defense FLIP's authors recommend, to 90.94 / 0.97 against its published 91.47 / 1.7, then price it: on truck$\to$deer it deletes 20% of every class, while a prototype audit on the frozen features deletes 6% for higher clean accuracy and lower attack success. On three harder pairs that audit leaves about a quarter of the attack standing. A count read off encoder geometry and clean labels, before any attack is run, separates the pair where the audit removes the attack from those where it only partly does, though on a single zero-count pair. Three results warn against the easy story. A better detection score does not mean a safer model: on two of four pairs Confident Learning scores the higher AUROC, and on dog$\to$cat leaves 2.6 times the attack success. The prototype family, our best static performer, is the first an adaptive attacker slips past. And past a point, catching more poisons stops reducing attack success. Check labels in a space that never learned them, and judge a defense by the retrained model, not by its detection score.

GeoGR: Spatial-Semantic Token Compression and Group Routing for Efficient 3D VLMs

GeoGR: Spatial-Semantic Token Compression and Group Routing for Efficient 3D VLMs

Yongxin Zheng、Hao Wu、Fang Li、Hu Fu、Peiran Yin 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Multi-view representations enable pretrained image and video vision-language models to understand 3D scenes, but their long visual token sequences make inference costly. Redundancy arises both from repeated observations of the same scene regions across views and from processing all visual tokens at every LLM layer, regardless of their relevance to the current computation. We propose GeoGR, a two-stage framework that jointly compresses scene representations and visual computation. At the projector level, GeoSemZip consolidates cross-view observations into 3D voxel tokens and combines semantic saliency with spatial coverage to construct a compact scene representation. Unselected voxel features are aggregated into contextual anchors to retain scene information that would otherwise be discarded. At the LLM level, we identify an effective visual computation window and observe that query-conditioned token relevance remains locally stable but changes over longer layer intervals. Guided by these findings, GroupRoute combines late entry and early exit with recoverable group-wise skipping. Low-relevance tokens bypass complete LLM layers while retaining their hidden states and can rejoin the active group at subsequent anchor layers selected under a computation budget. For compression-aware post-training, we construct a compact dataset of 67K samples by retaining 30% of the original training pool. With this protocol, GeoGR retains 95.2% and 96.8% of the dense post-trained models' performance on LLaVA-OneVision-7B and Video-3D LLM-7B, respectively, while accelerating LLM prefill by $6.8\times$ and $6.1\times$. These results demonstrate the consistent effectiveness of GeoGR across 3D VLMs with and without explicit 3D spatial encoding, highlighting a favorable performance-efficiency trade-off. The code is available at https://github.com/EIT-NLP/GeoGR.

2026-10-09

What Visual Representations Do World Action Models Need?

What Visual Representations Do World Action Models Need?

Kai Liu、Jiangran Lyu、Tingyu Deng、Yixin Zheng、Jingtian Wan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

World Action Models (WAMs) inherit powerful priors from pretrained video generation models, but their variational autoencoder (VAE) representations are optimized for visual reconstruction rather than manipulation. External visual representations can improve manipulation, but existing methods couple representation choices with different integration mechanisms, obscuring their individual effects. We systematically compare visual representations, five integration mechanisms, and feature combinations under controlled settings. Our study yields three main findings. First, strong general-purpose visual representations provide broader robustness gains and can match or outperform representations pretrained for specific visual capabilities. Second, channel-wise joint prediction achieves the highest average success rate; further analyses suggest that future-feature prediction and the action expert's effective use of added visual information contribute to its gains. Third, additional sources should be selected for the task-relevant information they contribute beyond existing representations, as the same source can help one combination but harm another. However, even the best evaluated composition provides a smaller additional gain than the initial improvement from adding a strong general-purpose representation to the VAE-only baseline. We instantiate the resulting Representation Recipe as R$^2$-WAM. It achieves state-of-the-art performance on RoboCasa GR1 Tabletop with an 84.7% success rate and reaches 92.8% on RoboTwin 2.0, improving over the VAE-only baselines by 5.9 and 2.2 percentage points, respectively. Taken together, these findings suggest that future work should prioritize stronger general-purpose visual representations and more effective mechanisms for integrating them into WAMs, with feature composition serving as a supplementary route to improvement.

Taken together, these findings suggest that future work should prioritize stronger general-purpose visual representations and more effective mechanisms for integrating them into WAMs, with feature composition serving as a supplementary route to improvement.

FlyHack: Stress-Testing Mechanistic Claims in a Connectome-Constrained Visual Model

FlyHack: Stress-Testing Mechanistic Claims in a Connectome-Constrained Visual Model

Aashish Dhawan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Connectome-constrained neural networks make biological components directly accessible to intervention, but their mechanistic conclusions can still depend on the comparison model, intervention, circuit representation and trained solution. We stress-test these dependencies in flyvis, a \emph{Drosophila} motion-vision model, using its 50-model ensemble, task-matched causal CNN controls and replicated interventions. At the level of model behaviour, three-level reverse-phi reversal in flyvis is strongly period- and velocity-dependent and also appears in task-matched CNNs; neither family reproduces the fly's stationary-sawtooth response. Adversarial direction reversal requires a perturbation bound of 0.21--0.27 times stimulus contrast in flyvis versus 0.13--0.25 in CNNs, but the gap over a parameter-matched CNN largely closes for larger CNNs. At the level of mechanism, matching attribution to the intervention matters: for held-out-mean replacement, first-order edge attribution patching reaches pooled AUROC 0.970 and Spearman 0.872, versus 0.933 and 0.126 when its zero-aligned score is evaluated against the same ground truth. Circuit conclusions also vary with representation and ensemble member. Greedy cell-type sets meet a model-relative T4/T5 tuning criterion in 25 of 50 models, while T4/T5 output silencing reduces the tested optic-flow behaviour to at most two of eight clips in 40. In six models where the cell-type search first succeeds, the corresponding connection sets can be pruned to 13--31 added classes; under an additional two-sided response-amplitude check, only two of those six first-success cell-type sets still meet the tuning threshold. These results support a narrower use of connectome-constrained models: they make mechanistic hypotheses directly testable, but the resulting claims must be scoped to the model family, intervention, representation and trained solution.

PAGE: Parts-Aware Guidance for Co-Speech Gesture Portrait Video Generation

PAGE: Parts-Aware Guidance for Co-Speech Gesture Portrait Video Generation

Yabiao Wang、Jiafu Wu、Yicheng Xu、Teng Hu、Shengzhe Zhou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Co-speech gesture video generation aims to synthesize realistic, audio-driven portrait videos from a single reference image and a speech signal. Despite advances in visual quality, diffusion-based approaches still suffer from degraded local details and imperfect audio-visual alignment. We present PAGE (Parts-Aware GuidancE), a general, backbone-agnostic optimization framework for diffusion-based generation that enhances local spatial fidelity and global audio-visual consistency at both the training-objective and denoising-trajectory levels. During training, we propose Parts-Specific Reweighting (PSR), which adaptively increases supervision in semantically important regions such as lips, eyes, and hands, improving visual fidelity in perceptually critical areas. During inference, we introduce Consistency Classifier Guidance (CCG), an audio-conditioned latent classifier that identifies and corrects audio-visual inconsistencies in the generated video. We further design Differential Guidance (DG), an efficient inference strategy that refines facial and body motion separately while avoiding cross-region interference. To enable large-scale evaluation, we construct the Chinese Announcer Speech (CAS) dataset, the first comprehensive Chinese benchmark for co-speech gesture portrait video generation. Compared with the widely used PATS dataset, CAS includes substantially more identities, video clips, and scene diversity. Extensive experiments on both PATS and CAS demonstrate that PAGE achieves state-of-the-art performance in perceptual quality, gesture diversity, and audio-visual alignment. Project page: https://aigc-explorer.github.io/PAGE/.

Project page: https://aigc-explorer.github.io/PAGE/.

Sonic-AMG: Streaming Human Motion Generation from Spatial Audio

Sonic-AMG: Streaming Human Motion Generation from Spatial Audio

Jingcheng Ni、Zekun Li、Zhiyang Dou、Xiaoyan Cong、Ziyao Zeng 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Modeling human reactions to spatial audio requires understanding both what is heard and where it comes from. Prior work generates motion offline from complete acoustic and spatial input sequences. Humans, however, react to sounds without knowing what they will hear next. We present Sonic-AMG, to our knowledge the first framework for streaming human motion generation from spatial audio. The model encodes acoustic content and body-relative source geometry as separate tokens. Conditioned on these tokens, a causal transformer with a flow head generates continuous motion latents, from which a causal decoder reconstructs motion segments. Standard teacher-forced training derives motion histories and body-relative source locations from ground-truth motion, whereas streaming inference relies on generated motion for both, resulting in a training-inference mismatch. To reduce this history discrepancy, we introduce rehearsal training, which combines full-sequence teacher-forced supervision with supervised prediction along short autoregressive self-rollouts. To keep spatial conditioning consistent with generated motion, we propose spatial feedback, which recomputes body-relative source locations from generated root poses during both rehearsal training and inference. On SAM, Sonic-AMG achieves state-of-the-art FID and R-precision. It further supports real-time online generation of coherent reactions across sound events and source locations. Project page: https://jingchengni.com/sonic-amg/.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散
2026-10-09

Decoding Register Tokens Preserves Global Scene Details

Decoding Register Tokens Preserves Global Scene Details

David Jacob Drexlin、Julius Hense、Jonas Dippel

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Pretrained vision transformers learn structured image representations spanning spatial patch tokens and non-spatial tokens. Recent analyses suggest that non-spatial register tokens encode global scene properties such as illumination, blur, and style. Yet patch-based image decoders discard these outputs, leaving the decoder to recover image appearance from patches alone. We investigate whether retaining register features improves decoding when the complete spatial patch representation is already available. We study this question using representation autoencoders (RAEs) with frozen register-equipped encoders. Our decoder processes four register tokens together with all patch tokens through joint self-attention. Appearance probes, paired reconstruction-error analysis, and fixed-decoder register substitutions examine which global image properties are accessible from registers and how their values affect reconstructed pixels. For generation, we train a multi-head attention pooling (MHAP) predictor to recover register features from sampled patches, keeping the pretrained diffusion model and its samples unchanged. On ImageNet-1K, our ViT-B decoders achieve mean reconstruction FIDs of 0.472 with DINOv2 and 0.442 with DINOv3, reductions of 9.5% and 5.2% relative to their respective patch-only baselines. The corresponding PSNRs are 19.73 and 20.54 dB. For generation, our DINOv2 models achieve a mean FID of 1.641, 9.8% below the patch-only baseline on identical sampled patches.

The Evidence Anchoring Boundary: Where Visual Interventions Matter and When They Help in Multimodal LLMs

The Evidence Anchoring Boundary: Where Visual Interventions Matter and When They Help in Multimodal LLMs

Qiji Zhou、Junyi Hu、Peiyu Wang、Yue Zhang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visual interventions provide a training-free way to reduce hallucinations in multimodal large language models. However, an intervention may have little leverage at some layers and may damage a correct answer even when it changes the output. We investigate where visual interventions retain leverage and when they improve predictions. First, we apply the same visual-residual edit at successive decoder layers, matching its RMS strength across depth. Across six checkpoints from three model families, we observe a persistent drop in intervention effect and define its operational onset for this edit as the \textbf{Evidence Anchoring Boundary (EAB)}. With boundaries fixed on calibration queries, held-out effects on disjoint evaluation queries before EAB are $13.78\times$--$26.10\times$ larger than those after it. Comparison with intermediate readouts shows that the edit can lose leverage before answer preferences stabilize. Second, we compare counterfactual subtraction across presence, attribute, and relation questions. Subtraction can correct a misleading preference, leave a decision unchanged, or remove a preference supporting the correct answer. Based on these observations, we develop calibrated selective routing, which preserves the original prediction unless calibration supports an intervention for the question type. It improves full-set attribute accuracy by $1.56$--$1.81$ percentage points across three checkpoints, while universal subtraction is neutral or harmful.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散遥感
2026-10-09

Learning Representations to Guide Pixel Diffusion Transformers

Learning Representations to Guide Pixel Diffusion Transformers

Zihao Pan、Mengting Chen、Cheng Shi、Taihang Hu、Zhengrong Yue 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visual representations are largely developed for understanding, yet what makes them useful for generation remains unclear. We study this question in pixel diffusion transformers and uncover two key insights. First, better representation quality does not necessarily translate into greater generative utility, as externally aligned semantics can be progressively lost during pixel synthesis. Second, architectural separation with specialized pixel decoders mainly redistributes semantic and spatial modeling, without necessarily yielding representations better suited to generation. These insights suggest that a useful generative representation must be both properly formed and actively exploited. Motivated by these insights, we introduce Representation Guidance (RG), which directly forms and exploits internal representations while retaining a plain Transformer architecture. A low-dimensional bottleneck forces generation-relevant information into a compact representation, which is then explicitly used to guide decoder generation by modifying the flow-matching target according to its induced prediction offset. RG learns clearer semantic and spatial structure while scaling efficiently in both parameters and computation. On ImageNet 256x256, RG-L/16 with only 459M parameters outperforms substantially larger pixel diffusion transformers, while RG-XL/16 achieves an FID of 1.55. RG further reaches an FID of 1.74 at 512x512 and extends to text-to-image generation, achieving 0.89 on GenEval and 87.1 on DPG-Bench.

2026-10-08

Shift-Aware Identity-Guided Latent Refinement for Referring Audio--Visual Segmentation

Shift-Aware Identity-Guided Latent Refinement for Referring Audio--Visual Segmentation

Kun Li、Sami Sebastian Brandt、Michael Ying Yang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Referring Audio--Visual Segmentation (Ref-AVS) is an emergent multimodal task requiring the precise identification and segmentation of a specific object based on a natural language expression grounded in both visual and auditory cues. Unlike traditional AVS, which segments generic sound sources, or referring video object segmentation (Ref-VOS), which relies solely on visual--textual grounding, Ref-AVS necessitates fine-grained tri-modal interaction across vision, audio, and language. However, existing methods often fail to maintain consistency when subjected to target shift, a phenomenon where the model erroneously transfers its focus between objects due to transient occlusions, acoustic fading, or identity ambiguities. To address this, we propose a novel Shift-Aware Identity-guided Latent (SAIL) refinement framework. It introduces a dual-token strategy that decouples frame-level dynamics from a global semantic anchor to explicitly model target-shift cues. We design a shift-aware refinement module that rectifies drift by aligning tokens with domain-specific evidence. Finally, we leverage a decoder that integrates the refined embeddings with a continuous memory update to maintain spatio-temporal coherence and identity consistency throughout the video sequence. Extensive experiments conducted on two Ref-AVS benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of our proposed method, significantly outperforming existing AVS, Ref-VOS, and Ref-AVS approaches. The code will be released after publication.

Referring Audio--Visual Segmentation (Ref-AVS) is an emergent multimodal task requiring the precise identification and segmentation of a specific object based on a natural language expression grounded in both visual and auditory cues. Unlike traditional AVS, which segments generic sound sources, or referring video object segmentation (Ref-VOS), which relies solely on visual--textual grounding, Ref-AVS necessitates fine-grained tri-modal interaction across vision, audio, and language. However, existing methods often fail to maintain consistency when subjected to target shift, a phenomenon where the model erroneously transfers its focus between objects due to transient occlusions, acoustic fading, or identity ambiguities. To address this, we propose a novel Shift-Aware Identity-guided Latent (SAIL) refinement framework. It introduces a dual-token strategy that decouples frame-level dynamics from a global semantic anchor to explicitly model target-shift cues. We design a shift-aware refinement module that rectifies drift by aligning tokens with domain-specific evidence. Finally, we leverage a decoder that integrates the refined embeddings with a continuous memory update to maintain spatio-temporal coherence and identity consistency throughout the video sequence. Extensive experiments conducted on two Ref-AVS benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of our proposed method, significantly outperforming existing AVS, Ref-VOS, and Ref-AVS approaches. The code will be released after publication.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM医学图像

Can Multimodal Decision Models Classify Breast Ultrasound Images? Benchmarking CLEF and CLEF-Flash Across Prompts and Datasets

Can Multimodal Decision Models Classify Breast Ultrasound Images? Benchmarking CLEF and CLEF-Flash Across Prompts and Datasets

Tran Cao Minh、Hai Tran、Ha Minh Tan

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multimodal decision models return probabilities over predefined alternatives, offering a structured interface for image classification. This study benchmarks inference with CLEF and CLEF-Flash on 2818 breast ultrasound images from Dataset B, BUSI, and BUS-BRA. Dataset B and BUS-BRA use binary benign-malignant classification, whereas BUSI includes normal, benign, and malignant categories. Model parameters remain unchanged, and original images are submitted without user-defined preprocessing. Three prompts provide minimal labels, an explicit classification task, or sonographic criteria. We report accuracy, macro F1, balanced accuracy, macro precision, throughput, and latency. CLEF-Flash with the clinical prompt achieves the highest accuracy on all three datasets: 74.85% on Dataset B, 66.28% on BUSI, and 51.63% on BUS-BRA. On BUSI, however, CLEF with the task-guided prompt achieves the highest macro F1 (64.51%) and balanced accuracy (68.72%). CLEF-Flash has lower latency in every matched comparison. Clinical instructions improve CLEF-Flash accuracy relative to the minimal prompt but reduce CLEF accuracy on all datasets. Confusion matrices reveal frequent benign-to-malignant errors, particularly on BUS-BRA, where all configurations fall below the 67.63% accuracy of a majority-class predictor. These findings demonstrate dataset-and prompt-dependent performance and motivate class-specific evaluation before applying multimodal decision models to breast ultrasound analysis.

Benchmarking Answer-Grounding Consistency in Remote Sensing Visual Question Answering

Benchmarking Answer-Grounding Consistency in Remote Sensing Visual Question Answering

Ping Guo、Zhiqi Huang、Xinran Li、Linkun Yu、Jingyin Pei 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

In grounded remote sensing visual question answering, a model produces a textual answer and identifies relevant objects in the image as grounding evidence. Existing evaluations measure answer correctness and localization quality, but do not directly assess answer-grounding consistency. We propose the Answer-Evidence Support Protocol (AESP) to assess this consistency through evidence support: whether the grounding evidence supports the model's own answer. AESP applies task-specific rules to existence, counting, and comparison questions. We further construct the Grounded Remote Sensing Visual Question Answering (G-RSVQA) benchmark, containing 15,027 samples with questions, reference answers, and all related object-level annotations. Experiments on seven grounded vision--language models reveal failures of evidence support across all three question types. Up to 50.82% of correct answers in the evidence evaluation subset are not supported by the provided grounding evidence.

Towards Physically Grounded Articulated 3D Assets: A Multi-Agent Pipeline and Benchmark

Towards Physically Grounded Articulated 3D Assets: A Multi-Agent Pipeline and Benchmark

Xudong Cai、Ruijie Zhu、Haoliang Shang、Ruining Li、Marc Pollefeys 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

In this paper, we study physically grounded articulated 3D asset generation---moving the goal from reproducing how objects look to capturing how they move and function. Inspired by the literature on procedural and agentic generation, we present Artisan, a system that extends the latest method Articraft by introducing a multi-agent pipeline with physical simulation feedback, enabling comprehensive attributes beyond geometry, appearance, and physically plausible articulation, such as collision-free motion, stability under gravity, and accurate mass and material properties. Furthermore, we construct a unified benchmark spanning four key aspects: spatial fidelity, physical plausibility, downstream task performance, and overall quality. The benchmark encompasses 100 reference assets from ArtVIP and LightWheel, 50 stability prompts, and 50 ABO objects with reference masses, with protocols evaluating various metrics. Through extensive evaluation of more than 10 representative models, Artisan achieves competitive spatial fidelity while attaining the lowest collision rates and highest stability rates among the evaluated methods. Together, our system and benchmark establish insights for advancing 3D generation towards physically plausible, functionally usable assets.

Together, our system and benchmark establish insights for advancing 3D generation towards physically plausible, functionally usable assets.

2026-10-09

Images Talk Back: Guiding MMDiTs by Suppressing Image-to-Text Feedback

Images Talk Back: Guiding MMDiTs by Suppressing Image-to-Text Feedback

Yucheng Yang、Xiaoxiao Sun、Shusheng Yang、Jiakang Chen、Serena Yeung-Levy 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Multimodal diffusion transformers (MMDiTs) allow the evolving image to update internal text representations through joint attention. These representations then guide subsequent image updates, raising a question: does this feedback help correct deviations from the prompt, or preserve them? We find that image-to-text (I2T) feedback can preserve the objects and attributes already represented in the evolving image, including errors. Transplanting I2T messages can rescue failures or disrupt successes. At matched intervention strengths, suppressing the influence of I2T feedback improves generation, whereas opposite controls degrade it. These findings motivate Semantic Feedback Guidance (SFG), which suppresses the influence of I2T feedback by guiding away from an auxiliary branch that strengthens this feedback and weakens text-to-image conditioning. The in-place variant, iSFG, directly suppresses I2T feedback and strengthens text-to-image conditioning, requiring no extra model evaluations. Temporal interventions motivate running the SFG branch during the first 20% of image sampling steps, requiring about $1.2\times$ the base NFE without classifier-free guidance (CFG). Experiments across three image-generation backbones show gains in alignment and preference-model scores. On FLUX, adding SFG to CFG raises GenEval from $0.6661$ to $0.7479$; on SD3.5M, iSFG adds $+1.33$ HPSv2.1 over CFG at zero extra NFE. The control also transfers to text-to-video and image-to-video generation, improving VBench scores. Code is available at https://github.com/yyc1215lll-creator/SFG.

Hide and Seek: HIDE Concepts to SEEK explanations for black box models

Hide and Seek: HIDE Concepts to SEEK explanations for black box models

Bhanu Tokas、Rahul Nair、Rockwell Jackson、Hannah Kerner

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Interpreting machine learning model predictions is a central challenge, particularly for black-box vision models. Concept-based attribution methods interpret models using human-understandable concepts. Existing concept-based methods can only interpret classification and regression models. They require access to model weights or large concept-annotated datasets, and most methods cannot provide image-level attribution. We propose Hide and Seek (HnS), a new method for concept-level attribution that does not require access to model weights or labeled concept datasets. HnS can interpret a wide range of vision models. Given a concept and image set, HnS retrieves concept-aligned images using a vision-language model, localizes the concept within each image, masks the localized region, and measures attribution from the change in the black-box model's output between the original and masked images. This enables faithful attribution by directly measuring the model's behavior and supporting local attributions. We validated HnS on CelebA and COCO-Stuff. Compared to existing methods, HnS showed better agreement with ground-truth attributions. We demonstrated the applicability of HnS on diverse tasks, including image captioning and satellite image segmentation, where no existing concept attribution method can be used. We provided our anonymous code in the supplementary material.

2026-10-09

SpatialASM: Internalizing Active Mentalization for Spatial Reasoning

SpatialASM: Internalizing Active Mentalization for Spatial Reasoning

Yuhong Liu、Shenxi Wu、Junyi Chi、Beichen Zhang、Xuanlang Dai 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

When solving complex spatial reasoning tasks, ordinary humans naturally revisit the scenes from task-relevant viewpoints and mentally reconstruct the spatial layouts from those viewpoints. But can Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) proactively think in the same way? We term this ability Active Spatial Mentalization (ASM), an intrinsic component of visual intelligence that remains substantially underdeveloped in current LVLMs. To bridge this gap, we introduce SpatialASM, a scalable framework to internalize ASM into LVLMs through training-time spatial simulation. SpatialASM employs an allocentric novel-view generator to ground hypothetical viewpoints in visual evidence and produces two complementary forms of supervision: i) explicit ASM trajectories that teach LVLMs when and how to adaptively invoke view planning and imagination during spatial reasoning, and ii) automatically-constructed perspective-taking tasks that further strengthens viewpoint-conditioned scene imagination. On seven benchmarks spanning relatively perception-oriented spatial understanding to more challenging tasks requiring multi-image, cross-view and viewpoint-transformation reasoning, SpatialASM yields performance gain on LVLMs of four LVLM backbones in spatial reasoning while retaining their original general vision capabilities. The experimental results demonstrate that our approach improves average accuracy across the seven spatial benchmarks. And the synthesis of ASM trajectories provides a scalable pathway to alleviate the scarcity of spatial Chain-of-Thought (CoT) supervision for eliciting active viewpoint exploration and mental scene simulation.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散视频理解
2026-10-09

KVeil: First-Place Solution to ECCV 2026 LifeGenIP Challenge on Unlearnable Videos

KVeil: First-Place Solution to ECCV 2026 LifeGenIP Challenge on Unlearnable Videos

Boheng Li、Yueming Xing、Yiming Li、Jie Zhang、Han Qiu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

The rapid development of personalized video generation has raised serious concerns over identity privacy, as a few facial videos of a person now suffice to reproduce their identity in synthetic videos. This report presents the first-place solutions to both phases of the ECCV 2026 LifeGenIP Challenge on Unlearnable Videos, which aims to protect facial videos from unauthorized customization under a limited and imperceptible perturbation budget. For Phase 1, we introduce Reference Capacity Collapse, a pair of complementary losses that degrade the information carried by the reference condition while keeping subsequent video representations dependent on it, together with a Motion-Guided EOT mechanism that improves robustness across unknown inference conditions. Phase 2 further considers tuning-based customization and unseen generative models. To address both threats within a single perturbation, we design a two-layer framework that protects the facial and background regions separately, constructing structure-aligned perturbations from a synthetic target identity in the former and strengthening the white-box disruption of Phase 1 in the latter. Our method achieves nearly perfect protection under white-box reference-based customization, and remains effective under other customization paradigms, various models, and temporal attacks. Both solutions are built upon our recent work LETHE. We hope our solutions can inspire future work on protecting facial videos against unauthorized customization. Our code is available at https://github.com/AntigoneRandy/KVeil.

Spend Where It Matters: Particle Search with MH Refinement for Budget-Constrained Audio-Visual Alignment

Spend Where It Matters: Particle Search with MH Refinement for Budget-Constrained Audio-Visual Alignment

Shivanshu Shekhar、Raghavendra Addanki、Tong Zhang、Uttaran Bhattacharya

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reward-guided inference-time alignment can improve joint audio-visual generation, but optimizing multiple alignment objectives requires many expensive model evaluations, making existing methods costly under limited compute. Particle-based search enables global exploration but incurs estimation error with a finite population, while Metropolis-Hastings (MH) can refine candidates locally but benefits from a strong initialization. We introduce \textbf{S}pend \textbf{W}here \textbf{I}t \textbf{M}atters (\modelname), a two-stage, training-free, reward-agnostic framework that combines particle search with MH refinement under a fixed compute budget. In the first stage, we adaptively allocate particles across denoising checkpoints using online variance estimates, minimizing estimation variance while concentrating compute where it is most useful. This search provides a promising candidate for MH refinement, which uses local noise-and-denoise proposals on completed outputs to further improve reward. By starting from a strong candidate, MH can recover additional reward without requiring a larger particle population or costly global exploration. We show that, at matched compute, combining particle search with MH refinement achieves higher rewards than either approach alone. On \textsc{JavisBench-Mini}, at closely matched compute, \modelname achieves mean relative gains across seven metrics of $30.7\%$ over BoN and $14.1\%$ over EvoSearch with JavisDiT++, and $9.2\%$ over BoN and $17.0\%$ over EvoSearch with LTX-2. Beyond joint audio-visual generation, we also demonstrate the benefits of adaptive particle allocation on SD1.5 image generation, improving AestheticScore by $2.2\%$ and ImageReward by $6.0\%$ over uniform allocation.

WaveMark: Robust Wavelet-Domain Image Watermarking for Diffusion Models

WaveMark: Robust Wavelet-Domain Image Watermarking for Diffusion Models

Jie Cao、Qi Li、Chaklam Cheong、Yutong Jin、Enze Yu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Image watermarking enables provenance tracing for photorealistic images generated by diffusion models. However, state-of-the-art (SOTA) removal attacks can suppress embedded signals with little perceptual degradation. This vulnerability limits the effectiveness of watermarks for intellectual property protection and misuse attribution. We propose WaveMark, a novel wavelet-domain watermarking method that embeds messages in a diffusion model's initial noise. To support robust recovery from perturbed carriers, WaveMark distributes keyed replicas of message bits across spatial positions, wavelet orientations, and scales. Each carrier groups coefficients from four latent channels and encodes a randomized bit through magnitude projection, providing an explicit decoding margin. We evaluate WaveMark with SD2.1 on SDP and MS-COCO Captions under 14 attacks. WaveMark achieves 100.0% bit accuracy (BA) without attacks, while maintaining image quality comparable to the evaluated baselines. Under SOTA MarkNull and MarkNull-A, it retains over 88% BA, outperforming all baselines.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM
2026-10-09

In-Context Learning Can Help Vision-Language Models Overcome Training Priors

In-Context Learning Can Help Vision-Language Models Overcome Training Priors

Kun Wang、Xindi Wu、Sanghyuk Chun、Olga Russakovsky、Esin Tureci 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-language models (VLMs) learn strong statistical regularities during training, which can make them fail to properly respond to visual evidence when input images violate those regularities. It is challenging to disambiguate these prior-induced errors from errors caused by the model missing the underlying visual capabilities. In this work, we use controlled visual in-context learning to encourage the model to focus on the visual evidence, and demonstrate that this alone is frequently sufficient to correct the errors -- suggesting that the models in fact do possess the relevant visual capabilities. Specifically, we show that across five vision-centric tasks, demonstrations yield large gains on prior-conflicting counterfactual examples ($+27.2\%$ for Qwen3-VL 235B and $+15.1\%$ for Qwen3-VL 32B) while leaving accuracy on prior-aligned examples nearly unchanged. These findings generalize across five VLM families with gains from $+9.7\%$ to $+30.3\%$. We analyze visual representations and find that demonstrations indeed redirect attention toward grounding cues while reducing reliance on cues that support the learned prior. Finally, we find that in-context learning is limited when the underlying visual capability is weak or absent. Together, these results show that current VLMs possess visual grounding capabilities that standard evaluations may overlook, underscoring the need for controlled evaluation design in diagnosing and improving model capability.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM
2026-10-09

CV-OPD: Contrastive Vision On-policy Distillation

CV-OPD: Contrastive Vision On-policy Distillation

Haowei Wang、Hao Lin、Qi Zhu、Jiayi Ji、Deyi Ji 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

On-policy distillation (OPD) reveals versatility and convenience during VLM post-training, providing dense supervision for student from teacher. However, we observed an anomaly that the distilled VLM could still answer the questions accurately even when the related visual regions were masked or replaced with noise. Building on this observation, our quantitative and qualitative experiments demonstrate that OPD, which uses the answer as a hint, has failed to teach students to genuinely seek out and rely on visual evidence. Our theoretical analysis suggests that this failure arises from a shortcut enabled by the lack of visual constraints during distillation. To address the issue, we introduce Contrastive Vision On-policy Distillation (CV-OPD), which compares teacher distributions induced by positive and negative crops while conditioning on the same student prefix and augments optimization with a margin-based contrastive constraint, which is modulated with discrepancy gating across token and sample level. Specifically, CV-OPD first introduces a negative teacher unrelated to the answer and designs a Contrastive Divergence Loss (CDL) that constrains the feature distribution of student by contrasting the positive and negative teachers, correcting how the student exploits visual regions. The CV-OPD introduces a Discrepancy Aware Gate (DAG) that uses token-level and sample-level discrepancies to select visually relevant tokens for optimization, directing the student's attention toward visual content. Extensive experiments across a broad range of fine-grained visual benchmarks demonstrate that CV-OPD substantially alleviates shortcut and consistently improves the visual reasoning capabilities of existing VLMs.

However, we observed an anomaly that the distilled VLM could still answer the questions accurately even when the related visual regions were masked or replaced with noise. Building on this observation, our quantitative and qualitative experiments demonstrate that OPD, which uses the answer as a hint, has failed to teach students to genuinely seek out and rely on visual evidence. Our theoretical analysis suggests that this failure arises from a shortcut enabled by the lack of visual constraints during distillation. To address the issue, we introduce Contrastive Vision On-policy Distillation (CV-OPD), which compares teacher distributions induced by positive and negative crops while conditioning on the same student prefix and augments optimization with a margin-based contrastive constraint, which is modulated with discrepancy gating across token and sample level. Specifically, CV-OPD first introduces a negative teacher unrelated to the answer and designs a Contrastive Divergence Loss (CDL) that constrains the feature distribution of student by contrasting the positive and negative teachers, correcting how the student exploits visual regions. The CV-OPD introduces a Discrepancy Aware Gate (DAG) that uses token-level and sample-level discrepancies to select visually relevant tokens for optimization, directing the student's attention toward visual content. Extensive experiments across a broad range of fine-grained visual benchmarks demonstrate that CV-OPD substantially alleviates shortcut and consistently improves the visual reasoning capabilities of existing VLMs.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM
2026-10-09

SheafPrune: Sheaf-Guided Graph Propagation for Visual Token Pruning

SheafPrune: Sheaf-Guided Graph Propagation for Visual Token Pruning

Xiaoyang Ma、Chi Wang、Hong Sun

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Multimodal large language models process the complete visual token sequence, increasing prefill cost even when many tokens are irrelevant to the instruction. Token-wise saliency captures limited inter-token dependence; graph-based selection instead depends on how relations govern importance propagation and ranking. We study support-constrained propagation with node-degree correction. Inspired by sheaf geometry, SheafPrune uses modality-specific maps and a sparse text--visual graph to transfer relevance, then propagates scores on a separate visual graph and corrects them for degree. It retains a hard top-$k$ subset once before the frozen language model. The selector has about $4.5$M parameters and is trained end-to-end through the frozen LLaVA-NeXT-8B backbone using only the language-modeling objective. Explicit retention-ratio conditioning lets one checkpoint serve every budget. Across four backbones, ten benchmarks and seven pruning ratios, with all baselines re-implemented under one protocol, SheafPrune achieves the best retention at $22$ of $28$ backbone--budget settings, with monotonically growing margins in the moderate-to-aggressive regime. On LLaVA-NeXT, retaining $10\%$ of visual tokens yields a $5.15\times$ net prefill speedup and an $84.0\%$ end-to-end FLOPs reduction. Together with component ablations, these results support graph-constrained importance propagation and degree correction as a design for visual token selection across the evaluated backbones and tokenization schemes, improving the trade-off between accuracy retention and inference cost. code:https://github.com/Bob0817912/SheafPrune_ICLR_2027

Beyond a Single Crop: Contextualized Embeddings for Player Re-Identification

Beyond a Single Crop: Contextualized Embeddings for Player Re-Identification

Ge Luo、Changhuo Yang、Weisi Fan、Rui Yang、Shaokai Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Person re-identification is typically addressed by learning identity-discriminative embeddings from individual person crops. However, player re-identification in broadcast sports videos presents a unique challenge: players from the same team often share similar appearances, while video sequences provide repeated observations of the same players under different viewpoints and surrounding contexts. Existing crop-based approaches largely ignore this contextual information, which can be critical for resolving visually similar identities. Here, we propose ContextReID, a contextualized embedding framework that represents a player by jointly considering other players in the same frame and observations from different frames of the broadcast sequence. By incorporating both co-occurring players and cross-frame observations, the model learns identity features relative to the surrounding visual context rather than relying only on isolated appearance cues. We implement this framework with a vision-language model that takes sets of player crops as input and generates context-aware embeddings for individual players. A combined contextual learning and retrieval objective encourages the model to capture cross-frame identity relationships while maintaining discriminative embeddings for player matching. Experiments across three multi-object tracking benchmarks show that ContextReID improves player retrieval over conventional single-crop embedding approaches in both Rank-1 and mAP. These results highlight the utility of spatial and temporal context for reliable player identification in broadcast sports videos.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉
2026-10-09

Jag: Direct Box Prediction for Efficient Visual Grounding

Jag: Direct Box Prediction for Efficient Visual Grounding

Xiuyuan Zhu、Ke Lu、Hao Wu、Zijin Du、Dongming Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Visual grounding combines image--language understanding with geometric prediction, yet autoregressive multimodal models often express a box as a coordinate sequence. Sequential decoding adds computation to the multimodal processing needed for localization. Adapting these representations to box geometry offers a route to direct prediction. We present Jag, which adapts a compact pretrained multimodal model for direct continuous box prediction. A lightweight regression head reads the last valid input-token state, conditioned on the image and referring expression, and predicts all four box coordinates together. Geometric supervision jointly adapts the language backbone, visual merger, and regression head, allowing localization losses to shape both the multimodal representations and the coordinate mapping. Inference retains image--language processing and returns the box in one forward pass, without coordinate generation or text parsing. Across benchmarks, Jag consistently improves grounding accuracy over its base model across the observed target sizes and expression groups. Component ablation show that jointly adapting the representations and head substantially improves accuracy over training the head alone. Against existing specialized grounding models, it achieves competitive accuracy with lower single-request latency and GPU memory use. These results show how supervision tailored to the geometric answer can specialize a compact pretrained multimodal model for accurate grounding and efficient direct prediction. Project page: https://xyzzzh.github.io/Jag/

MixTok: Adaptive Visual Tokenization with Global 1D Tokens and Sparse 2D Refinement

MixTok: Adaptive Visual Tokenization with Global 1D Tokens and Sparse 2D Refinement

Chenge Li、Yefei He、Xinyu Liu、Feng Chen、Hong Zhou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Efficient visual tokenization requires balancing representation compactness with the preservation of fine-grained image content. Conventional grid-based tokenizers allocate tokens uniformly across spatial locations despite non-uniform visual complexity, whereas compact 1D tokenizers aggregate global information but can struggle to preserve local details. We introduce MixTok, a hybrid visual tokenizer that combines a compact 1D global representation with sparse 2D tokens for local refinement. A content-adaptive router ranks candidate locations by refinement priority and determines the number of 2D tokens for each image under a target average token budget. At the highest-scoring locations, 2D token features replace the spatial features recovered from the 1D representation, and a shared decoder reconstructs the image from the resulting mixed features. On ImageNet 256 x 256, MixTok achieves highly competitive reconstruction and generation performance, with an rFID of 1.195 and a gFID of 1.95 at respective average budgets of approximately 128 and 131 content tokens per image.

2026-10-09

A4-MR: Abnormality-aware Anatomical Alignment for MR

A4-MR: Abnormality-aware Anatomical Alignment for MR

Noel D'souza、Zhijian Yang、Nishank Singla、Pranay Doshi、Krisztian Koos 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Recent advances in large-scale foundation models have enabled strong generalization across a wide range of clinical and research tasks in magnetic resonance imaging (MRI). However, existing vision-language MRI models remain limited in anatomy-specific localization and pathology-aware representation, particularly in real-world settings. We introduce A4-MR, a 3D vision-language foundation model trained on approximately 50,000 MRI studies comprising 491,000 series, roughly half of which are paired with radiology reports. A4-MR is designed to: (1) learn anatomy localization through large-scale pretraining with noisy labels; (2) align image and text representations at the anatomical level; and (3) learn distinct representations of healthy and abnormal anatomy. Across a diverse set of downstream tasks, A4-MR outperforms existing foundation models, including a +1.7 AUROC improvement over MedSigLIP for abnormality detection. Using a frozen encoder, A4-MR also surpasses an end-to-end task-specific MedNeXt model for knee segmentation by +1.4 Dice and performs comparably to the best task-specific model for abdominal organ segmentation on AMOS, within 0.4 Dice. Finally, without anatomical prompts, A4-MR localizes 3D anatomy comparably to MedSAM2 provided with ground-truth bounding boxes. Together, these results demonstrate that anatomy-level multimodal alignment and abnormality-aware representation learning can enable a single MRI foundation model to support localization, segmentation, and pathology-related tasks.

Together, these results demonstrate that anatomy-level multimodal alignment and abnormality-aware representation learning can enable a single MRI foundation model to support localization, segmentation, and pathology-related tasks.

Dataset-Specific Encoding of Image Content Underlies Cross-Dataset Failure in Brain MRI Foundation Models

Dataset-Specific Encoding of Image Content Underlies Cross-Dataset Failure in Brain MRI Foundation Models

Minh Sao Khue Luu、Bair N. Tuchinov

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Frozen features of brain MRI foundation models lose accuracy on new datasets, a failure usually blamed on the features encoding where each scan came from. We show that the failure lies elsewhere. Across 17 public datasets and eight frozen representations, every representation recognizes the MRI sequence within each dataset, yet most fail on an unseen one. The cause is neither dataset encoding nor a shift of each dataset: the content itself is encoded along different directions in each dataset. This explains which datasets fail, for the sequence and for sex, and why removing the dataset signal with INLP, LEACE, CORAL or adversarial training never improves sequence transfer. Only the broadly pretrained 3DINO and SAM-Med3D encode the sequence consistently enough to transfer; the brain-specific BrainIAC and BrainFM do no better than an untrained network. Code is available at https://github.com/BrainFM/brainfm-profiler/tree/main/representation_utility.

2026-10-09

EmbodiedCLBench: Evaluating Continual Learning for Self-Evolving Embodied Agents

EmbodiedCLBench: Evaluating Continual Learning for Self-Evolving Embodied Agents

Jie Huang、Yanan Chen、Ruixun Liu、Kaichen He、Xinyi He 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Continual learning requires models to accumulate past experience and transfer it to new tasks. Despite substantial research on continual learning, existing work remains largely confined to non-embodied settings, without extending to embodied environments. To address this gap, we introduce EmbodiedCLBench, the first benchmark in embodied simulation for evaluating the continual learning capability of self-evolving agents. With compositional generalization as the core evaluation principle, our benchmark evaluates whether agents can first learn from basic tasks and then recombine the acquired skills to solve complex, unseen advanced tasks. Based on EmbodiedCLBench, we conduct extensive experiments across multiple agent harnesses and learning methods, revealing consistent limitations and distinctive behaviors. Our results show that while advanced tasks can be hardly solved under zero-shot setting, the experience from basic tasks enables consistent improvement. Such benefit from the experience remains stable even if the task complexity increases. However, this improvement does not scale accordingly with the number of learning samples, and current agents cannot extract meaningful information from additional experience. Collectively, our findings highlight continual learning as a fundamental yet underexplored capability, and our benchmark offers valuable resources for designing effective self-evolving agents in embodied environments. Our code and dataset are available at https://pku-value-lab.github.io/EmbodiedCLBench-homepage.

WoodBR-Cross: A public wood-image dataset and individual-tree-disjoint benchmark for species identification

WoodBR-Cross: A public wood-image dataset and individual-tree-disjoint benchmark for species identification

Daniel Casanova、Alexandre Bahia Gontijo、Arnaldo Candido Junior、Silvana Nisgoski、Pedro Luiz de Paula Filho 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Wood-image benchmarks can yield optimistic estimates when images from the same tree occur in both training and test sets. WoodBR-Cross contains 4,875 transverse-surface images in 38 operational taxonomic classes, linked to 303 distinct trees identified by numeric catalogue witness codes (codigos-testemunho) in the Brazilian Forest Service collection. A species-level subset comprises 3,406 images, 20 classes and 214 trees. Three tasks compare duplicate-aware image partitions for all classes (A), image partitions for the species subset (B1), and individual-tree-disjoint partitions for that subset (B2). In B2, all images from each tree are kept together, so no tree is shared across training, validation or test within a fold. ResNet-50, ConvNeXt-Tiny and ViT-B/16 were evaluated with three outer folds, internal validation and five training seeds. Handcrafted and frozen-feature baselines complement border-only and randomized-label controls. Validation-selected ConvNeXt-Tiny achieved B2 tree-level macro-F1 of 0.8621 +/- 0.0317; frozen DINOv2 features yielded 0.9000 +/- 0.0722. Values are means and standard deviations across three seed-averaged fold means. Results estimate performance on previously unseen trees from this collection.

2026-10-08

MemReg: Streaming Outdoor LiDAR Point Cloud Registration with Hybrid Memory Buffers

MemReg: Streaming Outdoor LiDAR Point Cloud Registration with Hybrid Memory Buffers

Jiuming Liu、Mengmeng Liu、Guangming Wang、Lihao Liu、Michael Ying Yang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Prior outdoor LiDAR registration works are commonly limited by the pair-wise input paradigm, which neglects temporal correlations inherent within streaming sequences. In this paper, we propose a novel multi-frame outdoor point cloud registration network for streaming LiDAR scans enhanced with pose-related memory buffers. The key observation is that long-term temporal LiDAR sequences can provide rich global contextual information to complete sparse measurements, filter outliers, and address low-overlap problems, thereby boosting registration performance. Specifically, two hybrid memory buffers are designed, including an implicit memory feature buffer and an explicit memory pose buffer, to store and dynamically update pose-related temporal features. Moreover, a novel dynamic history weighting module is developed to adaptively fuse current and history pose-related features. Extensive experiments on three outdoor datasets, including KITTI, nuScenes, and Apollo-Southbay, demonstrate state-of-the-art performance of MemReg, surpassing all previous pair-wise methods and also multi-frame SLAM systems. Our method also generalizes surprisingly well to multiview indoor registration scenarios with rather competitive performance on 3DMatch, 3DLoMatch, and ScanNet. Code will be released at https://github.com/IRMVLab/MemReg.
