Dual-Stream Memory for Vision-Language-Action Policies

Dual-Stream Memory for Vision-Language-Action Policies

Yang Wang、Ping Liu、Ruiyu Wang、Xuhua Chen、Lingfeng Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Memory-dependent manipulation requires a policy to track execution state and recover visual evidence that is no longer observable. We propose dual-stream memory for vision-language-action policies, assigning these requirements to complementary representations. A task-agnostic Gated DeltaNet compresses history into one temporal token, supervised by progress prediction and action-conditioned feature reconstruction. A task-conditioned retriever retains frames above a fixed relevance threshold; linear multi-scale fusion encodes each frame into sixteen spatial tokens. We then freeze the temporal compressor and jointly optimize retrieval and policy learning. Across sixteen RoboMME tasks, the complete model achieves 49.22% mean success versus 44.51% for the published FrameSamp+Modul reference, improving fourteen tasks and all four suite means. On four real-robot tasks, mean success reaches 62.5% versus 37.5%, with the largest gains on repeated actions requiring execution-stage discrimination. These results support combining execution context with selective visual access, while failures under cup swaps expose persistent identity tracking as an unresolved requirement.

Deep Learning Approach for RealTime Contraction Detection in Laboring Dairy Cattle

Deep Learning Approach for RealTime Contraction Detection in Laboring Dairy Cattle

Sebastian Puchala、Dominik Gronkiewicz、Marcin Gołębiewski、Grzegorz Grodkowski、Grzegorz Gwardys 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Currently, the animal production sector is undergoing rapid development of techniques that allow automatic monitoring of animal behaviour and health. This paper presents a computer vision-based approach for the automatic recognition of labor contractions in cows. During the course of the study, videos depicting 100 cow births were collected. From the obtained videos, 8500 cow contraction actions and about 25 000 non-contraction sequences were extracted which constituted the dataset. Leveraging image processing techniques and deep learning algorithms like Convolutional Neural Networks (CNNs) and Vision Transformers (ViTs), our methodology extracts relevant features from video frames to detect and classify contractions. Our study demonstrates a high accuracy rate of over 95% in detecting labor contractions. This automated system has the potential to enhance productivity and animal welfare in the dairy industry by providing real-time monitoring and facilitating timely intervention during difficult calvings. The video demonstration of the system can be found at the following URL: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8fTSG5D7dZ0

Destination-Conditioned Adaptation with Semantic-Geometric Alignment for Generative Inbetweening

Destination-Conditioned Adaptation with Semantic-Geometric Alignment for Generative Inbetweening

Zhiyu Xu、Wei Shang、Yushen Xu、Wenwen Li、Zhiyan Xu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generative inbetweening aims to synthesize temporally coherent intermediate frames between a start frame and a target frame. Although pretrained image-to-video diffusion models provide strong priors for large-motion inbetweening, they are not explicitly designed to reach a specified target frame. They may therefore generate realistic individual frames while drifting from the target or producing unstable motion under large displacements and non-rigid dynamics. We propose DSG-GI, a framework that adapts pretrained latent video diffusion models through \textbf{D}estination-Conditioned Adaptation and \textbf{S}emantic-\textbf{G}eometric Alignment. Destination-Conditioned Adaptation treats the target frame as the destination of the sequence and provides temporally progressive guidance along the generated trajectory. Semantic-Geometric Alignment aligns the token-wise spatio-temporal relational geometry of backbone features with that of a frozen video foundation encoder and applies a flow-matching-aware latent flow constraint to samples near the data endpoint of the probability path. Together, these components strengthen destination guidance and preserve coherent semantic-geometric motion structure. Extensive experiments on standard and large-motion inbetweening benchmarks demonstrate improvements in temporal stability, target-frame faithfulness, and perceptual quality. Our code will be made publicly available.

High-Throughput Generative Video Super-Resolution on a Single Consumer GPU

High-Throughput Generative Video Super-Resolution on a Single Consumer GPU

Georgios Panaretou、Sotirios Chatzis

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generative video super-resolution (VSR) can recover visually compelling high-frequency detail by leveraging diffusion priors, but iterative sampling and heavy spatio-temporal coupling make practical deployments difficult. We study a different operating point: practical HD generative VSR on a single high-end consumer GPU without end-to-end retraining. To this end, we present a zero-shot framework built entirely from pretrained components. The proposed system decouples spatial enhancement from temporal stabilization. A time-distilled one-step diffusion backbone handles spatial upscaling, while temporal coherence is promoted by a lightweight pretrained flow-based interpolator that synthesizes intermediate frames between super-resolved keyframes. To further improve scalability, we introduce a resolution-aware hybrid attention scheme that, in a zero-shot manner, replaces selected high-resolution global-attention layers with neighborhood attention while preserving global attention in semantically critical low-resolution layers, while also improving temporal consistency. Experiments show that the proposed framework reaches up to ~32 FPS at 720p resolution under interleaved inference on a single NVIDIA RTX 4090. At 4K, the proposed attention substitution can yield up to a 44% speedup over the original attention implementation. Across standard VSR benchmarks, the method consistently shifts the speed-quality trade-off decisively toward runtime efficiency, trading reconstruction fidelity for strong no-reference perceptual quality, while remaining reasonably competitive against generative approaches in temporal consistency. It also exposes clear limitations in handling more challenging motion, which a simple motion gate partially mitigates. Code will be released upon acceptance.

It also exposes clear limitations in handling more challenging motion, which a simple motion gate partially mitigates. Code will be released upon acceptance.

GuidedNav: Shaping Vision-Language Navigation Representations through Pre-action Attention and Spatial Guidance

GuidedNav: Shaping Vision-Language Navigation Representations through Pre-action Attention and Spatial Guidance

Jingyu Guo、Jiaxin Huang、Chengxing Lin、Xiaoyu Luo、Cheng Wen 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-and-language navigation (VLN) requires an agent to track progress through an extended instruction, identify landmarks relevant to its current objective, and understand the surrounding spatial structure to act. End-to-end navigation policies typically rely on action supervision to implicitly learn these capabilities. Without explicit guidance on the evidence underlying each decision, however, policies can overfit to correlations predictive of training actions rather than learn the task-relevant grounding and geometry needed for generalization. Our insight is to guide the representations that connect instructions to actions: identifying what remains to be done determines which visual evidence matters, while understanding the geometry around that evidence informs how to move. We introduce GuidedNav, a unified framework that explicitly shapes these representations through training-time guidance. Subtask and landmark guidance steer native pre-action attention toward the active instruction segment and relevant visual regions, grounding action prediction in the current objective. Spatial guidance aligns the policy's internal representations with geometric features that capture long-horizon context and local spatial structure. Together, these objectives strengthen navigation capabilities while avoiding the inference overhead of intermediate textual reasoning and online geometric encoding. We also introduce Guided-R2R and Guided-RxR, two annotation-enriched navigation datasets that support more interpretable VLN research. GuidedNav establishes state-of-the-art performance among the compared monocular RGB policies, improving success rates by 8.1% and 13.1% relative to the strongest prior results on R2R-CE and RxR-CE val-unseen, respectively, while achieving faster inference. Extensive experiments further demonstrate the effectiveness and real-world transferability of our approach.

Extensive experiments further demonstrate the effectiveness and real-world transferability of our approach.

What Do Visual-Head Discovery Scores Actually Find? Attention Source, Necessity, and Random-Control Pitfalls in Vision-Language Models

What Do Visual-Head Discovery Scores Actually Find? Attention Source, Necessity, and Random-Control Pitfalls in Vision-Language Models

Md Abdul Kadir、Daniel Sonntag

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Attention heads that attend to image regions relevant to a question are increasingly used to steer, ground and interpret vision-language models (VLMs) and to reduce their hallucinations. They are mostly found by ranking heads with an attention- pattern score and validated by steering or masking them. We ask how the choices inside such a discovery score shape the heads it returns, and which of the two validations identifies them. On Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct we vary the query type, the discovery corpus and the token positions whose attention is read (the attention source) in a full 2 x 3 x 2 design, and test all 12 head-sets for steerability (CERS: Contrastive Evidence-Region Steering score) and necessity (NCS: Necessity Con- trast Score) against layer-matched random head-sets. (1) The attention source and the query type reshape the selected heads far more than the corpus: changing the source keeps a median of 49 of the top-100 heads, the query 55 and the cor- pus 84, while two halves of the same data share 96-99. (2) Necessity follows the attention source: head-sets discovered from query-span attention are more nec- essary than last-token ones (difference 0.083, p=6 x 10^-10), and the two re- ferring + query-span sets lie 17-19 standard deviations above 250 layer-matched random sets (Holm-adjusted p=0.048). The referring + query-span effect re- plicates on several recent VLMs. (3) Steerability is not specific to the discovered heads: no discovered head-set steers better than its layer-matched random sets (rank p >= 0.14), and random sets drawn from the same or from any layer span the discovered sets' scores. Necessity therefore separates the discovery settings, while steering at this strength does not. We turn these findings into a protocol for reporting visual-head discoveries.

UniMergeTrack: Hierarchical Model Merging for General RGB-X Visual Object Tracking

UniMergeTrack: Hierarchical Model Merging for General RGB-X Visual Object Tracking

Shiao Wang、Xiao Wang、Yuhe Ding、Jiarui Xu、Zikang Yan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

RGB-X tracking leverages complementary modalities, such as thermal infrared, event, or depth, to improve robustness under challenging conditions. Existing general-purpose RGB-X trackers typically follow two paradigms: independent task-specific training and joint training across modality combinations. However, the former suffers from knowledge isolation and model redundancy, while the latter introduces optimization interference across heterogeneous tasks. In this work, we present UniMergeTrack, a new model merging paradigm that preserves task-specific optimization while enabling effective cross-task knowledge integration. UniMergeTrack first trains modality-specific trackers independently and then performs hierarchical model merging. Specifically, transferable backbones are merged across RGB-X tasks via high-pass-filtered merging, and complemented by lightweight task-specific experts that preserve modality-specific cues. Tracking heads are merged within each task using optimal transport-guided channel alignment to mitigate hidden channel mismatch. A subsequent distribution alignment fine-tuning stage further improves compatibility between the unified backbone and task-specific heads. Extensive experiments on the LasHeR, RGBT234, VisEvent, VOT-RGBD22, COESOT, and CDTB benchmarks demonstrate that UniMergeTrack achieves state-of-the-art or competitive performance across RGB-Thermal, RGB-Event, and RGB-Depth tracking tasks, while exhibiting strong generalization to unseen datasets. The source code will be released upon acceptance. The source code of this paper will be released on https://github.com/Event-AHU/OpenEvTracking.

The source code will be released upon acceptance. The source code of this paper will be released on https://github.com/Event-AHU/OpenEvTracking.

Unified Generative Video Compression with Autoregressive and Bidirectional Video Diffusion

Unified Generative Video Compression with Autoregressive and Bidirectional Video Diffusion

Tianyu Zhang、Shuai Huo、Dengchao Jin、Hewei Liu、Meng Lei 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Video diffusion models can reconstruct visually compelling content from severely compressed representations at ultra-low bitrates, but they impose a rigid deployment trade-off. Bidirectional (BI) models achieve high quality through dense full-sequence attention, yet require the entire sequence before reconstruction and incur substantial latency. Autoregressive (AR) models enable low-delay streaming, but their restricted temporal context reduces compression efficiency. This tension is particularly relevant when a video stream must be decoded in real time yet is also stored for later, quality-oriented playback, making AR and BI models natural choices for the live and replay stages, respectively. To support both stages, we present UniGVC, a Unified Generative Video Codec that supports two decoding modes from a single bitstream. UniGVC-BI employs a BI-DiT with dense attention for quality-oriented random-access decoding, whereas UniGVC-AR uses windowed AR-DiT for low-delay streaming. Both paths share a hierarchical latent codec that recurrently produces the same conditions with ultra-low bitrates, allowing UniGVC-BI to serve as a conditional teacher for single-step distillation of UniGVC-AR. A tiny conditional video decoder further reduces streaming latency while preserving most of the reconstruction quality. On standard 1080p benchmarks, UniGVC-BI achieves the best overall rate-distortion-perception trade-off among the evaluated methods and produces temporally stable video at rates as low as 0.0005 bits per pixel. Meanwhile, UniGVC-AR remains competitive while decoding 1080p video at 36.85 FPS, over $250\times$ faster than UniGVC-BI. Code and models will be released later.

TraceICL: Thread-Resolved Demonstration Curation with Consistency Calibration for Robust Multimodal In-Context Learning

TraceICL: Thread-Resolved Demonstration Curation with Consistency Calibration for Robust Multimodal In-Context Learning

Yigit Yilmaz、Elena Petrova、Mehmet Kaya、Lucia Rossi、Amir Rahman 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Interleaved multimodal in-context learning (ICL) lets frozen large vision-language models (LVLMs) acquire new tasks from a handful of image-text-answer demonstrations, yet it remains fragile: demonstrations bind to the wrong images, redundant exemplars crowd out informative ones, and noisy answers are copied verbatim into predictions. We present AVA, a training-free framework that organizes the demonstration pipeline of multimodal ICL into three composable stages. A Thread-Resolved Decomposer (TRD) segments the interleaved context into per-sample threads and re-stitches them with explicit boundaries, restoring image-text binding. An Influence-scored Demonstration Selector (IDS) estimates each candidate's marginal influence with one extra forward pass, removes redundancy, and orders the survivors by descending value. A Weak-to-Strong Consistency Calibration (WSC) module scores each demonstration by a weak sibling model's reconstruction confidence and softly reweights its contribution during decoding. On five vision-language benchmarks with interleaved 4-shot prompts, AVA improves the strongest same-setting baseline by +1.7 points on average, with gains of up to +2.4 points on VizWiz, shrinks the require-visual performance gap on ScienceQA-IMG, and retains most of its gain under 30% noisy demonstrations, at a 26% latency overhead that an offline cache amortizes. No gradients are ever computed; the LVLM stays frozen.

GraftOOD: Grafting Out-of-Distribution Exemplars into Advantage-Weighted Rectification for Video Reward Optimization

GraftOOD: Grafting Out-of-Distribution Exemplars into Advantage-Weighted Rectification for Video Reward Optimization

Zheming Fu、Ruizhe He、Wenbin Ning、Xiaoxiao Ma、Xuan Yang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Video reward optimization can increase proxy rewards while reducing video dynamics, a mismatch we identify as \emph{motion collapse}, including under a video-level motion-quality reward. We propose \textbf{GraftOOD}, which replaces part of the online rollouts in selected prompt groups with out-of-distribution video exemplars. A shared velocity-regression objective combines group-relative reward rectification with sequence-level exemplar supervision, requiring neither exemplar generation trajectories nor an additional supervised-loss coefficient. We characterize its pointwise optimal field as a source-posterior-weighted combination of the two source-specific target fields. We construct \textbf{GraftSet-Video}, containing 9,588 format-matched exemplars for 2,397 prompts, to supply external supervision. On Wan2.1-1.3B, GraftOOD raises Dynamic Degree from 4.86 to 44.41 relative to reward-only FlowAWR, while HPSv3 decreases from 8.10 to 7.32 and VisionReward remains close. At the main setting, GraftOOD leads nine of ten metrics among three exemplar-augmented methods. Ablations of exemplar frequency and within-group count reveal dynamics--reward trade-offs rather than uniform gains from increasing exemplar usage. These results support external exemplar supervision as a complement to scalar reward feedback, without equating higher measured dynamics with coherent, visually plausible motion in the generated videos. Code is available at \href{https://github.com/Fuzm7/FlowAWR}{GraftOOD}.

目标检测/分割3D视觉医学图像
2026-10-09

SADL: A Size-Adaptive Decision Layer for Chronic Stroke Lesion Segmentation in ISLES 2026

SADL: A Size-Adaptive Decision Layer for Chronic Stroke Lesion Segmentation in ISLES 2026

Marek Wodzinski、Gniewosz Drwiega、Wojciech Szymanski

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Chronic ischemic stroke lesions are difficult to segment on T1-weighted MRI. Their appearance overlaps with healthy tissue, and their volumes span four orders of magnitude, so voxel-level and lesion level metrics often disagree. We build an eleven-member nnU-Net ensemble that covers five design axes: a baseline dice + top-K loss, heavy augmentation, per-lesion volume loss re-weighting, stochastic weight averaging, and a sampling-and-architecture block that pairs cohort-balanced sampling with a residual-encoder backbone. Weighted soft-map fusion of the members feeds a tuned decision layer, SADL, that estimates lesion volume, applies an adaptive per-lesion-size threshold, prunes components by voxel count and peak probability, and adds a never-empty rescue. All fusion weights and per-bucket parameters are fitted on out-of-fold (OOF) predictions, so tuning never touches the held-out test set. On a site-disjoint 91-case holdout, SADL reaches Dice 0.7322, lesion-F1 0.7461 and PR-AUC 0.8430 when scored online with the challenge's released evaluation code. A paired non-parametric bootstrap against a reference single ensemble member confirms the lesion-F1 gain at p=0.024, and the gain is largest in the small-lesion regime, where the rescue step recovers cases that a fixed threshold would silently discard.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散视频理解

TextVBench: Evaluating Text Rendering and Preservation in Video Generation

TextVBench: Evaluating Text Rendering and Preservation in Video Generation

Ruiqiang Zhang、Hengyi Wang、Chang Liu、Guanjie Wang、Zehua Ma 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Modern video generators can synthesize convincing scenes while rendering a few characters incorrectly, omitting requested text, or changing it across frames. Measuring these failures is harder than applying OCR independently to each frame: when a scene contains multiple text targets and multiple noisy OCR regions, the evaluator must first determine which observation belongs to which intended text. We introduce TextVBench, a benchmark and evaluation framework that formulates visual-text evaluation as a multi-target, multi-instance spatiotemporal correspondence problem. TextVBench contains two complementary tracks: TextVBench-T2V measures the creation and maintenance of prompt-specified text, while TextVBench-Ref2V measures preservation of text from reference images. Both tracks use target-conditioned candidate selection rather than unconstrained VLM transcription, support one target mapping to multiple OCR regions, and separate frame-level spatial fidelity from cross-frame temporal fidelity. A length-weighted harmonic score summarizes these factors, while extra-text diagnostics expose failure modes hidden by a single OCR score. The benchmark is organized around controlled text, layout, carrier, and motion attributes so that aggregate scores can be traced back to specific capability limits. Across the evaluated model runs, MiniMax-H3 reaches a T2V TextVScore of 68.32, while the strongest Ref2V score is 56.51; attribute slices reveal a pronounced drop as the number of simultaneous reference text regions increases.

2026-10-09

Radiomics-Based Thyroid Nodule Classification with Label-Conditional Conformal Prediction

Radiomics-Based Thyroid Nodule Classification with Label-Conditional Conformal Prediction

Laura Testa、Pierpaolo Brutti、Umberto Ferraro Petrillo

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Thyroid nodule malignancy assessment relies on ultrasound imaging, yet current risk stratification systems suffer from substantial interobserver variability, contributing to a high rate of unnecessary biopsies. We propose a radiomics-based framework combining quantitative feature extraction via PyRadiomics with a Generalized Additive Model (GAM) classifier, selected among six candidates for its favorable trade-off between discriminative performance and interpretability. To complement point predictions with uncertainty estimates, Cross-Conformal Prediction with label-conditional guarantees is applied, flagging ambiguous cases for clinical review rather than forcing a binary decision. Experiments are conducted on TN5000, a publicly available ultrasound dataset of thyroid nodules. After Spearman correlation filtering, 36 features are retained and the GAM achieves an AUC of $0.89$, a sensitivity of $0.83$, and a specificity of $0.82$ on the validation set. At a significance level $ε=0.09$, selected to ensure empirical coverage guarantees on both classes, 63\% of nodules on the test set receive a certain (i.e., single-label) prediction, with conditional precision of $0.94$ for certain malignant and conditional Negative Predictive Value (NPV) of $0.73$ for certain benign cases, while the remaining 37\% are referred to the clinician.

2026-10-09

ECGWmark: Deep Watermarking for Electrocardiogram Signals

ECGWmark: Deep Watermarking for Electrocardiogram Signals

Kuan-Yu Chen、Kuan-Chen Wang、Yu Tsao、Jian-Jiun Ding

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Secure transmission of electrocardiogram (ECG) signals remains a major challenge in telecardiology. While traditional mathematical watermarking can embed patient metadata directly into ECG signals, it is highly fragile against temporal desynchronization such as circular phase shifts. In contrast, existing deep watermarking approaches for temporal signals, such as audio, typically rely on lossy quantization, which compromises the high-frequency micro-morphological details critical for clinical diagnosis. To bridge this gap, we propose ECGWmark, a fully convolutional and quantization-free deep watermarking framework for medical time-series. ECGWmark operates in a continuous, full-resolution latent space and utilizes a localized frame-level mechanism to improve the robustness to severe desynchronization. Crucially, we introduce a differentiable Physiological Loss guided by a clinical classifier to preserve diagnostic semantics. Experiments on the benchmark MIT-BIH database show that ECGWmark maintains channel-bit extraction accuracy above 90% under the evaluated circular temporal shifts. Furthermore, under a rigorous inter-patient diagnostic evaluation, Class-V sensitivity changes from 71.97% to 76.50%, whereas Macro-F1 changes from 35.25% to 34.71% and Class-S sensitivity from 8.89% to 1.39%; these results indicate mixed class-level effects.

2026-10-08

Zebra3D: Efficient Hybrid Foundation Models For Scalable 3D Reconstruction

Zebra3D: Efficient Hybrid Foundation Models For Scalable 3D Reconstruction

Utkarsh Saxena、Ruijing Yang、Mehdi Rezagholizadeh、Akash Haridas、Vikram Appia 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Feed-forward 3D foundation models reconstruct scenes from unposed images, but the quadratic cost of global attention limits their scalability to large view sets. We present Zebra3D, a layerwise sparse-linear hybrid attention framework that improves the efficiency of pretrained 3D foundation models without training from scratch. Across VGGT, HunyuanWorld-Mirror, and $π^3$, we observe a broadly consistent depth-wise pattern: early stages are relatively tolerant to linear replacement, while sensitive middle-to-late stages tend to exhibit more concentrated attention. Zebra3D exploits this structure by assigning linear-centered attention to tolerant stages and tile-sparse attention to sensitive stages, using landmark summaries to preserve unselected context. Closed-form affine fitting enables training-free conversion, while optional post-conversion training combines task supervision with output and relational feature distillation. Across all three backbones, Zebra3D achieves strong reconstruction and camera-pose accuracy at high attention sparsity, with training-free variants remaining close to their dense counterparts. End-to-end speedups increase with input view count, reaching up to $7.1\times$ at 1,024 views. After post-conversion training, Zebra3D-VGGT reaches 80.5\% RealEstate10K AUC@30 at 94\% mean attention sparsity, within 1.1 percentage points of dense VGGT fine-tuned on the same data and schedule.

2026-10-08

Feature Competition and Its Representational Origins in Vision Language Models: Evidence from the Stroop Paradigm

Feature Competition and Its Representational Origins in Vision Language Models: Evidence from the Stroop Paradigm

Nurbüke Teker、Rui Xiao、Zeynep Akata、Shuchen Wu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

When you ask a vision-language model (VLM) a question with an image, it answers your question based on the image. But to act means to choose: saying one thing means choosing to respond not say another. This becomes an issue when the image contains multiple features that equally relate to the question at hand: how would VLMs choose to respond? This problem is precisely studied by the classical Stroop task in psychology: among a set of deliberately designed images whose text and color conflicts (e.g., \textcolor{blue}{RED}), participants are instructed to report the color. We adapt this psychological paradigm to a benchmark and prompted a range of contrastive and generative VLMs on their behavior. Across models, we observe a persistent bias toward the feature of text in Stroop images: when text and color disagree, models preferentially generate responses centered to the text rather than color. We then opened the black box and examined the source of this bias and why certain features influences the model's behavior more than others. In the embedding space, we found that textual cues are represented more strongly and separably than color cues. This representational asymmetry predicts intervention success: steering models toward text-related directions is substantially easier than steering them toward color-related directions. Using a classical psychological paradigm, we demonstrate, at both behavioral and representational levels, that distinct features within the same image can exert unequal influence on VLM behavior. Our findings suggest that VLMs assign different degrees of saliency to different features within the same image, highlighting the need to understand how cue-specific saliency shapes their performance in widely used downstream applications.

ForcingBooster: Adaptive Patch-wise Denoising for Fast Autoregressive Video Diffusion

ForcingBooster: Adaptive Patch-wise Denoising for Fast Autoregressive Video Diffusion

Zekai Gu、Peng Wang、Jiahao Lu、Yongsen Mao、Cheng Lin 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Autoregressive video diffusion models have become a leading approach to real-time interactive video generation and video world modeling. However, existing methods typically allocate computation uniformly across space when generating the next video chunk, updating all patches with the same denoising schedule and number of function evaluations. This ignores the spatial heterogeneity of video content: static, simple, or recurring regions are often easier to denoise, while other regions require further refinement or additional context. A uniform budget thus leads to redundant computation on easy patches, whereas reducing the fixed step count can leave difficult patches insufficiently refined. We introduce \methodname{}, a general framework that dynamically allocates denoising steps to individual patches: easy patches stop early to improve efficiency, while difficult patches receive more refinement steps. This design accelerates generation with negligible quality degradation. Our method is compatible with most existing few-step autoregressive diffusion models, requiring only a lightweight routing module with modest training. Experiments demonstrate that \methodname{} reduces the total number of denoising steps applied across video patches by about 50\% while maintaining visual quality comparable to the four-step baseline.

BLEND: Balancing Personalization vs. Generalization in Federated Vision-Language Models

BLEND: Balancing Personalization vs. Generalization in Federated Vision-Language Models

Payam Abdisarabshali、Fardis Nadimi、Kasra Borazjani、Naji Khosravan、Richeng Jin 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Federated vision-language models (FedVLMs) represent one of the emerging frontiers of machine learning, aiming to integrate federated learning within the fine-tuning pipelines of vision-language models (VLMs). In this work, we introduce BLEND, a new framework designed to jointly enhance the personalization and generalization capabilities of FedVLMs. BLEND proposes a selective VLM parameter fine-tuning and aggregation strategy that consists of (i) global vision and text adapters shared across clients, and (ii) a personalized vision projection adapter tailored to each client. BLEND further introduces a novel loss function that blends the outputs of the global and personalized adapters to achieve personalization, while simultaneously promoting generalization through a principled anchor design implemented via the projection heads of the zero-shot VLM. Extensive experiments demonstrate that BLEND consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, highlighting its ability to effectively balance personalization and generalization across diverse system settings. (Code is available at: https://github.com/payamsiabd/BLEND.git)

JMT-YOLO: Multi-task Domain Adaptation via Segmentation-Guided Consistency Learning

JMT-YOLO: Multi-task Domain Adaptation via Segmentation-Guided Consistency Learning

Zhenhua Yu、Jiahao Zhang、Wenchao Zhang、Ou Ye、Hengxiang Zhao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Domain adaptive object detection (DAOD) aims to reduce performance degradation due to distribution shifts between training (source) and testing (target) datasets. However, owing to insufficient modeling of fine-grained features and limited instance-level alignment, existing approaches become less effective under complex environmental conditions, such as extreme weather conditions and object occlusion. We overcome these challenges by proposing JMT-YOLO, a novel joint multi-task learning framework for DAOD. JMT-YOLO is based on the Mean Teacher architecture. It introduces an auxiliary semantic segmentation task and a masking mechanism to enhance the modeling of foreground-background semantic boundaries in the target domain. Specifically, fine-grained feature degradation is reduced by introducing a multiscale context-aware segmentation head equipped with a dense connection structure to effectively capture local and global contextual features. A mask-consistency loss is formulated by applying random masking operations to target images, thereby guiding the network to learn structural context and improving instance-level feature alignment. Extensive experiments on three benchmark domain adaptation tasks (Pascal VOC to Clipart1k, Cityscapes to Foggy Cityscapes, and KITTI to Cityscapes) demonstrate that JMT-YOLO achieves detection performances of 46.9%, 57.3%, and 61.0%, respectively. These results demonstrate superior performance over existing state-of-the-art and baseline methods, thereby validating the effectiveness and generalization of JMT-YOLO.

AD-SRF: Attack-Defense Semantic Reinforcement for Multi-Modal Image Fusion

AD-SRF: Attack-Defense Semantic Reinforcement for Multi-Modal Image Fusion

Hao Zhang、Zhiqian Zheng、Linfeng Tang、Xinyu Xiang、Jiayi Ma 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Existing image fusion methods struggle to balance visual quality and semantic attributes due to the inherent conflict between their optimization objectives. To address this challenge, we propose an attack-defense semantic reinforcement framework for multi-modal image fusion, termed AD-SRF. By decoupling the optimization of these two aspects and then losslessly reintegrating them, this framework injects rich semantic attributes into the fused image without compromising its visual quality. First, we develop a transformer-based restorative visual fusion network as a strong visual baseline. Equipped with the cross-modal retrieval module and the information restoration-fusion collaborative loss, it produces highly visible fused images even under degraded conditions. Second, we introduce an attack-defense game mechanism to strengthen the semantic attributes of the fused images. The attacker learns perturbation patterns that impair downstream segmentation, whereas the defender restores missing semantics from the original inputs and reintegrates them into the fused image through an imperceptible semantic map. Through iterative adversarial interaction, this mechanism drives the defender to continually strengthen the fused image's semantic attributes, boosting downstream segmentation performance without compromising visual quality. Extensive experiments demonstrate that AD-SRF achieves a win-win performance in both visual quality and semantic attributes. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/HaoZhang1018/AD-SRF.

ROSETTA: Rotation Subspace Estimation for Training-free Transmodal Alignment

ROSETTA: Rotation Subspace Estimation for Training-free Transmodal Alignment

Alex Junho Lee

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

When a query must be matched against a database built with a different sensor or processed by a different encoder, the independently trained encoders impose distinct coordinate systems on the feature space. No canonical reference frame exists to which all encoders can be aligned, and no single training procedure has fully closed this gap. We show that the relative transformation between any two such systems is a spatially preserving rotation in whitened feature space, whose angular velocity exhibits white-noise characteristics: large in norm, temporally uncorrelated, and unpredictable from the features themselves. However, the cross-covariance between encoder outputs has low effective rank, constraining only a small fraction of the rotation parameters, therefore local alignment is tractable. ROSETTA is a training-free calibration layer that estimates the local rotation via an expectation-maximization procedure, requiring no coobservation at query time. We validate ROSETTA on three public driving datasets spanning LiDAR, camera, thermal, and event modalities in cross-modal, cross-device, cross-representation, and cross-backbone settings, with milliseconds overhead per query.

Selective Spatio-Temporal Fruit Inspector (S$^2$FI): A Mamba-based Framework for Rotational Fruit Quality Assessment

Selective Spatio-Temporal Fruit Inspector (S$^2$FI): A Mamba-based Framework for Rotational Fruit Quality Assessment

Hao Tian、Jie Hu、Guoqi Shan、Fan Wang、Wenzhuo Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The global fruit and vegetable industry confronts severe post-harvest sorting challenges for small and medium-scale growers. Large intelligent sorting systems are too costly, while manual sorting suffers from low efficiency, inconsistent standards, and high labor demand. To bridge this gap between expensive equipment and inefficient manual work, this study develops a low-cost, high-efficiency, lightweight fruit sorting system driven by deep learning, suitable for small and medium growers. We propose the Selective Spatio-Temporal Fruit Inspector (S$^2$FI) architecture, which combines a 3D convolutional encoder with a Mamba-based backbone.It efficiently extracts spatiotemporal features, adaptively weighs informative frames during fruit rotation, and models long-range dependencies with linear complexity, reducing motion blur and occlusion. The hardware uses a dual-conveyor and curved rail design, supporting household power, fast deployment, and lightweight embedded deployment to improve affordability. We also built a conveyor-based fruit quality video dataset with 6,460 samples. Experiments on the held-out test set show that our method achieves the best trade-off among accuracy, efficiency, and practicality, outperforming the compared baselines on the test set, achieving an excellent balance of accuracy, speed, and practicality for real-world sorting.

CaptchaAgent: Verifiable Visual Reasoning for CAPTCHA Solving

CaptchaAgent: Verifiable Visual Reasoning for CAPTCHA Solving

Shangquan Sun、Ruoyu Chen、Zhirui Liu、Xiang Liu、Chenghao Qian 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Modern reasoning CAPTCHAs require fine-grained perception, compositional relation reasoning, and grounded browser interaction. End-to-end vision-language model agents remain brittle in this setting because a missed object, incorrect count, or confused relation can propagate directly to the final action. To address this problem, we present CaptchaAgent, a structured visual reasoning framework for interactive CAPTCHA solving. Instead of predicting an action directly from a screenshot, CaptchaAgent first constructs a visual intermediate representation (V-IR) that explicitly records entities, attributes, and spatial or topological relations. A symbol-only ASCII protocol further requires the parser to commit to a concrete object set and count. Building on this global representation, localized evidence refinement revisits ambiguous regions at higher effective resolution, while counterfactual consistency verification tests whether the resulting action remains stable under task-preserving transformations. Across three proprietary backbones on Open CaptchaWorld, CaptchaAgent improves Pass@1 by 21.3-25.8 percentage points over a commercially available browser agent baseline evaluated with the same backbones. On the separate offline Oedipus-data benchmark, it further improves average Pass@1 by 9.2-12.3 percentage points over the Oedipus solver across three matched backbones. The intermediate traces show that exact counting, continuous geometry, and ordered execution remain major sources of automation difficulty for the evaluated agents, providing practical guidance for designing more resilient compositional CAPTCHAs.

Structure-Aware Matching for Robust UAV Thermal-to-Satellite Geo-Localization

Structure-Aware Matching for Robust UAV Thermal-to-Satellite Geo-Localization

Chun Liu、Yuxin Wang、Zhenxiang Huo、Linlin Zhu、Tao Xu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

UAV thermal-to-satellite geo-localization localizes UAVs by matching real-time thermal images against georeferenced satellite images, enabling autonomous navigation in GNSS-denied and low-visibility conditions. However, substantial cross-modal disparities in color, brightness and viewpoint hinder conventional appearance-based methods from establishing reliable correspondences. While existing approaches predominantly address global modality shifts, they overlook the invariant scene structures inherently shared between thermal and satellite views. To bridge this gap, we propose SAMF, a Structure-Aware Matching Framework that explicitly exploits stable cross-modal structural cues to improve UAV thermal geolocalization. Specifically, the Directional Structure Enhancement Module (DSEM) strengthens robust structural representations by capturing directional and low-frequency spatial patterns, and the Modality-Aware Affine Remapping Module (MARM) subsequently applies affine correction to these enhanced features, further promoting cross-modal alignment. Additionally, we introduce the Thermal-UAV-Paired benchmark derived from the Thermal-UAV dataset. Extensive experiments on both the Boson-nighttime and Thermal-UAV-Paired datasets show that SAMF achieves state-of-the-art performance. Ablation studies further validate the effectiveness and synergistic complementarity of DSEM and MARM. The code and dataset are released at: https://github.com/YuxinWang879/SAMF.git.

2026-10-08

Domain Adaptation of SAM 2.1 for Building-Component Segmentation in Image-Based 3D Reconstruction

Domain Adaptation of SAM 2.1 for Building-Component Segmentation in Image-Based 3D Reconstruction

Ehsan Agha Ebrahimi、Yong-Cheol Lee、Keith G. Mills

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

3D reconstruction of building components using image-based scanning requires appropriate segmentation of the objects in the 2D building images. In order to benefit from consistent grouping of the 2D masks in 3D environments, vision foundation models such as the Segment Anything Model have been utilized by Group Anything with Radiance Fields (GARField) to perform 3D segmentation. This study fine-tuned SAM 2.1 on the HBD dataset, consisting of 2,679 annotated images from 42 buildings, to enhance multi-class segmentation of 2D building images, covering 11 classes (Wall, Window, Ceiling, Door, Column, Beam, Floor, Opening, Curtain Wall, Roof, and Lift). The model was evaluated using per-class IoU, Dice, and mIoU metrics, and the results show an improvement in mIoU from 49.9% to 69.6%, indicating a meaningful improvement in class segmentation and providing a more accurate model for distilling the masks in 3D. SegFormer was then used as a baseline, generating per-class masks for comparison with SAM 2.1, which indicates improvement in some object classes with larger surface area. The masks in the building images were grouped in the neural radiance field, and the pretrained SAM 2.1 was compared with the fine-tuned SAM 2.1 in the newly rendered images. This comparison shows that fine-tuning improves the grouping of building components at a user-selected grouping scale. This advances the application of Group Anything with Radiance Fields (GARField) in 3D, enabling better segmentation of building structures for 3D auto-reconstruction.

2026-10-09

Learning Features, Not Layouts: Feature-Axis Attention as a Transfer Prior for Medical Imaging

Learning Features, Not Layouts: Feature-Axis Attention as a Transfer Prior for Medical Imaging

Aon Safdar、Mohamed Saadeldin

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Natural-image-pretrained foundation models are increasingly adapted to medical imaging, yet transfer remains unreliable under domain shift, modality heterogeneity, and label scarcity. We trace part of this fragility to an overlooked design choice. Standard vision transformers place all global modeling on self-attention over spatial tokens, an object-layout prior well suited to natural scenes but not to medical images, where the discriminative signal lies in feature correlations and texture that recur across scanners while layout does not. We ask whether the axis of global attention, spatial versus feature, is itself a lever for transferable medical representations. Existing channel-aware hybrids confound this question, adding channel attention to a spatially-mixed backbone and leaving the axes coupled and the feature axis unmeasurable in isolation. We design FAD-ViT, a backbone that strictly decouples the two axes, making feature-axis attention the primary global operator and the attention axis a controlled variable. Evaluating comprehensively across 23 classification, segmentation, and low-data tasks under matched tokenization, capacity, and protocol, we find that shifting global attention from the spatial to the feature axis leaves from-scratch performance essentially unchanged, yet markedly strengthens transfer. Identical ImageNet pretraining yields up to roughly three times the transfer gain, with consistent improvements on every held-out segmentation task at up to a quarter the parameter count and far lower compute than standard and channel-attention baselines. The advantage is largest where texture rather than geometry is discriminative, tracking greater reuse of pretrained features. The axis of global attention is thus a design lever for medical transfer, and making it channel-primary is a simple, cheap, and principled choice.

Natural-image-pretrained foundation models are increasingly adapted to medical imaging, yet transfer remains unreliable under domain shift, modality heterogeneity, and label scarcity. We trace part of this fragility to an overlooked design choice. Standard vision transformers place all global modeling on self-attention over spatial tokens, an object-layout prior well suited to natural scenes but not to medical images, where the discriminative signal lies in feature correlations and texture that recur across scanners while layout does not. We ask whether the axis of global attention, spatial versus feature, is itself a lever for transferable medical representations. Existing channel-aware hybrids confound this question, adding channel attention to a spatially-mixed backbone and leaving the axes coupled and the feature axis unmeasurable in isolation. We design FAD-ViT, a backbone that strictly decouples the two axes, making feature-axis attention the primary global operator and the attention axis a controlled variable. Evaluating comprehensively across 23 classification, segmentation, and low-data tasks under matched tokenization, capacity, and protocol, we find that shifting global attention from the spatial to the feature axis leaves from-scratch performance essentially unchanged, yet markedly strengthens transfer. Identical ImageNet pretraining yields up to roughly three times the transfer gain, with consistent improvements on every held-out segmentation task at up to a quarter the parameter count and far lower compute than standard and channel-attention baselines. The advantage is largest where texture rather than geometry is discriminative, tracking greater reuse of pretrained features. The axis of global attention is thus a design lever for medical transfer, and making it channel-primary is a simple, cheap, and principled choice.

目标检测/分割
2026-10-09

CUBE: Class-conditioned Unknown Boundary Evidence for Open-Set Insect Species Recognition

CUBE: Class-conditioned Unknown Boundary Evidence for Open-Set Insect Species Recognition

Hao Tan、Qimin Zhang、Zhe Xiao、Zekai Zuo、Wanting Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Open-set insect species recognition faces significant challenges when deployed in real-world biodiversity scenarios, particularly when encountering unknown species that are visually close to known training classes. Unlike generic out-of-distribution samples, fine-grained unknown insects are often not visually anomalous. They instead tend to appear near the decision boundary of specific known species, leading to unreliable closed-set predictions. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{Class-conditioned Unknown Boundary Evidence (CUBE)}, a novel evidence-based framework that models unknownness around each known species rather than as a single global unknown class. Our key idea is to evaluate a test sample relative to the known species it most closely resembles, enabling local rejection for visually confusable unknown insects. CUBE learns class-conditioned boundary evidence from auxiliary species, preserves auxiliary species as shadow references during inference, and incorporates taxonomic candidate calibration to verify biological plausibility. When ecological metadata is available, we further introduce CUBE-E, which adds a lightweight location-consistency term without modifying the visual backbone. Experiments on the Open-Insect C-America benchmark show that CUBE achieves superior performance, outperforming state-of-the-art baselines with 95.8\%, 98.7\%, and 99.1\% AUROC on local, non-local, and non-moth unknown splits, respectively, while maintaining 94.9\% closed-set accuracy. With ecological metadata, CUBE-E further improves the results to 96.0\%, 99.7\%, and 99.5\% AUROC with 95.2\% closed-set accuracy.

With ecological metadata, CUBE-E further improves the results to 96.0\%, 99.7\%, and 99.5\% AUROC with 95.2\% closed-set accuracy.

Thyroid Nodule Segmentation in Ultrasound Images with Conformal Uncertainty Estimation

Thyroid Nodule Segmentation in Ultrasound Images with Conformal Uncertainty Estimation

Francesco Teodori、Pierpaolo Brutti、Umberto Ferraro Petrillo

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Thyroid nodules affect a large proportion of the general population. Although most are benign, a non-negligible fraction is malignant. Ultrasound imaging is the primary diagnostic modality, and accurate nodule segmentation is a prerequisite for CAD systems to classify malignancy and assess metastatic risk.\\ In this work, we propose a thyroid nodule segmentation framework that integrates a DeepLabV3+, channel-attention modules and post-hoc conformal prediction, providing both accurate mask and pixel-level uncertainty estimates. We address thyroid ultrasound nodule segmentation as the first stage of a pipeline that also includes malignancy classification and metastatic risk prediction. We adopt DeepLabV3+, whose Atrous Spatial Pyramid Pooling (ASPP) captures multi-scale context, with an EfficientNet-B5 backbone for its accuracy-efficiency trade-off. Additionally, we test three channel-attention modules: Squeeze-and-Excitation, CBAM and Grouped Channel Attention (GCA), paired with two loss formulations (Dice-BCE and Boundary).\\ Since binary masks do not indicate prediction confidence, we wrap the trained model with a split conformal prediction layer that produces pixel-level uncertainty maps with statistical coverage guarantees.\\ We evaluate all configurations on the TN3K public benchmark using a fixed split and directly compare them with state-of-the-art methods. The plain backbone already improves upon previously reported results, with CBAM + Dice-BCE emerging as the best overall configuration.

The plain backbone already improves upon previously reported results, with CBAM + Dice-BCE emerging as the best overall configuration.

What We Talk About When We Talk About Emotion Editing

What We Talk About When We Talk About Emotion Editing

Xingzu Zhan、Lin Gu、Ruogu Fang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Emotional image editing aims to change the emotion a scene conveys while preserving its essential content. Because the same scene can support different emotional interpretations, achieving an emotional goal requires visual changes grounded in the source context. Motivated by the Component Process Model, which links emotional responses to event appraisal, we investigate neural representations as a reference for these editing decisions. Using TRIBE v2, an fMRI encoding model, we analyze 400 source-target pairs across eight emotion categories. Hidden states from EmoEdit, EmoEditor, and AffectDelta correspond more strongly to predicted target-image responses in visual regions than in affect-related regions. This imbalance motivates ROI-ReFT, which combines source visual responses as a content reference with a predicted affective response change as an editing direction. Two interacting branches use both conditions to guide a frozen editor throughout generation. Predicted responses of the generated image supervise affective change and constrain visual drift. On a post hoc selected subset of 400 editing requests, ROI-ReFT achieves 91.75% target-emotion accuracy and 83.25% joint success, with the same output both matching the target emotion and reaching source-output DINO-I of at least 0.80. Our code and dataset will be publicly released.

ELMA: Benchmarking Anaphoric Compositions for Long-Term Text-to-Motion Generation

ELMA: Benchmarking Anaphoric Compositions for Long-Term Text-to-Motion Generation

Shun Kato、Dixin Yang、Kotaro Amaya、Yuiga Wada、Kazumi Fukuda 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Text-guided long-term human motion generation is essential for applications such as continuously operating digital avatars. Despite recent progress on short clips, existing models struggle with multi-segment instructions that involve anaphora, where constraints established in earlier segments must be preserved over time. In this paper, we introduce a novel task of long-term motion generation from such anaphoric compositions. To mitigate this, we present ELMA, an episodic long-term motion dataset with dense segment-level annotations that preserve cross-segment dependencies. Built via a fully automated collection and filtering pipeline, ELMA extracts high-quality, continuous 30-second clips from in-the-wild videos, capturing the episodic narratives necessary for long-range state modeling. Leveraging this dataset, we fine-tune state-of-the-art motion generation models to incorporate long-range context. Extensive experiments reveal fundamental shortcomings in current models when handling context-dependent instructions. We demonstrate that ELMA enables significant improvements in both global physical realism and semantic alignment, setting a robust new baseline for long-term motion synthesis.

LapMerge: Differentiable Exact-Budget Token Merging in Linear Time

LapMerge: Differentiable Exact-Budget Token Merging in Linear Time

Magdalena Trędowicz、Aleksandra Nowak、Tomasz Steifer、Łukasz Struski、Szymon Płotka 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Modern vision and language models process ever longer token sequences, and reducing them is the main lever on downstream cost. Token compression, however, faces a fundamental trade-off: selecting tokens discards information, while existing merging methods preserve it only through pairwise token-to-token matching or $MN$ token-to-output interactions. We introduce LapMerge, a differentiable merging operator that resolves this trade-off for ordered sequences: a learned mass distribution partitions the sequence into exactly $N$ equal-mass segments under a Laplace-kernel density, and prefix/suffix scans aggregate each segment, so both the forward and the backward pass run in $\mathcal{O}(M+N)$ time. This combination - an exact output budget, end-to-end differentiability, and linear cost - is precisely what previous reducers trade off against one another. The budget can be changed without changing operator parameters, and the learned mass doubles as a token-level map of where the budget is allocated. Across vision and vision-language tasks, LapMerge matches or improves on strong token reducers while being up to two orders of magnitude faster at large sequence lengths; it is particularly effective when token order reflects spatial or temporal locality, and works both as a drop-in reduction module and as a trainable architectural component.

Event-based Action Recognition by Pre-Training on Multi-Frame Representations

Event-based Action Recognition by Pre-Training on Multi-Frame Representations

Emma Norrby、Tomas Wilkinson、Per Nyblom、David Gustafsson

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Action recognition is a crucial task in domains like robotics, patient monitoring, or surveillance. Recently, event-based action recognition has attracted much attention due to the event camera's ability to capture fast motions and its improved privacy over conventional vision cameras. While frame-based event representations work well for short event sequences, their discriminative ability declines sharply with longer and more complex event sequences. In this work, we introduce a multi-frame representation that greatly improves recognition performance over its single-frame counterpart. We adopt a masked auto-encoding framework to pre-train a Vision Transformer using a sparsity-aware masking algorithm and token selection mechanism to improve downstream performance and control computational complexity. We also introduce multiple mask tokens for the decoder with a position-aware attention mechanism, leading to further improved performance. Our proposed method shows impressive performance for challenging long-duration and complex event sequences, yielding state-of-the-art performance on THU-EACT-50 CHL and DailyDVS-200.

2026-10-09

Spanning Diffusion and Flow-based Posterior Sampling via Exact Gaussian Conditioning

Spanning Diffusion and Flow-based Posterior Sampling via Exact Gaussian Conditioning

Teo Deveney、Lea Bogensperger、Hok Shing Wong

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Flow-matching and diffusion models are increasingly used as priors for linear inverse problems, however, most methods are bespoke to one class of trajectory prior. Plug-and-play methods have been proposed, however these implicitly decorrelate the guided trajectory, resulting in broad priors that can struggle when data is sparse. This work proposes $β$-Flow: a general plug-and-play method indexed by a correlation parameter $β$ that generalises over flow, diffusion, and decorrelated priors, and a confidence parameter $κ$ that controls how strongly the path distribution is enforced. Each step requires only one forward evaluation of a denoising network, followed by a Gaussian conditioning step. This step is an exact Kalman update that is not dependent on network gradients. We show that many existing guided samplers can be interpreted as instances or approximations of our method, each implicitly using a different trajectory prior and operator in place of the Kalman gain. From this viewpoint, we provide a qualitative comparison of methods in terms of their implicit biases and assumptions. We demonstrate consistently state-of-the-art level performance across 7 inverse problems over 4 datasets in terms of PSNR and SSIM. Moreover, we demonstrate the utility of this prior control, by showing that confident high correlations (strict priors) work best when observations are less informative, while low correlation is preferred when observations are more informative, reflecting the complementary roles of the prior and likelihood in Bayesian inference.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM3D视觉
2026-10-09

SimVTA: Simulation-Grounded Video-Trajectory Alignment for Driving World Action Models

SimVTA: Simulation-Grounded Video-Trajectory Alignment for Driving World Action Models

Changhui Jing、Haochen Tian、Hongchen Li、Tianyu Li、Caojun Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Driving world action models (WAMs) learn from paired trajectories and future observations. Post-training with reinforcement learning allows the policy to explore alternative trajectories and improve planning using driving rewards. However, driving logs contain future videos only for the recorded trajectories, leaving these alternatives without corresponding visual supervision. Thus, we introduce SimVTA, a simulation-grounded post-training framework that combines trajectory optimization with video supervision. First, we sample candidate trajectories for each observation and use a real-world simulator (e.g., World Engine) to render future videos for high-reward candidates in real-world scenes reconstructed with 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS). This provides trajectory-matched supervision independent of the model's own predictions. Under a limited rendering budget, we prioritize video supervision for higher-error futures among these rendered candidates. Finally, we optimize trajectory planning with Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) and use a flow-matching loss on the matched videos to update shared representations. At inference time, the model predicts trajectories directly, without simulator calls. On NAVSIM v1, applying SimVTA to a WAM initialization improves PDMS from 86.2 to 91.3 (+5.1). It achieves 88.2 EPDMS on NAVSIM v2 and an average HD-Score of 38.8 in closed-loop evaluation on HUGSIM. Ablations show that updating shared representations with video supervision improves planning, while prioritizing higher-error futures among high-reward trajectories further improves performance.

X-AVDD: Cross-Attentive Audio-Visual Dataset Distillation

X-AVDD: Cross-Attentive Audio-Visual Dataset Distillation

Saumyaranjan Mohanty、Aravind Reddy、Konda Reddy Mopuri

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Audio-visual dataset distillation aims to synthesize a small dataset from a large paired audio-visual dataset, while nearly preserving the training utility for neural networks. Existing approaches such as DM and AVDD face two critical limitations: 1) they are memory-intensive, making them difficult to scale to complex architectures and higher samples-per-class (SPC) settings, and 2) they often fail to preserve strong cross-modal semantic correspondence. To tackle these issues, we propose X-AVDD, a cross-attention-based distillation method that explicitly captures inter-modal relationships during synthesis. By decoupling cross-modal alignment from joint optimization, X-AVDD is substantially more memory-efficient, reducing end-to-end synthesis cost by $\sim 8\times$ GPU-hours, while producing highly aligned synthetic data. Empirically, X-AVDD significantly outperforms other audio-visual distillation methods. On VGGSound, an audio-visual dataset of short YouTube clips, X-AVDD improves top-1 test accuracy from $8.2\%\rightarrow24.0\%$ at SPC=$10$ and $9.8\%\rightarrow26.5\%$ at SPC=$20$. Furthermore, X-AVDD improves cross-architecture generalization (e.g., $25.5\%\rightarrow35.3\%$ for a ViT target on AVE dataset at SPC $=50$) and successfully scales to complex architectures such as AudioCLIP, achieving $62.3\%$ accuracy using only $5.65\%$ of the full dataset, against the full dataset accuracy of $72.4\%$ while completing in $9\times$ lower training time compared to the full dataset.

目标检测/分割3D视觉
2026-10-09

Efficient Confidence-Aware Densification for 3DGS

Efficient Confidence-Aware Densification for 3DGS

Manuel Hofer、Thomas Köhler、Felix Windisch、Bernhard Kerbl、Markus Steinberger 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

3DGS-MCMC has replaced the gradient-driven density control of 3D Gaussian Splatting with opacity-based sampling and relocation, and is now the de facto standard for prior-free densification under a fixed primitive budget. Because opacity stays high where the scene is already well reconstructed, this sampling sends most new primitives to the foreground and leaves the background under-reconstructed. At low budgets, prior-based methods such as ConeGS and EDGS outperform it, at the cost of a pre-trained radiance field or dense image matches and a separate initialization stage. We argue for a comeback of error-driven densification within the fixed-budget MCMC framework. Our confidence-aware densification keeps the fixed budget and gradual growth of 3DGS-MCMC, but places primitives by the image error instead of the opacity. New primitives are drawn mostly where screen-space gradients are large. We favor primitives with a low learned confidence, which marks regions the reconstruction still explains poorly, and relocate the primitives that contribute least to the rendered images to these places. Averaged over 24 scenes, our method outperforms 3DGS-MCMC in PSNR, SSIM and LPIPS at every budget from 100k to 2M primitives. At 100k, without a prior or an initialization stage, it recovers 0.84dB of the 1.05dB PSNR by which ConeGS leads 3DGS-MCMC, and from 500k on, it has the highest PSNR and SSIM of all fixed-budget methods we compare with, including ConeGS and EDGS. While inherently prior-free, our densification can also exploit priors: from the ConeGS or EDGS initialization, it matches ConeGS in PSNR and SSIM at 100k and is best in all three metrics at 500k.

Detection of extremely rare dead cells in fluorescence microscopy images

Detection of extremely rare dead cells in fluorescence microscopy images

Prateek Verma、Benjamin Butter、Jian Zhang、Xintao Wu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Dead cell detection in fluorescence microscopy is essential for phenotypic screening and drug efficacy assessment. However, automated identification remains challenging due to signal variability, optical artifacts, and colocalization ambiguities across multiple channels. Markers that define nuclear boundaries do not neatly coincide spatially or temporally with apoptotic markers. Furthermore, marker accumulation kinetics vary across individual cells and apoptosis stages, making universal thresholds unreliable. We present a systematic comparison of several computational approaches for detecting apoptotic cells in nucleus-stained specimens: (1) heuristic methods using intensity thresholds, distributions, correlations, and domain guidance; (2) classification using numerical features (Logistic Regression, Random Forest, XGBoost and LightGBM) and image features (ResNet-18 and EfficientNet-B0); and (3) anomaly detection, also using numerical features (Isolation Forest, one-class SVM and PCA reconstruction) and image features (convolutional autoencoders and Deep SVDD). Using a dataset of 258 images containing 65,527 cells/nuclei across multiple scenes with 243 manually annotated ground truth dead cells, we evaluate each method on metrics such as PR-AUC, Precision, Recall, F1-score, and macro-F1. Our results indicate that classification using image features (deep learning) and numerical features (traditional) achieved the best performance, with ResNet-18 and XGBoost significantly outperforming manually designed heuristics and anomaly detection methods. Our findings demonstrate that such biomedical problems remain highly challenging to solve even with the state-of-the-art computational and learning techniques. We aim to provide an effective decision-support tool to researchers while tackling such problems either fully-automatically or in a human-in-the-loop setting as an aide to domain scientists.

Dead cell detection in fluorescence microscopy is essential for phenotypic screening and drug efficacy assessment. However, automated identification remains challenging due to signal variability, optical artifacts, and colocalization ambiguities across multiple channels. Markers that define nuclear boundaries do not neatly coincide spatially or temporally with apoptotic markers. Furthermore, marker accumulation kinetics vary across individual cells and apoptosis stages, making universal thresholds unreliable. We present a systematic comparison of several computational approaches for detecting apoptotic cells in nucleus-stained specimens: (1) heuristic methods using intensity thresholds, distributions, correlations, and domain guidance; (2) classification using numerical features (Logistic Regression, Random Forest, XGBoost and LightGBM) and image features (ResNet-18 and EfficientNet-B0); and (3) anomaly detection, also using numerical features (Isolation Forest, one-class SVM and PCA reconstruction) and image features (convolutional autoencoders and Deep SVDD). Using a dataset of 258 images containing 65,527 cells/nuclei across multiple scenes with 243 manually annotated ground truth dead cells, we evaluate each method on metrics such as PR-AUC, Precision, Recall, F1-score, and macro-F1. Our results indicate that classification using image features (deep learning) and numerical features (traditional) achieved the best performance, with ResNet-18 and XGBoost significantly outperforming manually designed heuristics and anomaly detection methods. Our findings demonstrate that such biomedical problems remain highly challenging to solve even with the state-of-the-art computational and learning techniques. We aim to provide an effective decision-support tool to researchers while tackling such problems either fully-automatically or in a human-in-the-loop setting as an aide to domain scientists.

READ: Reading the Selective Scan for Faithful Video Attribution

READ: Reading the Selective Scan for Faithful Video Attribution

Magdalena Trędowicz、Piotr Kubaty、Arkadiusz Lewicki、Jacek Tabor、Łukasz Struski 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Selective state-space models are an efficient approach to long-sequence video modeling. However, their recurrent computation makes it difficult to determine which video tokens drive a prediction. Existing attribution methods often require additional backward passes or repeated model evaluations, making them substantially more expensive than the forward inference they aim to explain. In this work, we introduce READ, a parameter-free attribution method for selective state-space models that unrolls the recurrence at the position read by the classifier. READ traces the contribution accumulated in the recurrent state back to the video tokens that contribute to the final prediction. By anchoring attribution at the classifier's readout, it produces a single spatiotemporal relevance map from which frame- and region-level relevance can be obtained directly. Despite requiring only one forward pass, READ achieves competitive faithfulness and localisation compared with substantially more expensive attribution methods. Experimental results show that READ avoids the systematic relevance biases of existing methods while recovering meaningful temporal and class-specific structure across datasets and backbones. More broadly, our results show that the recurrence underlying selective state-space models is not only an efficient mechanism for inference, but also a direct source of interpretable evidence for their predictions.

Shortcut Learning in Geospatial Foundation Model Embeddings for Cross-Disaster Damage Assessment

Shortcut Learning in Geospatial Foundation Model Embeddings for Cross-Disaster Damage Assessment

Ali Shibli、Andrea Nascetti、Yifang Ban

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Geospatial foundation models can support disaster damage assessment, but high accuracy on familiar disasters may hide shortcut learning: prediction from event or scene characteristics rather than evidence that transfers to a new disaster. We study embeddings from TerraMind, Prithvi-EO-2.0, DOFA, and DINOv3 on the multi-disaster datasets xView2 and BRIGHT. Holding out an entire disaster, instead of randomly splitting image tiles, reduces mean balanced accuracy from 0.807 to 0.638 on xView2 and from 0.818 to 0.568 on BRIGHT. Four sets of tests examine what information supports prediction: event and acquisition context, before/after image inputs, building count, and correct image pairing. Pre-event images remain predictive, and a classifier given only the annotated number of buildings reaches balanced accuracy of 0.629 and 0.653. Combining before/after features performs best on average on xView2, but does not improve over pre-only features on BRIGHT. Shuffling image pairs within the same disaster and damage class causes modest losses. Motivated by these findings, we test whether feature transformations learned only from training disasters improve generalization to unseen disasters. These transformations improve performance, with supervised contrastive learning performing best on xView2 and PCA on BRIGHT. Our results highlight the importance of evaluation on unseen domains for assessing model robustness and show that simple feature-level transformations can improve generalization. Code and experiments can be found at https://github.com/alishibli97/gfm-shortcut-learning.

State-Isolated Vision Mamba via Update-Tendency Routing for Underwater Image Enhancement

State-Isolated Vision Mamba via Update-Tendency Routing for Underwater Image Enhancement

Yan Luo、Laibin Chang、Zefan Feng、Yi'ang Chen、Kui Jiang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision Mamba has recently emerged as an efficient alternative to convolutional and Transformer-based architectures by modeling long-range spatial dependencies through recurrent state evolution. Existing studies primarily improve token serialization, scanning strategies, or expert routing, yet largely overlook a state level question question: how should recurrent states be organized for spatially heterogeneous visual restoration? In this paper, we show that forcing tokens with heterogeneous update behaviors to recurrently modify a shared hidden state leads to persistent cross-content interference, which we term \textbf{State Contention}. We further argue that effective recurrent modeling should organize state evolution according to token compatibility rather than token ordering. Based on this observation, we introduce \textbf{State Isolation}, a new recurrent state organization principle for Vision Mamba,tokens with compatible state-update tendencies evolve within independent recurrent state streams. To instantiate this principle, we propose \textbf{IsoMamba}, a $Δ$-guided architecture that constructs model-intrinsic update representations, discovers update-compatible token groups through label-free clustering, and routes them to isolated Mamba experts following a \textbf{cluster--route--isolate} paradigm. In addition, we develop a dynamic channel-wise reweighting objective to compensate for wavelength-dependent reconstruction difficulty in underwater images. Extensive experiments demonstrate that IsoMamba consistently achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple underwater image enhancement benchmarks while providing cleaner hidden state responses, validating the effectiveness of state isolated recurrent modeling.

Extensive experiments demonstrate that IsoMamba consistently achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple underwater image enhancement benchmarks while providing cleaner hidden state responses, validating the effectiveness of state isolated recurrent modeling.

CORAL-KV: Compact Cross-Layer Prediction and Residual Coding for Autoregressive Video KV Caches

CORAL-KV: Compact Cross-Layer Prediction and Residual Coding for Autoregressive Video KV Caches

Albert Ge、Akash Haridas、Utkarsh Saxena、Ruijing Yang、Ian Colbert 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Autoregressive video diffusion models maintain a growing attention key-value (KV) cache, making memory a key bottleneck for long video generation. Recently, clustering-based quantization has emerged as an effective method to compress video KV caches by grouping similar key and value vectors then quantizing residual errors. However, when later frames attend to the compressed history, small reconstruction errors can compound as the video grows. Beyond the within-layer similarity exploited by clustering, we find strong dependencies across cache layers in video models. This motivates Coral-KV - Calibrated Offline, Residual-coded, and Adapted Locally - a KV-cache compression framework that uses cache states in each layer to predict keys and values in subsequent layers, then quantizes the remaining prediction residuals. We construct compact block-diagonal-plus-low-rank (block-DPLR) predictors: block-diagonal components model channel relationships within each attention head, while a global low-rank term models dependencies between heads. Coral-KV exposes a flexible fidelity-memory trade-off: it can reuse its predictors across videos or refit lightweight block-diagonal predictors to the current video for higher fidelity. Across extensive evaluations on both HY-WorldPlay and MAGI-1, our reusable configuration improves PSNR over QVG-4 by 0.32 dB and 4.95 dB, respectively, while using 48% and 37% less cache memory. Against QVG-Pro, our video-adapted configuration improves MAGI-1 PSNR by 3.77 dB while using 33.7% less cache memory. Coral-KV also maintains higher reconstruction fidelity over extended generation and reduces temporal discontinuities while retaining comparable generation latency.

2026-10-09

Can machines reason non-verbally?

Can machines reason non-verbally?

Shaheer U. Saeed、Zion Tse、Gregory Slabaugh、Daniel C. Alexander、Yipeng Hu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Modern machines can solve equations, follow logic and reason through language, yet they still struggle with problems that require visual non-verbal reasoning. This is evident even in advanced systems such as DeepSeek, ChatGPT and Gemini, which remain limited on visual tasks such as completing abstract patterns or delineating ambiguous regions-of-interest. Unlike explicit intermediate steps in verbal reasoning which can be verified through logic or automated tests, visual reasoning involves implicit intermediate steps with no clear methods for verification. Non-verbal reasoning is therefore challenging to model and, as a result, remains a fundamental limitation of current machine intelligence. Here we introduce GenVerOpt, a general framework that unifies generation, verification and optimisation into a reasoning pipeline for machines. By generating candidate solutions, verifying their correctness, and iteratively refining them, GenVerOpt enables per-sample optimisation rather than relying solely on dataset-level trends captured during model training. This process mirrors human problem-solving where multiple possibilities are imagined, evaluated, and refined until a correct solution emerges. Across diverse domains, GenVerOpt demonstrates capabilities beyond existing systems: it surpasses humans on abstract IQ puzzles, matches clinicians in prostate cancer segmentation, exceeds experts in liver vessel reconstruction and outperforms state-of-the-art approaches in tumour boundary alignment. These results show that machine reasoning can extend beyond language, establishing visual non-verbal reasoning as a foundation for more flexible and generalisable intelligence.
