MIRAGE: Metric Inference and Reliability-Aware Gaussian Estimation from Feed-Forward Vision

MIRAGE: Metric Inference and Reliability-Aware Gaussian Estimation from Feed-Forward Vision

Xinzhe Wang、Changjian Jiang、Kaiwen Song、Xudong Li、Kerui Ren 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Feed-forward 3D priors enable high-quality reconstruction from image streams but often lack reliable metric scale and suffer from long-term trajectory drift. In contrast, LiDAR--inertial estimation provides metrically accurate motion but lacks the image-space consistency needed for high-fidelity reconstruction. We propose $MIRAGE$, which anchors feed-forward visual geometry to LiDAR--IMU metric motion and regulates online pose corrections according to correspondence support and cross-modal agreement, jointly enabling accurate metric pose estimation and high-fidelity Gaussian reconstruction. Experiments on Oxford Spires, M2DGR and INS show that MIRAGE achieves accurate metric trajectory estimation, outperforming COLMAP, a widely used but computationally expensive reference for vision-only benchmarks. Motivated by the scarcity of long-horizon monocular benchmarks for large-scale indoor reconstruction, we further build a multimodal data-collection platform and use MIRAGE to establish accurate 6-DoF reference trajectories for a new vision-only benchmark. We benchmark recent monocular large-scale reconstruction methods on this dataset, providing a systematic evaluation of their performance in long-horizon indoor scenes.

2026-10-08

Fly-by-Code: Embodied Coding Agents for Aerial Manipulation with Active Visual and Physical Feedback

Fly-by-Code: Embodied Coding Agents for Aerial Manipulation with Active Visual and Physical Feedback

Jaewoo Lee、Jeongyeon Seo、Sihyun Cho、Gyeongrak Choe、Yutong Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Embodied coding agents construct robot policies from natural-language instructions by generating executable programs and refining them from execution feedback. This refinement assumes that execution feedback reveals whether the task succeeded and, after a failure, provides sufficient evidence to guide program revision. Aerial manipulation violates this assumption in two ways. Payload constraints restrict onboard camera coverage and leave task-critical outcomes outside the robot's view (partial observability), while strong dynamic coupling and limited control margins make physical interactions fragile (limited interaction robustness) and leave failure causes unresolved. Re-examining the same feedback cannot recover observations that were never collected or separate failure causes that the available evidence does not distinguish. To acquire this missing evidence, we introduce Active Feedback (AF), an active "View and Probe" framework. When execution feedback leaves unresolved questions, the agent synthesizes a separate diagnostic program that runs in simulation to inspect unseen outcomes from informative viewpoints (View) or test hypotheses about failure causes through targeted physical interactions (Probe). Across six simulated aerial manipulation tasks with 60 trials per condition, AF reduces false completion declarations from 25 to 5 and raises final task success from 31.7% to 75% compared with terminal feedback under the same coding-agent configuration and task-execution limit. Using shared robot APIs, task programs refined with AF in simulation run on a physical aerial manipulator without task-program source-code modification and complete multi-stage manipulation tasks in the real world. These results show that executable diagnosis fills gaps in execution feedback for embodied coding agents, improving both completion assessment and program refinement in aerial manipulation.

These results show that executable diagnosis fills gaps in execution feedback for embodied coding agents, improving both completion assessment and program refinement in aerial manipulation.

2026-10-07

TerraAgent: Demonstration-Grounded Multi-Agent Reasoning for Long-Context Remote Sensing Visual Question Answering

TerraAgent: Demonstration-Grounded Multi-Agent Reasoning for Long-Context Remote Sensing Visual Question Answering

Jingwen Huang、Yiming Fang、Hanxiao Wu、Boyuan He、Keyi Luo 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Remote sensing visual question answering (RS-VQA) requires answers that truly depend on image evidence, yet current remote sensing multimodal large language models (RS MLLMs) are still evaluated mainly by accuracy, which cannot reveal whether a correct answer was produced without using the image. This setting introduces three challenges: demonstrations retrieved across different ground sample distances (GSD), sensors, and city textures may be poorly matched; real queries often involve long visual contexts from multi-scale tiles, multi-temporal frames, and retrieved knowledge, where evidence can be diluted and errors can propagate through multi-step reasoning; and prior-only answering remains invisible to standard metrics. We present AVA, a training-free framework that wraps a frozen RS MLLM with three modules. GDR performs GSD-bucketed, scene-graph-based demonstration retrieval and filters demonstrations whose answers are unsupported by their images. MACE decomposes each query into an evidence-typed sub-question DAG, routes sub-questions to suitable perception tools, and records verified provenance in a shared evidence ledger with bounded re-perception. CVA audits the final answer through counterfactual perturbations of cited evidence, including ablation and swap, and computes a visual-dependency score for confidence calibration and evidence-forcing re-query. On RSVQA-LR/HR, VRSBench, and LHRS-Bench, AVA improves average accuracy by up to 2.40 points over the strongest same-setting baselines, raises the visual-dependency score from 0.28 to 0.49, reduces the prior-only answer rate from 46.2 percent to 21.7 percent, and lowers the hallucination rate from 17.1 percent to 9.8 percent, with 2.6x the cost of a single backbone forward pass and no fine-tuning.

2026-10-09

MotionCoach: Constructing Multimodal Guidance for Video Professional Motion Editing

MotionCoach: Constructing Multimodal Guidance for Video Professional Motion Editing

Siyi Qian、Hanqing Yang、Weihuang Lin、Zihao Yang、Yichen Lu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Video editing models achieve strong performance in modifying appearance, backgrounds, styles, and motions, empowered by large-scale training data. Beyond general scenarios, professional motions are significant in performance, sports, and martial arts. This paper focuses on professional motion editing, which involves fine-grained motion technique and temporal structure. Given professional motion names but lacking detailed descriptions, existing models often fail to generate professional motions, such as incorrectly lifting the front wheel under a Stoppie instruction. To address this challenge, we introduce MotionCoach, a trained multimodal agent that uses multi-turn tools and performs self-verification, producing a grounded prompt and a reference clip as reliable and sufficient multimodal guidance to inject external professional motion knowledge into video editing models. To supply training data, we build a data pipeline to construct trajectories. We cold-start the agent on MotionCoach-SFT-10k and apply Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) on MotionCoach-RL-2k with rewards on the motion guidance prompt, the motion outcome video, and the format. For evaluation, we introduce VPMEBench, comprising 314 professional motion editing tasks with fine-grained references and annotations. Extensive experiments show that MotionCoach improves the overall score from 55.1 to 65.6 on Seedance 2.0 and from 51.2 to 64.5 on MiniMax-H3. Learning to construct multimodal guidance enables precise professional motion editing without retraining video editing models.

多模态/VLM
2026-10-09

MVGraph: Benchmarking LVLMs on Evidence Composition across Multi-View Visual Graph

MVGraph: Benchmarking LVLMs on Evidence Composition across Multi-View Visual Graph

Yingjie Zhu、Han Cheng、Xuefeng Bai、Youcheng Pan、Yang Xiang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

We introduce MVGraph, a benchmark for evaluating large vision-language models (LVLMs) on multi-graph reasoning where the evidence required by a query is distributed across multiple interrelated visual graph views. Although such evidence commonly resides in source-specific graphs, temporal snapshots, or local observations, existing graph reasoning benchmarks remain confined to a single self-contained structure, assessing only graph perception and reasoning while leaving the alignment and composition of evidence across views underexplored. MVGraph comprises 8k instances spanning 8 \textit{graph-theoretic} tasks, abstracted from two multi-graph scenarios (partial and temporal graph views). Each problem is programmatically constructed and verified to be unsolvable from a single view. Beyond end-to-end evaluation, we decompose multi-graph reasoning into four complementary capabilities---Perception, Alignment, Composition, and Reasoning---and design 29 diagnostic tasks with 64k instances to probe each one, along with golden intermediate representations for evaluating the intermediate process. Across 15 LVLMs, we reveal the difficulty of multi-graph reasoning and identify cross-view edge alignment and relation-level composition as prominent bottlenecks through stage-oriented diagnostics. Meanwhile, temporal constraints and increasing view counts expose further capability boundaries. Code and data are available at https://github.com/AAAndy-Zhu/MVGraph.

Across 15 LVLMs, we reveal the difficulty of multi-graph reasoning and identify cross-view edge alignment and relation-level composition as prominent bottlenecks through stage-oriented diagnostics. Meanwhile, temporal constraints and increasing view counts expose further capability boundaries. Code and data are available at https://github.com/AAAndy-Zhu/MVGraph.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM
2026-10-09

Video Codec-Guided Training-Free Token Compression for Video Large Language Models

Video Codec-Guided Training-Free Token Compression for Video Large Language Models

Xihua Sheng、Ruijie Liu、Chang Wen Chen

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Video large language models (VLLMs) rely on long visual token sequences to understand complex temporal content, which makes visual token compression important for efficient inference. Existing training-free compression methods mainly reduce the number of tokens sent to the language model, but the compression process itself can still require many visual feature comparisons, especially for intra-frame token protection and inter-frame temporal token merging. This paper proposes a video codec-guided training-free token compression method for VLLMs that uses coding information already available in compressed video as a low-cost search prior. The method aligns reference-frame dependencies, motion vectors, and residuals with the visual token grid. Residual information forms compact candidate pools for token protection, while motion information is propagated along reference-frame chains to construct localized candidates for temporal token merging between sparsely sampled frames. Local visual feature similarity comparison is then used within these candidates to make the final compression decisions, without modifying the base VLLM. Experiments on three representative VLLMs and four video-understanding benchmarks show that the proposed method achieves performance comparable to the state-of-the-art training-free baseline FlashVID, and in some settings surpasses it, while substantially reducing the feature comparisons required by token protection and temporal matching. These results demonstrate that codec information can effectively improve the accuracy-complexity tradeoff of video token compression.

A Lightweight Velocity Correction Framework for Improving Instruction Following in Few-Step Text-to-Image Generation

A Lightweight Velocity Correction Framework for Improving Instruction Following in Few-Step Text-to-Image Generation

Yating Gao、Zhenlong Zhu、Wen Zihao、Lenan Zhang、Jiexiong Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Few-step text-to-image generators produce high-quality images with few network forward passes, yet struggle with complex prompts involving multiple subjects, interactions, spatial relations, counting, and attribute binding. Can these errors be reduced without changing the generator's parameters or increasing its forward-pass count? We propose a lightweight correction framework for a frozen 6B-parameter generator. The generator predicts velocities that determine how its intermediate image representation is updated; a corrector with fewer than 20M parameters learns residual adjustments at selected sampling steps. Because earlier corrections change the states encountered at later steps, our On-Trajectory Flow-Bridge Supervision derives residual targets at the states actually reached during corrected generation. A single corrector is shared across selected steps and runs in parallel with the generator. Only the corrector is trained, requiring neither reward optimization nor re-distillation. To our knowledge, this is the first framework for lightweight velocity-residual adaptation of fully frozen few-step text-to-image generators that preserves their original sampling schedule and forward-pass count. On our target complex-scene distribution, the framework achieves a +17.60-percentage-point NetWin over frozen Z-Image-Turbo in pairwise instruction-following evaluation, with only 26.9 ms (0.84%) additional end-to-end latency per image and no increase in backbone evaluations.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM3D视觉视频理解机器人视觉
2026-10-09

MeshBind: Point-to-Mesh Binding for Non-Rigid Scene Flow Automatic Annotation

MeshBind: Point-to-Mesh Binding for Non-Rigid Scene Flow Automatic Annotation

Yinqi Chen、E Li、Shuaijun Wang、Weiyuan Peng、Meiying Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Scene flow estimation is essential for many autonomous driving (AD) and robotic applications, but obtaining ground-truth scene flow for non-rigid objects such as pedestrians is highly difficult. We present MeshBind, an automatic annotation method that uses mesh models to generate per-point scene flow for non-rigid objects. The novelty of this work is threefold: 1) developing a multi-view module that reconstructs an articulated mesh model from six surround cameras and registers it to the LiDAR frame; 2) developing a local-frame binding technique that attaches each point to the mesh via a per-triangle tangent-normal frame, invariant under rigid motion yet adaptive to articulated deformation; 3) developing a three-layer flow registration pipeline that combines ego-vehicle motion compensation, box binding, and mesh binding to produce full-scene per-point flow. We implement MeshBind in SF-Studio, an interactive annotation tool that visualises flow and allows human mesh corrections with automatic recomputation. We also release SFB (SUScape-Flow Benchmark), a large-scale benchmark built on the public SUScape dataset, covering 41k frames with per-point non-rigid pedestrian annotations and a Rigid/Semi-Rigid/Non-Rigid evaluation protocol. Experiments show that fine-tuning on SFB reduces three-way endpoint error (EPE) by 2-3x, and brings non-rigid EPE to the same level as rigid EPE.

Experiments show that fine-tuning on SFB reduces three-way endpoint error (EPE) by 2-3x, and brings non-rigid EPE to the same level as rigid EPE.

2026-10-09

Win-Win Fusion: Multi-Modal Image Fusion with Visual-Semantic Collaboration

Win-Win Fusion: Multi-Modal Image Fusion with Visual-Semantic Collaboration

Hao Zhang、Xuhui Zuo、Jiayi Ma

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Existing multi-modal image fusion (MMIF) methods generally face an optimization gap between the visual and semantic domains, limiting the enhancement of perception capabilities. To overcome this long-standing barrier, this work proposes W2F, a new visual-semantic mutual reinforcing paradigm for MMIF. First, we reinforce the foundation for visual-semantic collaboration by amplifying feature complementarity through two targeted designs. Specifically, the segmentation-guided fusion loss introduces object-aware structural guidance into visual fusion, while the embedded information restoration prior strengthens the ability of visual fusion to provide reliable perceptual evidence for the semantic branch under degraded conditions. Then, we introduce a strong multi-modal information interaction mechanism composed of interactive gated mixed attention (IGM-Att) and progressive coupling attention (PC-Att). These modules ensure comprehensive scene representation from both local and global perspectives, providing informational support for visual-semantic collaboration. Finally, we construct a collaborative mechanism to couple visual fusion and semantic fusion. It employs a feature search module to extract complementary key features from both tasks, enriching the representational potential of visual and semantic information. Meanwhile, a gradient correction strategy ensures collaboration from the optimization perspective, enhancing performance for both visual and semantic fusion through consistency learning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that W2F surpasses state-of-the-art methods in both visual appeal and decision accuracy. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/HaoZhang1018/W2F.

APEX: Asynchronous Prefix Denoising for Extreme Compression

APEX: Asynchronous Prefix Denoising for Extreme Compression

David Dinkevich、Nisan Chiprut、Yoav HaCohen、Dani Lischinski

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Fewer latent tokens promise cheaper diffusion. Yet aggressive latent compression creates a reconstruction-generation dilemma: adding channels improves reconstruction but makes generation harder. We introduce APEX, short for Asynchronous Prefix denoising for EXtreme compression, which pairs a hierarchical latent space with asynchronous denoising over channel groups. Our autoencoder orders channel prefixes from structure to detail, while one shared denoiser generates them at staggered local times without adding tokens. On ImageNet-512, APEX outperforms existing methods at every evaluated channel count under 64x and 128x compression. At matched training and guided sampling compute, it also outperforms a 256-token model at every evaluated budget, with larger gains at lower compute. Code is available at https://daviddinkevich.github.io/APEX.

Stride: Scale-Aware Multi-Agent Evolutionary Refinement for Infographic Generation

Stride: Scale-Aware Multi-Agent Evolutionary Refinement for Infographic Generation

Yan Zhou、Yang Yang

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Infographic generation challenges text-to-image models because a single image must satisfy numerous visual, textual, and semantic constraints. Iterative agentic refinement with a multimodal verifier is a natural remedy, but existing methods face a scale mismatch: local edits cannot correct global composition errors, while full regeneration may destroy already-correct regions. We introduce Stride, a constraint-grounded multi-agent framework that treats refinement over any frozen generator as a history-aware evolution strategy. Stride separates constraint evaluation - whether each requirement is satisfied - from visual grounding - where and how it is violated. It then routes global revisions to a composer subagent and local corrections to a retoucher subagent. Each subagent conducts internal planning and generates multiple candidates, while Stride accepts a candidate only when it improves the verifier score, reducing regression across iterations. Because stochastic generators and imperfect verifiers make any single step unreliable, it is this combination of scale-aware routing, per-step redundancy, and monotonic acceptance that yields stable improvement across iterations. With fully open-weight generator-verifier systems, Stride outperforms proprietary systems including Nano-Banana-2.0 and Seedream-5.0-Pro on BizGenEval, matches GPT-Image-2.0 and surpasses Seedream-5.0-Pro on IGenBench, and achieves a new state-of-the-art score on WISE, while substantially outperforming existing agentic methods across all evaluated benchmarks.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM医学图像
2026-10-09

When MLLMs miss what humans read: diagnosing and learning to read squint text

When MLLMs miss what humans read: diagnosing and learning to read squint text

Chen Peng、Shan Huang、Jianbo Huang、Linrui Qin、Qibo Sun 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multimodal LLMs can fail to read text embedded in an image's coarse structure (text a human recovers by squinting) and report that no text is present. We name this class Squint Text and study it as a recognition deficiency, motivated by cases from a production moderation pipeline. We introduce SquintBench, comprising 1076 production-derived moderation images, and SquintCtrl, a controlled synthetic suite for mechanism diagnosis. Frozen-weight analysis shows that hidden text leaves a coarse signal while glyph identity is weakly represented; identity becomes substantially more accessible when the correct spatial regions are supplied, implicating spatial selection as a major bottleneck. Suppressing high-frequency content also improves reading at a fixed visual-token budget, while visual token count may also contribute. These findings motivate Squint OPSD: a frozen teacher receives a downscaled view and the ground-truth transcription as privileged context, and its token-level distributions supervise a student along on-policy trajectories. At inference, the student reads the original image alone. Squint OPSD reaches 88.61 ANLS on SquintBench, versus 26.50 for the unadapted backbone, 24.44 for the best general-purpose model on the original view, and 68.60 at best for an off-the-shelf model under a downscaled view. These results substantially narrow the gap between available visual evidence and its generated read-out without auxiliary views at inference.

Why DiT Models Underperform as Representation Learners without Long Skip Connections

Why DiT Models Underperform as Representation Learners without Long Skip Connections

Benyuan Meng、Yiliang Zhang、Jin-Wen Wu、Qianqian Xu、Qingming Huang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Diffusion feature, i.e., intermediate activations extracted from diffusion model backbones, has emerged as a promising approach for unifying generation and vision understanding (discrimination). Despite the advancement of diffusion backbones into Diffusion Transformers (DiT), it has been found in previous literature that DiT models tend to yield weaker features despite their stronger generation capability. This poses a threat to good generation-understanding unification. In this work, we show via controlled from-scratch training that long skip connections (LSCs) affect feature quality: a DiT with LSCs can produce better features. Furthermore, through detailed diagnosis, we reveal the mechanism causing this effect. Specifically, the input to a diffusion backbone contains both the latent image and the diffusion noise, where the diffusion noise, though appearing in the form of noises, actually reflects the information of the diffusion trajectory. To make the final prediction of a noisy variable ($v$ or $ε$), the diffusion backbone needs both signals. However, carrying both signals through the main backbone effectively "pollutes" the image signal with noises, leading to poor feature quality. In comparison, LSCs can shortcut diffusion noises directly to where they are needed, leaving much weaker noise signals in the main backbone, thus improving feature quality. To name the broader impact, this reveals that LSCs are not a mere trick but closely related to the diffusion objective. The code is at https://github.com/Darkbblue/dit-lsc.

LETHE: Defending against Unauthorized Image-to-Video Generation via Value Diversity Collapse

LETHE: Defending against Unauthorized Image-to-Video Generation via Value Diversity Collapse

Boheng Li、Yueming Xing、Yiming Li、Jie Zhang、Tianwei Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Image-to-video (I2V) diffusion models can animate a single reference image into high-quality, subject-consistent videos, posing threats of misuse as anyone can turn personal photos into fabricated videos without consent. A promising solution is to proactively add an adversarial perturbation to the image before release, such that videos generated from it would be unfaithful or visually corrupted. However, due to an insufficient understanding of what governs how subsequent frames read the reference image in modern I2V models, existing protections struggle to offer strong protection under common perturbation budgets. In this paper, we approach the problem by exploring this underlying reading mechanism. Through a convex geometric analysis, we theoretically identify the spread of the reference value vectors as a key factor governing this reading process, and empirically find that even a mild contraction of this spread can severely degrade the generated video, making it an efficient lever for protection. Guided by this insight, we propose LETHE, a novel protection method that directly disrupts this reading process. LETHE pairs two complementary objectives, where a value collapse objective drives the reference value vectors toward uniformity, while an attention attraction objective draws subsequent frames toward these collapsed tokens to amplify corruption. We further construct two human-centric I2V protection benchmarks and conduct extensive experiments with multiple modern I2V models, which demonstrate the state-of-the-art protection performance and versatility of LETHE.

Multi-Source and Multi-Dimensional Information Fusion in Resource-Constrained Edge Networks

Multi-Source and Multi-Dimensional Information Fusion in Resource-Constrained Edge Networks

Yushi Wang、Zhengxin Yu、Guhan Zheng、Wang Miao、Merouane Debbah 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multi-source semantic information fusion enables edge networks to support global inference using observations collected by distributed terminals. However, limited communication and computation resources restrict semantic uploading, leaving the edge server with incomplete tokens that differ in modality, semantic granularity, arrival time, and channel condition. The value of these tokens also depends on the complementary or redundant information provided by other terminals. In this paper, we propose a multi-source and multi-dimensional semantic information fusion framework that coordinates terminal uploading and edge-side fusion under resource constraints. We develop Hyper-Slot to combine semantic content with available token attributes through iterative token-to-slot assignment and slot-to-token feedback, producing a compact representation for global inference. To encourage useful semantic uploading, we further design a contribution-based incentive mechanism that uses Hyper-Slot assignments to evaluate information coverage and cross-type complementarity. A bi-level Stackelberg-Nash game determines the server reward pool and terminal transmission-quality strategies while accounting for computation and communication energy costs. Experiments on a tri-modal extension of COCO2014 and image-text retrieval on Flickr30K show that Hyper-Slot improves mAP by 3.66 percentage points and RSum by 15.9 points over the strongest evaluated baselines, respectively, while reducing the number of parameters by more than 80% compared with a naive concatenation-based fusion baseline.

QCLR: A Plug-in Robustification Module via Quantization for Contrastive Learning in Self-Supervised Skeleton-Based Human Action Recognition

QCLR: A Plug-in Robustification Module via Quantization for Contrastive Learning in Self-Supervised Skeleton-Based Human Action Recognition

Nanzhu Chen、Feixiang He、Yunfeng Diao、Duygu Sarikaya、He Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Skeleton-based human action recognition has gained increasing attention due to the stability of skeleton data across diverse environments and advances in motion capture technologies. Following the success of supervised learning approaches, self-supervised learning approaches, particularly contrastive learning ones, have achieved impressive performance. However, they remain vulnerable to adversarial attacks. In this work, we propose a plug-in robustification module via quantization for contrastive learning in self-supervised skeleton-based human action recognition (QCLR), which enhances the robustness of representations against adversarial attacks while preserving their representational capability. Specifically, we employ a dual-level quantization scheme across the encoding pipeline, which breaks the gradient propagation and absorbs small adversarial perturbations within the quantization bins. To mitigate the impact of inevitable quantization error, we further design an attention-guided multi-faceted quantization strategy to maintain the representation quality. Experiments on several benchmark datasets demonstrate that integrating QCLR into a progressive lineage of mainstream contrastive learning models substantially improves robustness against both gradient-based white-box and gradient-free black-box adversarial attacks, while preserving highly competitive classification accuracy.

2026-10-09

OWLET: On-Policy Weighted Learning via Evidence Transfer for Video Large Language Model Post-Training

OWLET: On-Policy Weighted Learning via Evidence Transfer for Video Large Language Model Post-Training

Qitan Lv、Ke Fan、Hong Wang、Wen Wu、Yilin Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Post-training has become a key stage for eliciting reasoning capabilities in Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs). Existing post-training methods mainly follow two paradigms: reinforcement learning (RL) and on-policy distillation (OPD). However, they do not fully exploit visual evidence for effective training. RL supervises the policy with a single trajectory-level reward, which is too coarse-grained for video reasoning: the same response-level advantage is applied to every generated token, disregarding that the tokens of a video response are grounded in the visual stream to very different degrees. OPD provides token-level supervision through output-distribution matching, but lacks direct supervision of the spatiotemporal representations that produce those outputs. We observe across extensive experiments that the same model reasons more accurately when its video view combines global temporal context with question-relevant details. Comparing the same student-generated response under this view and uniform sampling reveals changes in token likelihoods and internal representations, providing a training signal for evidence use. Building on this observation, we propose OWLET, an evidence-privileged RL method that derives training signals from a richer teacher view. This view couples uniform global context with question-conditioned local windows, providing visual evidence without requiring a larger model. To better exploit this privileged visual evidence, OWLET introduces (i) \textbf{evidence-aware credit redistribution}, which compares token likelihoods across the two views and redistributes positive response-level advantages according to their relative evidence support; and (ii) \textbf{spatiotemporal state alignment}, which aligns the two views' hidden states on verifier-accepted responses.

Post-training has become a key stage for eliciting reasoning capabilities in Video Large Language Models (Video-LLMs). Existing post-training methods mainly follow two paradigms: reinforcement learning (RL) and on-policy distillation (OPD). However, they do not fully exploit visual evidence for effective training. RL supervises the policy with a single trajectory-level reward, which is too coarse-grained for video reasoning: the same response-level advantage is applied to every generated token, disregarding that the tokens of a video response are grounded in the visual stream to very different degrees. OPD provides token-level supervision through output-distribution matching, but lacks direct supervision of the spatiotemporal representations that produce those outputs. We observe across extensive experiments that the same model reasons more accurately when its video view combines global temporal context with question-relevant details. Comparing the same student-generated response under this view and uniform sampling reveals changes in token likelihoods and internal representations, providing a training signal for evidence use. Building on this observation, we propose OWLET, an evidence-privileged RL method that derives training signals from a richer teacher view. This view couples uniform global context with question-conditioned local windows, providing visual evidence without requiring a larger model. To better exploit this privileged visual evidence, OWLET introduces (i) \textbf{evidence-aware credit redistribution}, which compares token likelihoods across the two views and redistributes positive response-level advantages according to their relative evidence support; and (ii) \textbf{spatiotemporal state alignment}, which aligns the two views' hidden states on verifier-accepted responses.

Automated Detection and Encoding of Cartographic Signs in Historical Maps

Automated Detection and Encoding of Cartographic Signs in Historical Maps

Remi Petitpierre

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Symbols and icons in archival maps represent an expansive source of evidence for historical geography. In this paper, we present a two-stage framework for (1) detecting cartographic signs in historical maps and (2) generating interpretable sign embeddings that capture both semantics and stylistic variations. We also introduce CaSiDD, an open benchmark dataset spanning multiple sign categories and diverse cartographic styles, designed for generic sign detection. Using YOLOv10-X, we demonstrate the practicality of automated sign detection on historical maps. We then present strategies for creating contrasted, informative embeddings of cartographic signs. We approach embedding design systematically, maximizing expressiveness while suppressing digitization or background material confounders. Our final method, based on an adapted RADIOv4 backbone, effectively encodes both sign form and semantics, while producing interpretable representations. This work lays a foundation for scalable evidence extraction for the comparative study of map signs within cartographic culture and geohistorical research.

2026-10-09

CompactSplat: Spatially Adaptive Gaussian Distribution for Feedforward Scene Reconstruction

CompactSplat: Spatially Adaptive Gaussian Distribution for Feedforward Scene Reconstruction

Bing He、Jingnan Gao、Yunuo Chen、Zhengxue Cheng、Rong Xie 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reconstructing 3D scenes from sparse views without per-scene optimization remains highly challenging, especially for recovering accurate geometry and fine textures. While recent feedforward approaches leverage generalizable 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) for scene generation, they typically assign one or multiple Gaussians to each pixel. Such uniform allocation not only produces highly redundant representations by wasting primitives in homogeneous areas, but also fails to exploit the inherent geometric priors of planar surfaces, often resulting in sub-optimal structural modeling. To address these limitations, we present CompactSplat, a novel feedforward framework that enables a content-aware allocation of 3D Gaussians. By integrating texture-guided spatial partitioning with hierarchical sampling, CompactSplat adaptively distributes primitives - concentrating dense Gaussians in complex regions while tiling flat areas with expanded, scale-modulated primitives. Moreover, our framework inherently supports dynamic sparsity control at inference time, enabling seamless trade-offs between rendering fidelity and memory footprint without requiring network retraining. Extensive experiments on RealEstate10K, DL3DV, and ScanNet demonstrate that CompactSplat consistently outperforms prior methods on both standard metrics and high-resolution rendering consistency, achieving high-fidelity reconstructions with significantly fewer primitives.

From Glance to Focus: Mitigating Recognition Laziness in MLLMs for Fine-Grained Recognition

From Glance to Focus: Mitigating Recognition Laziness in MLLMs for Fine-Grained Recognition

Yutong Yang、Zhuqi Liu、Mouxing Yang、Xi Peng

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) generally fall short of expectations in Fine-Grained Visual Recognition (FGVR), although they exhibit promising FGVR foundations, i.e., visual perception and broad category knowledge. Most existing studies seek to remedy this deficiency by adopting domain-specific post-training, at the risk of compromising generalization to unseen domains. Different from these efforts, in this paper, we revisit the FGVR mechanism underlying MLLMs from both the empirical and theoretical perspectives and uncover an inference-time phenomenon termed FGVR Laziness. In brief, FGVR Laziness refers to the insufficient association between visual cues and category concepts under certain candidate category lists and recognition guidance, leaving the FGVR foundations of MLLMs largely under-utilized. To mitigate FGVR Laziness, we propose Laziness-resIstant Visual rEcognition (LIVE), a training-free framework which jointly refines candidate lists and recognition guidance to elicit the latent FGVR capability of MLLMs. Specifically, LIVE first performs query-adaptive candidate inference to derive a compact yet reliable candidate list. Meanwhile, LIVE constructs category-specific FGVR skills by mining the intra-category characteristics and inter-category differences. Finally, LIVE selectively invokes detailed skill guidance according to recognition uncertainty and category confusion, shifting MLLM-based FGVR from a cursory glance to a focus on associating discriminative visual cues with category concepts. Extensive experiments on 13 benchmarks against 24 state-of-the-art methods spanning fine-grained category recognition, action recognition, and general category recognition, verify the effectiveness and generalizability of LIVE across different MLLM backbones. The code will be released upon acceptance.

The code will be released upon acceptance.

AV-FlowEdit: Training-Free Prompt-Driven Audio-Visual Editing with Event-Aware Spectral Trajectory Preservation

AV-FlowEdit: Training-Free Prompt-Driven Audio-Visual Editing with Event-Aware Spectral Trajectory Preservation

Shaohua Pan、Xiao Liu

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Joint audio-video diffusion models generate synchronized content, but editing existing clips requires target changes without disrupting non-target structure or cross-modal correspondence. We present AV-FlowEdit, a training-free and inversion-free framework for prompt-driven audio-video editing. Our key insight is that source--target flow differences provide both cross-modal editing directions and preservation cues. Under shared perturbations, they jointly update the video and audio branches, supporting visual-only, audio-only, and joint edits while retaining pretrained cross-modal interactions. To suppress drift accumulated during sampling, Cross-Modal Event-Aware Spectral Trajectory Preservation combines flow saliency, aligned audio activity, multiscale low-frequency displacement, and diffusion-stage scheduling. This adaptively preserves unsupported regions while allowing intended audio-visual events. AV-FlowEdit requires no model training, external masks, or optimization-based inversion. Experiments show strong prompt alignment and temporal consistency, together with competitive audio quality and cross-modal coherence.

Evolving Creatives with Clicks: CTR-Driven Advertising image Editing via Attribute Disentanglement and Preference Alignment

Evolving Creatives with Clicks: CTR-Driven Advertising image Editing via Attribute Disentanglement and Preference Alignment

Xu Zhang、Haohan Wang、Zheng Zhang、Yingjie Xia、Wei Feng 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

The optimization objective for e-commerce advertising creatives has shifted from aesthetic quality to online business metrics like click-through rate (CTR). Recent methods generate appealing ad images from product images by constructing CTR-based reward signals and refining prompt generation via Reinforcement Learning. Such paradigms equally penalize all attributes within an underperforming image, even though some are actually beneficial to clicks. In this work, we propose Evolving Creatives with Clicks (ECC), which reformulates creative optimization as fine-grained editing rather than generation from scratch. ECC selectively refines attributes that hurt CTR while preserving those that contribute positively. Specifically, faced with a rich attribute space across massive images, ECC first identifies 28 attributes significantly correlated with CTR via ANOVA and Random Forest regression. Based on attribute contrasts between images with disparate CTRs, we instruction-tune a prompt model to generate editing suggestions, then refine it with real click preferences since reference-derived suggestions are not uniformly beneficial. Furthermore, we introduce Consistent Preference Optimization (CPO) as ECC's preference optimization strategy, which enforces consistency across semantically equivalent shuffled suggestions to mitigate DPO overfitting and improve preference generalization. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ECC significantly improves the CTR of ad images, outperforming state-of-the-art models by a substantial margin. The complete data pipeline, including attribute extraction and analysis, the resulting dataset, and source code will be released upon acceptance.

Redirecting Teacher Supervision: Temporal-Response-Guided Latent Distillation for Video-Language Models

Redirecting Teacher Supervision: Temporal-Response-Guided Latent Distillation for Video-Language Models

Xinkai Lu、Junli Zha、Haolan Wei、Jiahui Wang、Chao Zhou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

On-policy distillation (OPD) transfers capabilities through teacher predictions on student-generated sequences, as studied by GKD and MiniLLM for language models and Video-OPD for video tasks. We ask how internal supervision can further support temporal learning. Direct hidden-state alignment does not explicitly use the student's temporal responses to choose a supervision direction. We propose Temporal-Response Guidance (TRG), which measures representation responses to local temporal perturbations, adds the teacher correction's projection onto their span back to the original correction, and restores its original norm. The projection is not normalized separately, and correction components outside the response space are retained. With an orthogonal tangent projector and a detached target, TRG changes the supervision direction while preserving the cosine-loss value and aligned-representation gradient norm at target construction. On TempCompass, Video-MME, MVBench, and Video-MMMU, TRG improves the 4B student's macro accuracy by 2.86 percentage points over the reported output-only OPD baseline.

Verifier-Mediated Multi-LLM Coordination for Multi-Robot Path Planning

Verifier-Mediated Multi-LLM Coordination for Multi-Robot Path Planning

Vivek Khatana、Zijian Song、Naira Hovakimyan、Petros Voulgaris

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Foundation models are increasingly used for high-level multi-robot task planning, yet independently generated symbolic plans can become jointly infeasible after geometric grounding because of shared spatial and temporal constraints. We study execution-aware coordination of large-language-model (LLM) planners for multi-robot navigation. We compare a centralized architecture, in which one LLM jointly reasons over the robot team, with a decentralized multi-LLM architecture in which each robot independently proposes a task plan. A geometric verifier grounds the candidate plans into robot trajectories, detects predicted inter-robot conflicts, and converts conflict information into structured feedback for targeted replanning. This verifier-mediated architecture separates semantic task reasoning from execution-level feasibility while enabling iterative coordination among LLM planners. Experiments in CARLA and on Crazyflie aerial robots evaluate coordination performance as task complexity increases and characterize the trade-offs between centralized reasoning, decentralized verifier-mediated coordination, and physical executability.

Distributed Harmonic-Covariance Feedback for Hole Closure in Planar Robot Swarms

Distributed Harmonic-Covariance Feedback for Hole Closure in Planar Robot Swarms

Jitian Liu、Subhrajit Bhattacharya

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We consider hole closure in a planar multi-agent system whose sensing disks form a time-varying Čech complex. We propose a harmonic-covariance feedback that uses harmonic edge signals to locate holes and converts those signals into agent forces. For distributed evaluation, we use a fixed-degree polynomial of the regularized Hodge Laplacian. This polynomial is evaluated through local operator applications. The closed loop has a unique global trajectory from every safe initial state. The model data and initial state are supplied with procedures for approximation to any requested accuracy with explicit error bounds. We assume that hole area is measurable and bounded, holes evolve continuously without splitting or merging, and only finitely many temporary new holes appear. Under these conditions, offline finite calculations produce rational lower bounds for the cumulative hole area on the time horizon without querying trajectory data. These bounds converge to that cumulative area, whether finite or infinite. If it is finite, holes close in the time-averaged sense: the average normalized hole area over every fixed-length time window tends to zero as time tends to infinity. In a 15-second numerical demo, all holes close by 9.9s and remain closed through the end.

2026-10-09

Beyond Actions: Learning Future Operation Targets for Vision-Language-Action Models

Beyond Actions: Learning Future Operation Targets for Vision-Language-Action Models

Min Lin、Bokai Zhou、Jianqi Lin、Kaiyuan Yang、Yicheng Chang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Actions specify what a robot should execute, but do not describe what those commands will achieve. Vision-language-action (VLA) policies nevertheless learn primarily by predicting action chunks from images, language, and state. We introduce Action--Future Coupled Effect Learning (AFCE), which makes a future-grounded physical Effect the policy's primary prediction target. An offline tokenizer constructs temporally ordered Effect tokens from demonstrated actions, realized robot responses, and sparse visual transitions. Reconstruction objectives encourage the representation to retain executable detail and observed physical change. A flow-matching VLA predicts Effects from current images, instruction, and robot state, while a state-conditioned decoder converts them into action chunks. Embodiment-specific interfaces allow single- and dual-arm tasks to share the Effect model. On DexJoCo, AFCE achieves $50.97\%$ overall success, improving over $π_{0.5}$ and UVT by 11.82 and 15.21 percentage points, respectively. It leads on all five bimanual tasks, highlighting the value of Effect supervision for coordinated dexterous manipulation. The project page is available at https://afceproject.github.io/AFCE-Web/.

PATACON: GPU-Parallel Tangent-Bundle RRT-Connect for Constrained Motion Planning

PATACON: GPU-Parallel Tangent-Bundle RRT-Connect for Constrained Motion Planning

Gahyun Oh、Junho An、Suhyun Jeon、Suhan Park

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Fast whole-body motion planning enables continuous replanning for humanoid robots in dynamic environments while satisfying collision avoidance and kinematic constraints. However, constrained motion planning incurs substantial computational cost due to repeated constraint projection in addition to collision checking and nearest-neighbor search. Recently, GPU-based massively parallel planners have been proposed to reduce computational cost. Nevertheless, these planners still steer in the ambient configuration space, so projecting candidates that deviate from the manifold remains a significant source of overhead, particularly in highly constrained, high-dimensional problems. In this work, we introduce PATACON, a GPU-parallel bidirectional planner that explores tangent space to exploit the local manifold geometry induced by known constraint functions. Experiments on dual-arm closed-chain and humanoid whole-body constrained planning tasks show that PATACON outperforms the prior work as configuration-space dimension and constraint codimension grow, achieving up to an 11.25 times speedup. These results suggest that GPU-parallel tangent-space exploration brings constrained whole-body motion planning closer to real-time performance.

2026-10-09

Low-Latency Mapless ESDF Queries for MAV Reactive Collision Avoidance

Low-Latency Mapless ESDF Queries for MAV Reactive Collision Avoidance

Leonard Freißmuth、Cedric Le Gentil、Helen Oleynikova、Stefan Leutenegger

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reactive collision avoidance in unknown, cluttered environments must perceive obstacles and correct motion within milliseconds. Existing methods rely on maps for high-rate collision queries, but their acceleration structures require pre- processing. That cost is amortized in long-horizon planning, where a map serves millions of queries; reactive planning, however, is a near-field, short-horizon problem with potentially only hundreds of queries per measurement, where building a map may not pay off. We propose a mapless, Graphics Processing Unit (GPU)-accelerated Euclidean Signed Distance Field (ESDF) query with negligible pre-processing that operates directly on posed depth images, with resolution set by pooling the image rather than by a voxel size. On an embedded GPU it is simultaneously more accurate and lower in total latency than every voxel-grid configuration we measured between 2 cm and 20 cm, at one to two orders of magnitude less memory at matched resolution. With our query integrated into a Model Predictive Controller (MPC), simulated runs in laser-scanned forests at up to 9 m/s collide in 0.4 % through dense branches, against 5.9 % with the best voxel grid and 5.5 % with the best mapless baseline at our resolution, whose failures we attribute to voxel resolution and total latency respectively. We deploy the system onboard at two forest sites and fly at commanded speeds of up to 4 m/s.

Hierarchical Graph Neural Network for Probabilistic Power Flow with Grid Topology Contingencies

Hierarchical Graph Neural Network for Probabilistic Power Flow with Grid Topology Contingencies

Jinbao Wang、Jun Liu、Shiliang Zhang、Xuehui Ma、Lin Yan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Data-driven surrogates reduce the nonlinear solves required for probabilistic power flow (PPF), but models tied to a fixed bus--branch graph often generalize poorly when outages change electrical coupling. This paper proposes a topology-decomposition-driven hierarchical bidirectional graph neural network (TDD--HBGNN) for PPF under unseen topologies. For each realized topology, the network is decomposed into fundamental topological units (FTUs), dynamically homogeneous voltage clusters (DHVCs), and a core backbone sub-grid (CBSG), with equivalent-admittance representations preserving electrical ports between units. A sensitivity-based linear prior captures the first-order voltage-magnitude and phase-angle response, while the HBGNN learns topology-dependent nonlinear residuals. Impedance- and uncertainty-aware bottom-up aggregation condenses local perturbations, while mode-modulated top-down broadcasting restores system context at buses. We evaluate our approach on the IEEE 118-bus and Polish 2746-bus systems under unseen N--1 and N--2 contingencies against six baseline methods. Compared with these methods, TDD--HBGNN achieves 2.47--4.20-fold lower all-bus phase-angle errors and lower phase-angle errors in every held-out event. Its voltage-magnitude advantage is concentrated near the outaged branches, where paired error ratios reach 1.75--2.58 on the IEEE system and 1.87--1.97 in Polish N--2 events. All system--contingency--baseline comparisons favor TDD--HBGNN in joint voltage--angle error. Moreover, shared hierarchical operators keep the model size at 0.658 million parameters across both grids and yield 0.161--1.793~ms forward latency per probabilistic case. These results demonstrate system-wide angular accuracy, outage-local voltage accuracy, and parameter-efficient scaling across unseen topologies.

Data-driven surrogates reduce the nonlinear solves required for probabilistic power flow (PPF), but models tied to a fixed bus--branch graph often generalize poorly when outages change electrical coupling. This paper proposes a topology-decomposition-driven hierarchical bidirectional graph neural network (TDD--HBGNN) for PPF under unseen topologies. For each realized topology, the network is decomposed into fundamental topological units (FTUs), dynamically homogeneous voltage clusters (DHVCs), and a core backbone sub-grid (CBSG), with equivalent-admittance representations preserving electrical ports between units. A sensitivity-based linear prior captures the first-order voltage-magnitude and phase-angle response, while the HBGNN learns topology-dependent nonlinear residuals. Impedance- and uncertainty-aware bottom-up aggregation condenses local perturbations, while mode-modulated top-down broadcasting restores system context at buses. We evaluate our approach on the IEEE 118-bus and Polish 2746-bus systems under unseen N--1 and N--2 contingencies against six baseline methods. Compared with these methods, TDD--HBGNN achieves 2.47--4.20-fold lower all-bus phase-angle errors and lower phase-angle errors in every held-out event. Its voltage-magnitude advantage is concentrated near the outaged branches, where paired error ratios reach 1.75--2.58 on the IEEE system and 1.87--1.97 in Polish N--2 events. All system--contingency--baseline comparisons favor TDD--HBGNN in joint voltage--angle error. Moreover, shared hierarchical operators keep the model size at 0.658 million parameters across both grids and yield 0.161--1.793~ms forward latency per probabilistic case. These results demonstrate system-wide angular accuracy, outage-local voltage accuracy, and parameter-efficient scaling across unseen topologies.

目标检测/分割多模态/VLM机器人视觉
2026-10-09

Reflect-VLA: Internalizing Reflective Guidance via Dual-Path On-Policy Self-Distillation for Autonomous Driving

Reflect-VLA: Internalizing Reflective Guidance via Dual-Path On-Policy Self-Distillation for Autonomous Driving

Yuechen Luo、He Chong、Fang Li、Shaoqing Xu、Hexin Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Reinforcement Learning (RL) substantially improves planning performance in autonomous-driving Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models. However, existing RL methods rely on scalar trajectory rewards that measure overall quality but fail to identify specific planning errors or provide actionable correction signals. In long-tail scenarios, errors in scene understanding, behavior selection, and trajectory execution are compressed into a single score. We observe that Reflective Guidance turns trajectory evaluation into explicit diagnoses and corrective instructions, substantially improving planning accuracy. Yet such guidance requires an initial prediction and its evaluation results, making it unavailable for direct single-pass inference. We propose Reflect-VLA, which treats Reflective Guidance as training-time privileged information and internalizes its planning benefits through Dual-Path On-Policy Self-Distillation (Dual-OPSD). Unlike scalar-reward optimization, Dual-OPSD uses the driving score only to route student-generated trajectories. It distills dense token-level corrective distributions from the teacher for risky trajectories while applying self-imitation to preserve reliable ones. To provide reliable corrective distributions on student rollouts, the teacher is progressively constructed through Driving-Knowledge Pretraining, Planning-and-Refinement SFT, and Self-Refinement Rejection Sampling. At inference time, Reflect-VLA generates trajectories in a single pass without explicit guidance or iterative refinement. Experiments on NAVSIM v1/v2 and Bench2Drive demonstrate competitive planning performance while reducing training time by over 90% compared with RL.

Desertification Forecasting in Semi-Arid Regions: Using Deep Learning to Probe the Observability Limits of Land Degradation Dynamics

Desertification Forecasting in Semi-Arid Regions: Using Deep Learning to Probe the Observability Limits of Land Degradation Dynamics

Filipe Moura Da Silva、Cláubio Landney Lima Bandeira

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Forecasting desertification has received far less attention than mapping it. We use a convolutional LSTM (ConvLSTM) trained on ten annual Earth Observation (EO) variables (2000-2023) over the semi-arid state of Ceará, Brazil, on a 256x206 grid at roughly 2.2 km resolution, to measure how much of the year-to-year evolution of a MapBiomas-derived degradation index (IDV) can be predicted from satellite data. The network predicts a bounded change on top of the last observed state, is trained in two stages (single-step, then recursive rollout) and is evaluated on a contiguous spatial and temporal holdout. State-wide mean IDV rose from 0.238 to 0.350 between 2000 and 2023, about 0.005 per year, but cell-level change is dominated by year-to-year jumps, and a gradient-boosting model given all channels explains only 4.7% of its variance. Against this ceiling, the ConvLSTM lowers RMSE relative to persistence by 1.6% one year ahead and 4.4% four years ahead in a held-out southern band (p < 0.001), beats persistence with a constant drift and reproduces 53-69% of the regional drift. Drift-aware baselines only help from the third year onward, by up to 4.4% in RMSE at six years. An earlier formulation that redrew the whole field each year predicted changes unrelated to the observed ones (correlation -0.06) and produced a spurious decline when run recursively. Channel knockouts suggest that elevation and EVI degrade the forecast, while slope and aspect have no measurable effect. Monte Carlo dropout and stochastic scenario maps convey the uncertainty that the expected-value forecast hides. Under climatological forcing, mean IDV rises from 0.350 in 2023 to 0.370 in 2030. EO-based desertification forecasting is therefore reliable for the regional trend, not for individual cells.

EndoRSI: Physical-Evidence-Driven Recursive Improvement of Endoscopic Robot Policies

EndoRSI: Physical-Evidence-Driven Recursive Improvement of Endoscopic Robot Policies

Chi Kit Ng、Yidong Zhang、Tianchun Wu、Siu Hing Lui、Hanlin Zhao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Artificial intelligence is beginning to automate scientific discovery, yet physical robot-policy research still relies on scientists and engineers to interpret experimental failures and decide how controllers should be revised. We present EndoRSI (Endoscopic Recursive Self-Improvement), a human-governed, self-improving robotic research system in which the embedded EndoForge coding agent iteratively develops and refines endoscopic robot policies using evidence from physical experiments. Physical execution remains isolated within a safety-constrained runtime under human authorisation. In motorised endoscopy, validation evidence first caused the agent to reject an initial action representation, while subsequent physical experiments falsified an offline-supported insertion strategy by revealing hazardous closed-loop behaviour absent from the source data. Failed physical rollouts were then incorporated into policy revision; insertion success subsequently increased from 2/21 in the first generation to 17/21 in the fourth. The frozen final policy retained competence across visual and hardware shifts and was the best performing autonomous policy across six independent ex-vivo porcine specimens. These results show that physical robot interaction can serve as experimental evidence for agent-driven robot policy research.

LeapBot-JAC: Joint Action Chains for World Action Models

LeapBot-JAC: Joint Action Chains for World Action Models

Chenhao Zhu、Baibing Liu、Pei Liu、Rui Yang、Lang Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Action-chunking policies predict future controls but execute only a prefix before replanning. How should a fixed action-token budget be organized to support execution? We propose LeapBot-JAC, which allocates sparse future-control references and dense execution targets within one shared action expert. Both chains are supervised by demonstrated controls and jointly denoised at aligned physical-time positions. Directed attention allows execution tokens to read reference tokens subject to the host's visibility constraints. JAC adds neither action tokens nor parameters and retains the host's world-modeling process; both chains participate in inference, while only the execution chain drives the robot. On LIBERO, JAC reaches 98.3% success with FastWAM and LeapBot-WA, up from 97.0% and 97.3%. On zero-shot LIBERO-Plus, their success rates improve from 53.5% to 70.4% and from 73.1% to 77.3%, respectively. RoboTwin and two real-world tasks provide additional deployment evaluations. Single-training-seed comparisons and FastWAM ablations support action-role organization as a practical design choice under a fixed action-token budget.

TAME-Nav: Temporal Asymmetric Mixture-of-Experts Distillation for Agile and Versatile Robot Navigation

TAME-Nav: Temporal Asymmetric Mixture-of-Experts Distillation for Agile and Versatile Robot Navigation

Lingjie Zhang、Jun Xue、Shanze Wang、Haoyu Xi、Xinming Zhang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Agile and reliable navigation is a core capability for embodied robots navigating complex spatial constraints and dynamic interactions. However, a single policy often struggles to accommodate the distinct decision-making demands of diverse scenarios. We present TAME-Nav, an asymmetric Teacher-Student navigation framework built around a temporal soft mixture of experts (Soft-MoE). The Teacher combines clean observations with causally maintained dynamic-obstacle tracks to construct privileged representations, while the Student learns complementary navigation features from corrupted observation histories through temporal encoding, soft expert routing, and latent distillation. Procedural scenario generation and parallel task-level simulation broaden training coverage, while deployment-informed asymmetric domain randomization supports sim-to-real transfer. Across in-distribution and unseen compositional scenarios, TAME-Nav achieves higher success rates, lower collision rates, and higher navigation scores than representative baselines. On a real quadruped robot, it completes all twenty trials across local-trap escape and narrow-passage navigation with sudden dynamic obstacles, with only one minor contact.

AutoBio+: Benchmarking Dexterous Manipulation with Dynamic Experimental Phenomena in Realistic Laboratories

AutoBio+: Benchmarking Dexterous Manipulation with Dynamic Experimental Phenomena in Realistic Laboratories

Xuanye Wu、Yue Shi、Yongcan Yang、Yiqun Wang、Zhaoqi Wan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Automating biological laboratories requires robots to perform dexterous tool interactions under realistic visual conditions while handling dynamic experimental phenomena, presenting significant challenges for robotic manipulation. To enable systematic evaluation under these conditions, we introduce AutoBio+, a simulation benchmark and framework for dexterous manipulation in realistic biological laboratory environments. AutoBio+ comprises 14 tasks across 4 robot embodiments, spanning functional tool use, precision manipulation, language-conditioned visual grounding, and instrument operation. We introduce controllable dynamic experimental phenomena, including flames, vapor, and bubbles, to capture the evolving visual conditions of laboratory procedures. To further enhance environmental realism, we develop a hybrid rendering pipeline that integrates interactive simulated foreground objects with Gaussian-splatting backgrounds reconstructed from 6 real laboratory scenes. For efficient large-scale data generation and evaluation, we further build a parallel simulation framework on top of mjlab, proposing Parallel Finite-State Machine (PFSM) and integrating batched rendering with decoupled trajectory replay for demonstration generation and model-independent closed-loop policy evaluation. Our evaluation reveals substantial variation in policy performance across tasks, exposing distinct strengths and weaknesses of current manipulation policies. Real-world experiments further demonstrate a reduced sim-to-real gap, supporting the validity of AutoBio+ for realistic manipulation evaluation. Supplementary materials are available at https://autobio-plus.github.io/AutoBioPlus.

Probabilistic Ship Trajectory Forecasting using Transformer Models on AIS Data

Probabilistic Ship Trajectory Forecasting using Transformer Models on AIS Data

Frederik E. T. Schöller

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Anticipation of vessel trajectories is an essential task when navigating at sea. This applies to both human navigators and autonomous surface vessels. Trajectory forecasting enables sub-routines such as collision avoidance to assess the situation and react accordingly in time. This paper investigates Transformer and Performer models for trajectory prediction of surface vessels in confined waters. Using widely available position data from the Automatic Identification System (AIS), candidate models are trained and evaluated. Historical information on how other vessels have moved in the local area surrounding each agent is conveyed using positional heat maps. The heat maps are generated using Kernel Density Estimation (KDE) and encoded with a Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) before entering the Transformer models. The models are trained with quantile loss to efficiently obtain a model-aware prediction interval, as this is crucial for timely collision avoidance. The proposed model architectures are shown to greatly outperform previous work while the inclusion of local heat maps enables the prediction of vessel movement that is not otherwise indicated from the previous positions.

Noise Space Learning for VLA Adaptation

Noise Space Learning for VLA Adaptation

Junjie Lu、Chuheng Zhang、Yucen Wang、Yuhua Jiang、Xinyao Qin 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Pretrained vision-language-action (VLA) models encode broad manipulation priors, yet adapting them to specific tasks requires balancing limited robot interaction, computational resources, and safety constraints. Noise space learning (NSL) adapts flow-matching-based VLAs by learning a context-conditioned distribution over noise while keeping the pretrained VLA fixed. Building on prior noise-steering methods, we formulate NSL as an interface for VLA adaptation that accommodates different learning algorithms and instantiate it with PPO, SAC, and AWR. Theoretically, we provide an information-theoretic analysis of how NSL enables efficient adaptation. Empirically, we study these NSL algorithms on LIBERO, RoboTwin, and four real-robot manipulation tasks under interaction, computation, and execution constraints. Across these settings, we develop a suite of effective NSL variants and derive practical recommendations for choosing among them under different resource and deployment regimes.

Chronos Policy: Learning Robot Policies on Physical Time

Chronos Policy: Learning Robot Policies on Physical Time

Changhao Wang、Jaden Clark、Shuran Song

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Robot policies often convert sensing and control into fixed-rate observation-action sequences, leaving physical time implicit in sequence position. This assumption breaks when observation rates or delays vary, or when inference latency changes. We introduce Chronos Policy, which makes physical time explicit by conditioning on observation capture times and desired action execution times. We study two ways to incorporate time into a diffusion transformer: adding timestamp embeddings to token features, or encoding physical time in positional encoding through time-aware rotary positional encodings (time-aware RoPE). We find that time-aware RoPE is more robust, allowing attention to depend directly on physical time while keeping token content unchanged. During training, we temporally randomize which observations are sampled and when the action chunk begins, exposing the policy to a wider range of sensing and execution schedules that may occur during deployments. This also enables learning from asynchronous, multi-rate data without forcing all measurements onto a fixed temporal grid. Across simulation and real-world experiments, Chronos Policy improves performance on dynamic tasks, where observation-to-action timing is critical, and on multisensory tasks, where the relative timing between observations matters. On standard quasi-static tasks, it preserves nominal performance while improving robustness to changes in sensing and inference timing.

BT-Agent: Deploying Onboard Robot Agents with Behavior Trees and Multi-Fidelity Evaluation

BT-Agent: Deploying Onboard Robot Agents with Behavior Trees and Multi-Fidelity Evaluation

Fabio Hübel、Adam Lilja、Yue Yin、Siming He、Claire Tomlin 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Deploying language-model agents onboard mobile robots requires task reasoning and responsive execution within limited compute and memory. We propose BT-Agent, an onboard architecture combining LLM-driven task decisions with predefined behavior-tree (BT) skills. These skills enforce execution conditions and return structured failure feedback. We demonstrate fully onboard operation with a 9B model on a quadruped across five task categories. Intervention tests show that local checks stop ongoing navigation without waiting for another model decision, while the agent remains active to retry or report constraints. To compare agent designs efficiently, we introduce a 161-task multi-fidelity benchmark, evaluated in text on all tasks and in Habitat on 39. Text supports 10.9$\times$ higher evaluation throughput, while simulation reveals how perception and motion failures change task outcomes. BT-Agent's largest success gains over the strongest Act/ReAct baseline occur at 4B and 9B: approximately 10 and 5 percentage points. Ablations identify structured tool calls and informative failure feedback as the most useful components.

2026-10-09

Dream to Remember: Continual Steering of Generalist Robot Policies with World Models

Dream to Remember: Continual Steering of Generalist Robot Policies with World Models

Bahey Tharwat、Iman Nematollahi、Marvin Alles、Xingyuan Zhang、Akshay L Chandra 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Generalist robot policies are trained with imitation learning and are bounded by the quality and coverage of their demonstrations. Reinforcement learning can improve and extend them, either by fine-tuning the policy weights or by training a small module to steer the input noise of a frozen generative policy. When tasks arrive sequentially, both approaches suffer from catastrophic forgetting. Fine-tuning on a task degrades prior tasks, and steering only shifts forgetting to the shared steering module. We propose Dream to Remember (D2R), which continually steers a frozen generalist policy toward new tasks, while retaining its earlier skills. As the steering module learns a new task, a frozen pretrained world model rehearses prior tasks through imagined rollouts, anchoring the module to its earlier behavior without a replay buffer or new data. When the world model is faithful enough, D2R learns the new task entirely in imagination. Otherwise, it learns the task in the environment while using the world model only to rehearse prior tasks. On CALVIN, D2R improves eight tasks in sequence entirely in imagination, raising average success from 63.7% to 74.8%, while LoRA fine-tuning on the same sequence drops it to 11.7%. With a frozen off-the-shelf generalist policy and world model, $π_0$ and A2World, D2R learns unseen LIBERO-90 tasks in the environment while keeping $π_0$'s performance on the LIBERO suites it was fine-tuned on, even though the world model is not accurate enough for policy optimization. Finally, on a real Franka robot, D2R improves three tasks in sequence entirely in imagination while retaining the earlier improvements.

SmolWAM: A Latent World-Action Model for Robotic Manipulation on Edge Devices

SmolWAM: A Latent World-Action Model for Robotic Manipulation on Edge Devices

Timi Owolabi、Hikmah Olawore、Frances Adelakun、Ayotomiwa Oyewumi

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

World-action models (WAMs) predict a robot's actions together with the observations those actions will produce. However, the strongest models rely on video diffusion transformers that are far too computationally expensive for resource-constrained edge devices. We present SmolWAM, a latent WAM that we derive from the 2B-parameter Cosmos Policy by keeping its pretrained trunk and rebuilding its inference interface: single-frame visual latents, cached text features that preserve the trunk's attention sinks, 12 of 28 transformer blocks, and 1,008 tokens from which actions and future visual latents are read out in one rectified-flow step. Trained in 3.6 GPU-hours, SmolWAM succeeds in 95.5% of 400 LIBERO episodes at 57 ms and 2.3 TFLOP per 16-action chunk with 2.4 GiB of GPU memory, 11x faster than its teacher with 18.5x fewer FLOPs and 3.2x less memory, and its world head still forecasts scene motion.

JoyInside-VLM-Agent: Product-Conditioned Multimodal Policy Learning for Embodied Agents

JoyInside-VLM-Agent: Product-Conditioned Multimodal Policy Learning for Embodied Agents

Gangyi Li、LuYao Wang、Jingfu Sun、Jingyi Yuan、Hang Liu 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Embodied agents must translate multimodal observations and user requests into executable actions while adapting to product constraints, changing goals, and execution feedback. We present JoyInside-VLM-Agent, a ReAct-style multimodal agent developed through product-conditioned trajectory construction and staged policy learning for robotic platforms and smart hardware. A closed-loop sandbox generates multimodal interaction trajectories covering goal revisions and failure recovery, which are checked against execution outcomes and semantic criteria. Supervised fine-tuning and multi-teacher on-policy distillation precede reinforcement learning with DPER (Dual-Source Privileged On-Policy Self-Distillation with Token-Level Evidence Routing). DPER routes token-level evidence between a frozen privileged teacher and a privileged student sharing the rollout policy's parameters, modulating reward-derived token advantages while preserving their signs. Training rewards combine generative process evaluation, multi-judge rankings, and task-queue consistency. JoyInside-VLM-Agent achieves a BFCL v4 overall score of 77.44, exceeding the evaluated baselines; results on the other public benchmarks are mixed. On JoyInside-Devices and JoyInside-Robotics, the evaluated system, which includes domain-specific skill refinement, leads the baselines on all reported metrics. Two real-world quadruped case studies illustrate visually grounded search, replanning after execution failure, and explicit failure reporting.

Ultrasound and Magnetic Resonance Elastography of the Neck: Biomechanical Basis, Clinical Applications and Methodological Challenges

Ultrasound and Magnetic Resonance Elastography of the Neck: Biomechanical Basis, Clinical Applications and Methodological Challenges

Rashedul Hasan、Anna Yang、Kishani Kandasamy、Hossein Bahreinizad、Md Ashikuzzaman 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Many neck disorders, from chronic neck pain and headache to thyroid and nodal malignancy and radiation-induced fibrosis, both arise from and alter the mechanical properties of neck tissues, yet clinical assessment still relies on morphology, palpation and symptoms. Ultrasound and magnetic resonance elastography (MRE) now allow these properties to be measured in vivo, but the neck literature differs widely in methodology and remains dispersed across modalities and tissues. This review brings together the biomechanical basis of neck tissue behavior, the clinical conditions that alter it, and the principles and applications of ultrasound elastography and MRE in the neck. We show that ultrasound elastography has demonstrated diagnostic value for thyroid nodules and cervical lymph nodes, while neck MRE has been shown to be feasible but remains at an early stage. Across both modalities, reported values depend strongly on fiber orientation, posture, muscle activation and acquisition conditions. We therefore propose reporting recommendations and discuss how measurements beyond stiffness, including anisotropy, viscosity and nonlinearity, could improve disease management and inform computational models of the neck.

CeDR-Nav: Cross-Embodiment Mapless Navigation via Offline Demonstration Recasting

CeDR-Nav: Cross-Embodiment Mapless Navigation via Offline Demonstration Recasting

Xianghui Wang、Xinming Zhang、Yiwei Qian、Siwei Cheng、Shanze Wang 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Learning-based methods have demonstrated strong performance in mapless robot navigation. However, a learned policy remains restricted to the embodiment on which it was trained. Many cross-embodiment navigation methods require time-consuming online training and can still underperform target-specific policies. In fact, demonstrations collected on a source embodiment already contain navigation experience that is potentially reusable for deriving a policy for each target embodiment, but the embodiment mismatch prevents these demonstrations from directly serving as valid supervision for the target. In this article, we present CeDR-Nav, a cross-embodiment demonstration recasting framework that trains a dedicated navigation policy for each target embodiment from existing source demonstrations without online data collection. Geometry-consistent scaling with per-demonstration validity assessment converts the source demonstrations into target training data, and a target-specific Gaussian mixture policy generates feasible avoidance maneuvers for each target embodiment. Extensive quantitative simulations and real-world deployments across multiple robot platforms demonstrate the effectiveness of CeDR-Nav, with an average success-rate gain of 13.8 percentage points over DRL-DCLP in cross-embodiment simulations and target-specific policy training completed within seconds.
