多模态/VLM生成模型/扩散3D视觉机器人视觉
2026-10-09

HorizonNav: Geometry-Guided Video Diffusion for Anticipatory 3D Robot Navigation

HorizonNav: Geometry-Guided Video Diffusion for Anticipatory 3D Robot Navigation

Hajira Amjad、Valerii Serpiva、Viacheslav Liubimov、Didar Seyidov、Dzmitry Tsetserukou 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We present HorizonNav, a video diffusion-based framework for anticipatory robot navigation that transforms a single RGB image and a text prompt into a geometrically aligned representation of plausible surrounding 3D space. Given the initial observation and a textual description of the reconstruction objective, a vision-language model, guided by designed instructions, interprets the scene and constructs structured prompts for a video diffusion model. The resulting video sequence provides plausible future observations of the environment, which are subsequently processed using monocular ORB-SLAM3 for camera pose estimation and Video Depth Anything for dense depth prediction. Our central contribution is a geometry-guided depth alignment stage that combines the estimated camera poses with triangulated visual landmarks to constrain dense depth predictions across multiple views. This alignment improves the geometric consistency of the reconstructed 3D environment, addressing inconsistencies that temporal coherence of the generated video alone cannot resolve. The experimental evaluation of navigation in imagined 3D environment shows that across a 19-scene horizon study, planning-feasible scenes increase from 13/19 at P20 to 15/19 at P81, while successful RRT trials increase from 62/90 to 75/90. In a separate corridor study, 6 of 25 evaluable generated-camera trajectories violate the reconstructed collision constraints; RRT planner recovers feasible alternatives in 5 of those 6 cases. A larger archive contains 58 usable reconstructions from 67 attempted cases, of which 36 support a downstream plan and 22 do not, exposing reconstruction and free-space failure modes rather than hiding them without lidar. Across 15 corridor reconstructions, the mean reconstruction error is approximately 0.30 m at matched structural landmarks.

目标检测/分割3D视觉机器人视觉
2026-10-09

Mapless, Stabilizing, and Anti-Stuck Flipper Control for Articulated Tracked Robots

Mapless, Stabilizing, and Anti-Stuck Flipper Control for Articulated Tracked Robots

Valentýn Číhala、Pavel Sarga、Martin Pecka、Tomáš Svoboda、Karel Zimmermann 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Nearly every autonomous or semi-autonomous flipper controller assumes access to an elevation map of the terrain under and in front of the robot. The terrain beneath the chassis is occluded in the sensors, so the map is remembered through odometry integration, whose drift corrupts it. We present Stabilizing Anti-Stuck (SAS), a mapless controller that combines a stabilization term for body roll and pitch with an anti-stuck term that frees the robot when it gets stuck. As input, SAS uses the IMU, the flipper joint angles, and a motion cue that only tells whether the robot is moving. The cue is computed over a short window and never accumulated, so SAS does not inherit the odometry-integration drift that corrupts the map. We deploy SAS on a tracked robot with four flippers and evaluate it against manual teleoperation and prior flipper controllers in a large comparison, both in simulation and on a real obstacle. In simulation and on the real robot, SAS achieves traversal quality comparable to state-of-the-art map-based controllers. On a log hidden in tall grass, invisible to the LiDAR, the best map-based controller gets stuck while SAS traverses the hidden obstacle without a problem.

2026-10-09

When LiDAR Fades, Radar Takes Over: LiDAR Reliability-Aware Adaptive Localization Under Airborne Obscurants

When LiDAR Fades, Radar Takes Over: LiDAR Reliability-Aware Adaptive Localization Under Airborne Obscurants

Moumita Mukherjee、Avijit Banerjee、Anton Koval、George Nikolakopoulo

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

LiDAR-inertial odometry (LIO) is widely used for real-time localization and mapping, but its reliability can degrade substantially in subterranean environments affected by smoke, dust, and other airborne obscurants, whereas radar remains comparatively robust. This article presents a one-class temporal learning framework for LiDAR degradation detection and reliability-aware localization under such conditions. A broad set of degradation-sensitive cues, including spatial, cross-modal, geometric/motion, intensity, and measurement-recency features, is evaluated to characterize changes in LiDAR sensing quality. Their temporal evolution is modelled over sliding windows using an LSTM autoencoder trained exclusively on nominal operation, enabling deviations from healthy sensor behaviour to be detected without requiring labelled degradation data. The resulting LiDAR reliability estimate governs adaptive selection between LIO and radar inertial odometry (RIO), retaining LIO during reliable sensing and transferring localization authority to RIO when degradation is detected. The framework is evaluated using real-world ground robot data collected in GNSS-denied subterranean tunnels under nominal and dense-smoke conditions. Experimental results demonstrate degradation detection, adaptive modality switching, and continued localization output under severe LiDAR degradation. A feature family ablation study further shows that a reduced feature representation can improve degradation-aware localization performance in the evaluated dense-smoke scenarios, highlighting the importance of feature parsimony in one-class sensor degradation detection. The code will be released as open source to support reproducibility and further research.

2026-10-02

Imagine the Future, Internalize the Gist: Efficient VLA Reasoning via Internalized Spatiotemporal Imagination

Imagine the Future, Internalize the Gist: Efficient VLA Reasoning via Internalized Spatiotemporal Imagination

Shenglan Li、Zhendong Mi、Hengyi Zhu、Jingwu Luo、Chun Kit Chan 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Vision-language-action (VLA) models increasingly incorporate intermediate reasoning to improve robotic manipulation, yet existing approaches primarily reason about observed states without explicitly anticipating future scene evolution. Extending such reasoning to explicit future rollouts at every inference step, however, introduces substantial computational overhead. We propose IG-VLA, a VLA reasoning framework that enables models to imagine the future and internalize the gist. Our Latent Spatiotemporal Reasoning learns to imagine task-relevant future scene evolution directly in visual representation space, guiding action prediction without costly pixel-level video generation. To further reduce inference overhead, we introduce Scene Gist Memory, which internalizes reasoning-derived scene-behavior associations into a compact Scene Gist Token, preserving the benefits of future reasoning while bypassing explicit future imagination at inference. Extensive experiments on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and VLABench demonstrate the effectiveness and efficiency of IG-VLA. On the LIBERO-Plus Language suite, both the reasoning and gist policies outperform the strongest baseline by nearly 6% in success rate. The gist policy also achieves up to 6.38x speedup over baselines, reducing inference latency from 1081ms to 169.5ms per action chunk on a single NVIDIA A6000 GPU. These results demonstrate that future spatiotemporal reasoning can be effectively internalized for efficient VLA deployment.

2026-10-08

Trajectory Prediction for Autonomous Driving in Road Construction Zones Using Human-in-the-Loop Driving Data

Trajectory Prediction for Autonomous Driving in Road Construction Zones Using Human-in-the-Loop Driving Data

Shuo Zhang、Aman Sunesh、Semiha Ergan、Kaan Ozbay

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Ego-trajectory prediction in urban roadway work zones remains an underexplored topic in autonomous-driving research, with limited dedicated data and systematic evaluation. Roadway work zones involve temporary changes in the lane layouts and connectivity of the roads, complicating interactions with surrounding vehicles and workers. These changes make future vehicle trajectories difficult to predict. To address these challenges, we collect WZDrive-Data, a multimodal dataset comprising 1,183 work-zone driving segments from 33 drivers, using a human-in-the-loop driving simulator with prerecorded worker animations and VR-controlled workers. Driver control and gaze signals are recorded from human participants, while vehicle trajectories and scene information are obtained from the simulator. Our proposed multimodal ego-trajectory predictor, WZDrive, achieves a mean minADE (K=6) of 1.09 m and minFDE (K=6) of 2.12 m. Compared with the best-performing state-of-the-art baseline for each metric in our benchmark, WZDrive reduces minADE (K=6) by 7.18% and minFDE (K=6) by 13.18%. This research addresses a gap in autonomous-driving research on urban work zones by combining human-in-the-loop data collection with multimodal learning from human driving signals and in the context of work zones.

What Should Embodied Agents Remember? Benchmarking and Evaluating Memory Modality for Embodied Agents

What Should Embodied Agents Remember? Benchmarking and Evaluating Memory Modality for Embodied Agents

Junxiang Wang、Yuchen Liu、Yi Wu、Guofei Chen、Nir Pechuk 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Memory allows large language model (LLM) agents to use past experience, often stored as textual records, when responding to requests. For embodied agents that control robots, visual and spatial observations introduce a broader choice of memory modalities, affecting both the information available for subsequent tasks and the model inference costs of constructing and using memory. We introduce MeMoREval, a benchmark for studying this problem through a controlled experience-request protocol. Agents first experience a sequence of events without knowing the subsequent tasks, and subsequently they use memory to respond to information and action requests without receiving new observations. The benchmark comprises 53 tasks across four simulated indoor environments and measures task success alongside the inference costs of memory construction and use. Our reference evaluations reveal that storing frames outperforms storing generated descriptions at lower write-time inference cost for the primary model, while storing frames with scene graphs achieves the highest aggregate task success for every tested model. Extensions to accumulated experience, selective frame retention, and visual input to memory writers demonstrate how the benchmark accommodates further memory-design investigations. Two physical-robot demonstrations establish the feasibility of applying its protocol beyond simulation.

2026-10-08

PanTact: Teaching Whole-Body Dexterity with a Wearable Contact Interface

PanTact: Teaching Whole-Body Dexterity with a Wearable Contact Interface

Xiaomeng Xu、Han Zhang、Zhian Ruan、Xiaofeng Guo、Jinkun Cao 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Humanoid robots have many degrees of freedom and contact surfaces, yet most loco-manipulation research restricts manipulation to the hands and avoids other body contacts with the environment. To extend manipulation beyond the hands, we present PanTact, a system that learns humanoid whole-body dexterity, the capability to manipulate with any suitable body surface, directly from robot-free human demonstrations. Our key idea is to use contact as manipulation-centric information that transfers across embodiments. Contact locations and timing specify the interactions needed to accomplish a task, while the robot chooses how to coordinate its body to realize them within its own physical constraints. Specifically, PanTact captures, learns, and executes whole-body contact via: 1) a wearable, robot-free data collection interface that augments egocentric vision and bimanual UMI grippers with whole-body modular contact patches to record body contact locations and states; 2) a visuomotor policy that mitigates the egocentric observation gap and predicts whole-body contact goals; and 3) contact-aware whole-body control formulations that realize active contact goals using suitable robot surfaces. We deploy PanTact on a Unitree G1 humanoid without additional body contact sensors, demonstrating whole-body dexterity involving body support, pushing, sweeping, and load-sharing. Website: https://pantact.github.io

2026-10-09

Reinforcement Learning via Fixed-point Flow Matching

Reinforcement Learning via Fixed-point Flow Matching

Denis Blessing、Onur Celik、Gerhard Neumann

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

In maximum entropy and behavior-constrained reinforcement learning (RL), the optimal policy is given by a Boltzmann distribution. Generating samples from this distribution can be challenging, as it is often complex and multimodal. While flow policies trained with flow matching provide a powerful framework for representing such distributions, they traditionally rely on access to training data, which is generally unavailable in online RL. To address this limitation, we propose fixed-point flow matching (FFM), a data-free procedure for training and fine-tuning flow policies in RL. FFM leverages the target flow identity to formulate the actor update as a fixed-point iteration, utilizing a flow-matching-style regression objective where the critic's action gradients serve as regression targets. We show that the optimal policy is a fixed point of this iteration. Through extensive experiments, we demonstrate that FFM effectively fine-tunes pretrained flow policies in the offline-to-online setting and enables efficient and accurate training of flow policies from scratch in online RL. These results establish fixed-point flow matching as a principled approach for learning expressive flow-based policies without requiring training data.

Multimodal Image-based Robotic Assistance and Guidance Environment (MIRAGE): A Novel Unified Framework for Surgical Navigation and Visualization

Multimodal Image-based Robotic Assistance and Guidance Environment (MIRAGE): A Novel Unified Framework for Surgical Navigation and Visualization

Ehsan Nasiri、Yi Zheng、Michael A. Kokko、Xiaoyao Fan、William R. Warner 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

This paper presents the computational architecture and quantitative preclinical evaluation of MIRAGE, a multimodal image-guided robotic surgery framework for real-time visualization and navigation. It integrates stereovision scene reconstruction, augmented-reality (AR) video overlay, quantified heads-up display (HUD) navigation, and intraoperative ultrasound within the da Vinci console's TilePro display (Intuitive Surgical). Additional modules include optical/electromagnetic tracker registration, stereo calibration, fiducial rigid registration with RMSE feedback, and 3D point-cloud reconstruction. Video I/O and rendering run in a C++ backend, while a Python GUI coordinates calibration, registration, reconstruction, visualization, and user interaction through an in-process zero-copy binding layer. The framework formalizes spatial transforms, module states, and synchronization of heterogeneous video and tracking streams. Preoperative calibration uses synchronized checkerboard image pairs and optimization, with estimated intrinsics, extrinsics, and rectification reused intraoperatively. MIRAGE was validated in phantom experiments across da Vinci S, Si, Xi, and SP platforms. Across clinically relevant working distances, mean 3D reprojection error was 0.6mm, with best 0.1mm RMSE at 100mm on Xi. Results support MIRAGE as a preclinical technical-validation platform for accurate, responsive, and extensible multimodal surgical guidance.

2026-10-09

Prediction-Informed Landing Window Planning for UAV Landing on USV in High Sea States

Prediction-Informed Landing Window Planning for UAV Landing on USV in High Sea States

Kaizhen Li、Xi Zhou、Dan Zhang、Xiaowei Li

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Autonomous landing of unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) on unmanned surface vehicle (USV) is difficult in high sea states. Existing hierarchical landing methods often infer future USV motion only implicitly from short observation histories, which limits their ability to adapt to severe and rapidly changing sea conditions. This paper presents a prediction-informed hierarchical control framework. A pretrained model is developed to explicitly predict the USV attitude during high-level landing decisions. The prediction helps identify suitable landing windows and choose the right touchdown time, while a conventional low-level flight controller maintains UAV stability and tracks motion commands. To evaluate the framework under realistic disturbances, a real-sim validation setup is constructed in which USV-motion data collected in real sea conditions are transferred to a MuJoCo USV. The experiments demonstrate the feasibility of using explicit attitude prediction to improve landing-window selection and support timing-aware UAV landing planning in rapidly varying marine environments.

2026-10-09

The value of information and the emergence of communication in evolved e-puck robots

The value of information and the emergence of communication in evolved e-puck robots

Fernando Montes-Gonzalez

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

We study when communication emerges between autonomous agents and what role an innate action selection architecture plays. Two e-puck robots, reconstructed in PyBullet from their Webots model and validated against it, must find a feeding zone that can only be detected when a robot passes over it. Each robot can emit a one-bit signal whose direction is detected by the receiver. Control is provided by a GRU recurrent network with a residual path, either operating alone or as a channel within an Embedded Action Selection Architecture (EASA). Fitness measures only the time the robots spend over the food. When the robots receive a compass pointing to the food, use of the signal disappears completely. Without a compass, in a small arena, three independent runs achieved similar performance using different strategies: a referential signal, a permanent beacon, or no use of the signal. As the arena expands, the strategy based on a referential signal generalizes best. In contrast, evolving directly in the large arena does not increase the frequency of communication, but it does separate the strategies, and the referential signal clearly outperforms solitary searching. Within EASA, innate reflexes constrain the network and reduce performance. As its priorities evolve, evolution consistently weakens those reflexes. The results suggest that communication emerges in proportion to the value of the information it transmits, and that an innate scaffolding designed for isolated agents can be an impediment in a social task.

Failure-Resilient Multi-Robot Coordination with $k$-Connectivity Constrained Submodular Maximization

Failure-Resilient Multi-Robot Coordination with $k$-Connectivity Constrained Submodular Maximization

Yoonjae Hwang、Hojune Kim、Ellen Novoseller、Guangyao Shi、Gaurav S. Sukhatme 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Multi-robot coordination is essential in applications such as coverage, target tracking, and information gathering, where robots must jointly select actions to maximize team performance. Submodular maximization provides an effective framework for such coordination, and resilient variants further account for robot failures or adversarial removals. However, existing methods typically overlook the impact of robot loss on the communication network, which may disconnect the surviving team and prevent continued coordination. To address this issue, we introduce Resilient $k$-Connected Submodular Maximization, which jointly considers task performance, resilience, and communication connectivity. The resulting feasible family is not a matroid, preventing the direct application of classical curvature-greedy guarantees. We overcome this challenge through a Menger-based connectivity reduction, a connectivity-aware greedy algorithm with a feasibility-preserving fallback, and a new curvature-based analysis that captures the cost of enforcing connectivity through a single factor $ρ$. Our method achieves a worst-case post-failure objective within a factor of $[\max(1-ν_f(\mathcal{I}),h(N,β))]^2 ρ\frac{1}{κ_f}(1-e^{-κ_f})$ of the optimal connectivity-respecting resilient assignment, while recovering the classical unconstrained guarantee in the appropriate limits. Experiments on multi-robot target tracking validate the proposed method and characterize the practical impact of connectivity constraints.

A Miniaturized Wi-Fi HaLow-Based Wireless Imaging Transfer System for Capsule Endoscopy

A Miniaturized Wi-Fi HaLow-Based Wireless Imaging Transfer System for Capsule Endoscopy

Michele Bertozzi、Lorenzo Amelio、Sebastiano Strangio、Nikita J. Greenidge、Benjamin Calmé 等

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 暂无开源

Wireless capsule endoscopy is constrained by severe limitations in size, available power, and reliable data transmission through biological tissues. Although recent advances in miniature imaging sensors have improved image quality, the wireless transmission of color images at practical frame rates remains a challenging task under the tight capsule constraints. We present the design and the experimental validation of a miniaturized Wireless Imaging Transfer (WIT) system for capsule endoscopy based on a sub-GHz IEEE 802.11ah (Wi-Fi HaLow) wireless link. In addition, a preliminary in-vivo validation of Wi-Fi HaLow communication is reported, showing that a stable through-body wireless link can be established across biological tissues in a clinically relevant porcine model. These in-vivo results provide direct experimental support for the adoption of sub-GHz Wi-Fi HaLow for capsule endoscopy applications. Experimental results demonstrate stable wireless image transmission at a resolution of 320x320 pixels, with measured end-to-end data rates above 12 Mbps and a transmission energy efficiency of 20.7 nJ/bit. We also report a detailed power consumption analysis, with a breakdown of the contributions from the imaging sensor, illumination, digital processing, and RF transmission. When all active subsystem contributions are considered, including the camera, digital processing, RF transmission, and illumination at 60% LED brightness, the total power consumption is approximately 477 mW, corresponding to a complete-chain energy consumption of approximately 39.6 nJ/bit. Overall, the measured performance confirms that sub-GHz Wi-Fi HaLow provides a promising trade-off among throughput, robustness, and energy efficiency for wireless imaging in capsule endoscopy.

MARGoS: A Configuration-Driven Platform for Reproducible MARL Experiments in ARGoS

MARGoS: A Configuration-Driven Platform for Reproducible MARL Experiments in ARGoS

Tom Philipp Backert、Mohammad D. Soorati、Javad Ghofrani

论文页面 PDF HTML 版本 ⭐ GitHub 开源

Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) has become a prominent research direction, particularly within collective robotics, where the ARGoS simulator is widely used to study emergent swarm behavior. Yet despite this growth, systematic support for reuse and reproducibility in MARL-based swarm robotics remains limited and insufficiently standardized: a single experiment is assembled from manually aligned simulator files, training scripts, configuration values, paths, seeds, and environment details, so reuse and reproduction stay fragile even within one research group. To address this gap, we introduce MARGoS, a lightweight, configuration-driven platform developed in this project that elevates these experiment-defining inputs to first-class, platform-managed artifacts. MARGoS enables reproducible experimentation through configuration hashing, end-to-end seed propagation, frozen (immutable) configurations, structured logging, environment fingerprinting, and exportable experiment bundles that facilitate sharing, replay, and verification of results across machines and over time. A five-dimension evaluation (architecture, efficiency, reproducibility, usability, and collaboration) supports our claim that managing experiment variation as a platform responsibility makes ARGoS+MARL experiments easier to reuse, reproduce, and hand off. Our code is available at https://github.com/tombackert/margos.
